38: ## 2. Four exact targets and the screening decisions
39: ### B — power stability of an unequal-temperature constrained minimum (selected)
40: Let H_n=ℓ²(N₀ⁿ), N=Σa_j†a_j and
41: τ_r=(r+1)^(−n)(r/(r+1))^N,   g(r)=(r+1)log(r+1)−r log r.
42: Let T_{η,b} be the n-mode tensor power of a thermal attenuator with **independent** environment τ_b, identical transmissivity η in every mode, and arbitrary n-mode input. All entropies are in nats, and ||·||₁ is unhalved.
43: **Target B:** for every integer n≥2 there is α_n>0 such that for every r,b>0 with r≠b, every 0<η<1 and finite E>nr, there is C=C(n,E,r,b,η)<∞ for which every centered state ρ with
44: Tr ρN≤E,   Tr ρa_j=0 (all j),   S(ρ)=ng(r)
45: satisfies
46: ||ρ−τ_r||₁ ≤ C [S(T_{η,b}(ρ))−ng(ηr+(1−η)b)]^α_n.                 (B)
47: The bracket Δ is nonnegative by EP. The exponent is permitted to depend on n, while the constant sees all the other displayed parameters. No endpoint, uniform-in-n, or uniform-as-E→∞ estimate is claimed. Target B would in particular identify every centered exact optimizer. It is **a target, not a theorem**.
48: For an uncentered input use D(−μ)ρD(μ), μ_j=Trρa_j. This leaves both entropies and Δ unchanged, reduces energy by |μ|², and asks for closeness to the thermal state displaced by the input's own mean. There is no independence assumption across its n modes. Fixing the isotropic thermal environment matters: arbitrary common symplectic transformations are symmetries of the *two-free-port* inequality but change this fixed environment unless passive. The extremal family in B is therefore displaced isotropic product thermals, not all Gaussian states. At r=b the linear equality literature becomes relevant, but B deliberately retains the unequal-temperature obligation. At E=nr the Gibbs entropy-energy equality already forces τ_r; E>nr is the nontrivial regime.
49: ### C — all-code GAD exponential strong converse (rejected: exact current duplicate)
50: This paragraph measures capacity, Rényi information and rates in bits, with a base-2 exponent; conversion to e^(−ck) multiplies the exponent by log 2. The screened claim was: for every 0<γ,ν<1 and R>C(A_{γ,ν}), there are c>0 and k₀ such that every k-use unassisted classical code of at least 2^(kR) equiprobable messages, allowing entangled inputs and collective decoding, has success ≤e^(−ck) for k≥k₀.
51: Fang v2 Theorem 1 proves more: all orders α>1 have additive sandwiched Rényi Holevo information and the exact limiting exponent is sup_{α>1}(α−1)/α [R−χ̃_α(A)]. The proof uses triangular Schatten compression, multiplicativity after arbitrary positive diagonal sandwiches, phase-covariant diagonal reference states, then the information radius. This is an exact full-scope reduction, not an inference from ordinary Holevo additivity. I07 had already rejected it. We retain no C nomination. This is a current **author theorem** comparison, not an independent acceptance of its proof.
52: ### G — a universal cubic threshold exponent (unresolved screen, not selected)
53: The precise target is existence of one numerical c>0 such that for every finite two-player one-round game G with Boolean acceptance predicate, v=ω*(G)<1, d=|A||B|, every 0<δ<1−v and k≥1, and every finite-dimensional tensor-product joint strategy for k independent repetitions,
54: Pr[W_k≥ceil((v+δ)k)] ≤ exp[−cδ³ k/(1+log d)].                  (G)
55: The same bound would pass to the unrestricted finite-dimensional supremum. It would not imply a commuting-operator version. PR already proves universal fifth-power and fixed-law cubic bounds. Its full conditioning-to-threshold transfer takes an error Bτ^β to rate of order δ^(1+1/β). Thus obtaining G by this transfer requires **uniform square-root** conditioning control; simply rerunning Song's τ^(1/4) estimate gives δ⁵. The root-state construction supplies square-root control only with κ_μ, which diverges as support masses vanish or support paths lengthen. Replacing a root by a weighted graph mean might improve κ_μ to an inverse gap, but that still permits unbounded constants. It does not pay a universal cubic rate.
56: Song's all-win cubic estimate is a different event and uses an extra near-perfect-success step; the pinned source explicitly separates that operation from its generic conditioning lemma. We do not silently substitute all-win amplification for threshold amplification. No new sampling lemma or distribution-independent quantum rounding was established here. Current Song search results were used for orientation; the SSRN direct request failed, and no claim of a complete new Song proof audit is made. All comparisons required for the above quantified obstruction are explicit in the accepted PR operations.
57: ### S — polylogarithmic precision in uniform Boolean-oracle synthesis (rejected: paid by lower operations)
58: The target was a deterministic generator, given 1^n and an integer precision k≥1, of one circuit over H,T,T†,CNOT and a single Boolean oracle, with qubits, gates, calls, address length and generation time poly(n,k), such that **for every** U∈U(2^n) **some target-dependent oracle** yields full diamond error ≤2^(−k), coherently with clean-workspace comparison before discarding ancillas. There is no bound on computing or explicitly listing the oracle.
59: Here is a concrete reduction, rather than just the phrase “error reduction.” The source has contraction r₀=3/4, M=4096(n+1)² and fewer than J=8M(n+1) levels. Choose integer L with
60: 18J(3/4)^L ≤ ε/4,   ε=2^(−k).
61: Then L=O(k+log(n+1)). Its fields/encoders permit every integer L≥1, with h=ceil(log₂(L+1)), b=nL+h, K=b(L+1). Summing the actual one-recursive-call costs gives
62: Q=O(J(K+n+h)),  S=O(J[L(K+n+h)+K+n])
63: qubits and slots, with clean operator error ≤ε/4. Each field row still has at most L input columns: the integer power-sum multiset encoding has modulus exceeding L2^(nL). No register-size cost is omitted.
64: For compilation the source's proof, before its deliberately loose ξ^(−3) final bound, establishes net refinement e_{j+1}≤C₀e_j^(3/2), ℓ_{j+1}≤5ℓ_j. Start with its fixed net sufficiently fine that b₀=C₀²e₀<1. Induction gives C₀²e_j≤b₀^((3/2)^j). Therefore a word of accuracy ξ has length O((1+log(1/ξ))^κ), κ=log(5)/log(3/2). This uses precisely the already-proved commutator recursion. Choose per-slot error ξ=ε/[4(S+1)]. The source's phase-sensitive two-word lookup and ideal-prefix telescoping give total compilation error ≤ε/4. Its address has O(Q+log(S+1)+log(ℓ+1)) bits; each lookup uses polynomially many exact reversible gates. Word choices reside in the oracle, so the uniform generator need not find them. Total clean operator error is ≤ε/2 and the pure-vector trace bound gives full diamond error ≤ε, even with an external reference. The construction is block diagonal in any supplied label, so coherent relative phases are retained.
65: This proves S at author level **as a technical consequence of accepted source operations**, without a new mechanism. The oracle's truth table can still have 2^poly(n,k) bits. A current random-oracle lower bound, [Huang–Ramkumar–Wright 2610.06700v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06700v1), addresses a different oracle quantifier and is not used as a contradiction. No efficient oracle-generation result is claimed.
66: ## 3. Route I: a quantitative thermal-balance construction
67: EP §5 identifies thermal limits from exact number-basis recurrences. We paid a stable version on the full infinite Fock space. It controls a specified defect, rather than asserting that entropy deficit already controls that defect.
68: ### B114: stable thermal balance under a uniform energy cap
69: For fixed n≥1, r>0 and any state ρ with TrρN≤E<∞ define
70: R_j=(r+1)a_jρ−rρa_j,
71: ε_R²=Σ_j||R_j||₂²,
72: t=[2/(2r+1)]^(1/2) ε_R,
73: C_r,n=((2r+1)/(r+1))^n.
74: These are ordinary Hilbert–Schmidt norms; R_j are in fact trace class by finite-energy factorization. Put c=Tr(ρτ_r)/Tr(τ_r²). Then
75: 0≤c≤C_r,n,   ||ρ−cτ_r||₂≤t.                                  (1)
76: For every integer K≥0,
77: ||ρ−τ_r||₁ ≤ 2 sqrt(binomial(K+n,n)) t
78:                  +4[√E+C_r,n√(nr)]/√(K+1).                    (2)
79: In particular, for 0<t≤1,
80: ||ρ−τ_r||₁ ≤ [2^(1+n/2)+4√E+4C_r,n√(nr)] t^(1/(n+1)).         (3)
81: For t=0 the left side vanishes; in all cases it is also ≤2. Constants are explicit, fixed-mode, and not uniform in energy or mode count. No faithfulness, mode-product form, or higher moment is needed for (1)–(3).
82: **Proof of (1).** Vectorize Hilbert–Schmidt matrices in the number basis, |p><q|↦|p>⊗|q>. On the resulting 2n-mode space define
83: D_j=(r+1)a_j⊗I−rI⊗a_j†,
84: E_j=(r+1)I⊗a_j−ra_j†⊗I.
85: Their commutators are [D_j,D_k†]=[E_j,E_k†]=(2r+1)δ_jk, with all cross annihilator and cross annihilator/creator commutators zero. They are a finite-mode two-mode squeezing transform of the usual annihilators: write tanh θ=r/(r+1), so cosh θ=(r+1)/√(2r+1), sinh θ=r/√(2r+1). For U(θ)=exp[θ(a_j b_j−a_j†b_j†)] on each mode pair, conjugation U(θ)†a_jU(θ) implements the first formula, and similarly for b_j. Differentiating gives the two coupled linear equations u'=−v†, (v†)'=−u and their cosh/sinh solution. Equivalently this is the standard finite-dimensional symplectic transformation in the Schrödinger representation. Its transformed oscillator number operator therefore has spectrum {0,1,2,...} and a one-dimensional vacuum. Hence, as closed quadratic forms,
86: Σ_j(D_j†D_j+E_j†E_j) ≥ (2r+1)(I−|Ω_r><Ω_r|),                 (4)
87: where Ω_r=τ_r/||τ_r||₂ under vectorization. One can check the vacuum explicitly: its coefficients are (1−q²)^(n/2)q^|p| on |p,p>, q=r/(r+1); both annihilator lists kill it. The squeezing unitary proves completeness of the oscillator basis, not merely uniqueness of this formal recurrence.
88: Finite energy places |ρ> in the form domain: ||a_jρ||₂²=Tr(ρ²a_j†a_j)≤Tr(ρa_j†a_j), and ||ρa_j||₂²≤Trρ(a_ja_j†). These statements follow first on finite number cutoffs, then by Hilbert–Schmidt convergence; ρ²≤ρ bounds the traces. Thus (4) applies without an illicit bounded inverse. D_j|ρ>=|R_j> and E_j|ρ>=|R_j†>, because ρ is self-adjoint. Orthogonal projection on Ω_r is |cτ_r>. Consequently (4) gives (2r+1)||ρ−cτ_r||₂²≤2ε_R². Finally Trτ_r²=(2r+1)^(−n), ||τ_r||∞=(r+1)^(−n); positivity and trace one imply the stated bound on c. This proves (1).
89: **Proof of (2)–(3).** Let P=1_{N≤K}, of rank d_K=binomial(K+n,n). For any state σ, q=Trσ(I−P), the gentle compression estimate is ||σ−PσP||₁≤2√q. For a pure state this follows by diagonalizing its two-dimensional P/(I−P) span (the norm is √(4q−3q²)); a pure decomposition, convexity and Cauchy–Schwarz prove it for mixed states. Markov gives q≤TrσN/(K+1). Thus
90: ||ρ−cτ_r||₁≤√d_K t+2√(E/(K+1))+2c√(nr/(K+1)).
91: Its trace is 1−c, so |1−c| is at most the same quantity. Adding |1−c| proves (2). For t≤1 choose K+1=ceil(t^(−2/(n+1))). This is at most 2t^(−2/(n+1)), and d_K≤(K+1)^n by embedding the total-number simplex in the n-dimensional integer cube. Substitution proves (3). If t=0 then ρ=cτ_r and trace one gives c=1. ∎
92: ### Explicit connection to the source's metric defect
93: For a **faithful centered** state with finite energy, use EP's thermal metric and defect at parameter r, when the dual defect norm Q²=Σ_j||q_j||²_{F,*} is finite. No Hessian comparison is assumed in this observation. In an eigenbasis with eigenvalues p_l,
94: q_j(Z)=Tr(R_j†Z),
95: ||Z||_F²=Σ_lr h(r) ℓ(rp_l,(r+1)p_r)|Z_lr|²,
96: where h(r)=log((r+1)/r) and ℓ is the logarithmic mean. By ℓ(x,y)≤(x+y)/2 and p_l,p_r≤1,
97: ||Z||_F²≤h(r)(2r+1)||Z||₂²/2.
98: The centered projection is orthogonal in this metric and q_j(I)=0. To justify it using exactly the source test domain, fix a finite spectral-block Z and let P_M increase through spectral projections containing its block. The centered finite-block tests Z_M=P_MZP_M−[Tr(ρP_MZP_M)/Tr(ρP_M)]P_M converge in the F metric to Z−Tr(ρZ)I, since the diagonal metric weight is p_l and Trρ(I−P_M)→0. As R_j is trace class, q_j(Z_M)→q_j(Z). Duality and orthogonal projection therefore give |q_j(Z)|≤||q_j||_{F,*}||Z||_F≤[h(r)(2r+1)/2]^(1/2)||q_j||_{F,*}||Z||₂. Hilbert–Schmidt density and duality now prove
99: ε_R²≤h(r)(2r+1)Q²/2,   ||ρ−cτ_r||₂≤√h(r) Q.                (5)
100: Thus (2)–(3) apply with t=√h(r)Q. This explicitly converts the source's *weighted* dual defect to the ordinary norm needed in B114; they are not silently identified.
101: At EP's particular regularized minimizers, its complete cancellation proves A_*²+M_*²≤2Cζ/ε, with fixed positive weights w_i and uniformly bounded energies. Apply (5) to each state after its own displacement. It gives a centered trace-distance rate O(ζ^(1/[2(n+1)])). The means have size O(√ζ). To undo centering, for a thermal state and displacement α,
102: ||D(α)τ_rD(α)†−τ_r||₁≤2√(2r+1)|α|.
103: Indeed purify τ_r, write D(α)=e^{iH_α}, use |e^{ix}−1|≤|x| on the self-adjoint generator, and evaluate Trτ_rH_α²=(2r+1)|α|²; the pure-vector trace estimate and partial trace give the bound. Hence the same O(ζ^(1/[2(n+1)])) rate holds without centering, for 0<ζ≤1. These constants depend on the source's fixed auxiliary parameters, its sublevel energy bound and w_i. This strengthens its qualitative recurrence-limit step quantitatively, but does not change the class of states to which its stationarity estimate applies. It is **technical**, with a full reduction to thermal oscillator diagonalization, duality and energy cutoffs.
104: ### The unpaid part of Route I
105: For B, one needs a bound on the actual near-minimizer, for example Q(ρ)≤CΔ^β after centering (with an appropriate definition/regularization if not faithful). This would combine with (5)–(3) to give α=β/(n+1). EP only supplies Q at its **auxiliary Gibbs minimizers constructed from a hypothetical strict negative witness**. Its precise difference identity, `eq:relative-entropy-difference`, and component Hessian contraction use the exact Gibbs equation and global minimality of that penalized functional. They do not apply to an arbitrary small nonnegative Δ. Letting ζ tend to zero does not create a coupling between the original ρ and those minimizers.
106: An attempted repair adds a tether to ρ or restricts the minimization to a small trace ball. A trace-ball boundary introduces a normal term in the Euler equation; a quadratic Hilbert–Schmidt tether adds 2λ(σ−ρ). The relaxation pairing then contains Tr[(σ−ρ)L_rσ], which the energy cap alone does not bound by λ times the thermal metric defect with the needed favorable sign. Moreover small objective value by itself gives no small gradient uniformly near eigenvalues zero: the entropy Hessian contains reciprocal logarithmic means. The source's output replacement bounds its own output logarithm but not the original state's small input eigenvalues. No quantitative tether/approximate-stationarity estimate is established. B114 pays the stable recurrence, not this missing entropy-to-defect transfer.
107: ## 4. Route II: doubling, information loss and thermal filtering
108: This route uses a different mechanism: recover near-independence from near-preservation of information under a deliberately noisy channel. The following quantitative statement is proved fully and is independent of B114.
109: ### Q114: energy-controlled strict information loss
110: Let X be m≥1 bosonic modes, K any separable reference with S(ω_K)<∞, and ω_XK any state with Trω_X N≤E<∞. Put Φ=T_{1/2,2}, acting on X only, and
111: ℓ=I(X;K)_ω−I(Y;K)_(Φ⊗id)(ω)≥0.
112: All mutual informations are finite, since S(X) is bounded by the energy constraint. For every integer L≥0,
113: ||ω_XK−ω_X⊗ω_K||₁
114:  ≤ 2·3^m·2^L√(2ℓ)+8√(E/(L+1)).                             (6)
115: The left side is also ≤2, so the infimum of these upper bounds may be capped at 2. In particular, for 0<ℓ<1,
116:  ≤2√2·3^m ℓ^(1/4)+16√(E log2/log(1/ℓ)).                      (7)
117: For ℓ=0 it is zero. The bound is independent of reference dimension, but not of m or E. It is a trace-norm statement; no small-relative-entropy converse is asserted.
118: **Proof, including the channel construction.** The heterodyne representation is
119: Φ(ρ)=∫ Q_ρ(z) π_z d^(2m)z,
120: π_z=D(z/√2)τ_{1/2}D(z/√2)†,
121: Q_ρ(z)=π^(−m)<z|ρ|z>.
122: Its characteristic function is χ_ρ(w/√2)e^(−5|w|²/4), which agrees with a balanced mixer with bath mean 2. This verifies the representation by uniqueness of Weyl characteristic functions. For α=z/√2,
123: <0|π_z^(−1)|0>=(3/2)^m<-α|3^N|−α>=(3/2)^m e^|z|².
124: The middle equality is the convergent coherent-state series Σ3^k|α|^(2k)/k!=e^(3|α|²), with its normalization factor. Cauchy–Schwarz in the quadratic form of π_z gives π_z≥c(z)|0><0| with c(z)=(2/3)^m e^(−|z|²). Therefore Φ is a sum of two CP branches, one replacing its input by vacuum with effect
125: G=∫c(z)|z><z|d^(2m)z/π^m=3^(−m)2^(−N).
126: The polar coherent-state integral has kth diagonal coefficient 2^(−k−1) in one mode, proving this identity. The other branch Λ₀ is measure-and-prepare with positive kernel π_z−c(z)|0><0| and effect I−G. As I−G≥(1−3^(−m))I, it factors as Ψ₀((I−G)^(1/2)ρ(I−G)^(1/2)) for a channel Ψ₀. Thus Φ is implemented by the binary instrument with filters √G,√(I−G), then erasing X on outcome 1 and applying Ψ₀ on outcome 0, finally forgetting the outcome.
127: Let p=TrGω_X>0 and ξ=p^(−1)(√G⊗I)ω(√G⊗I). Data processing before and after this instrument, with its classical flag retained, gives exactly ℓ≥p I(X;K)_ξ: the flag-reference information and branch-0 upper bound cancel. This is Li's Eq. (59), reproduced here at its full operator scope. Branch entropies are finite: the filters commute with N and are contractions; each conditional reference entropy is bounded by S(ω_K) divided by its positive branch probability; output branch energies are bounded by the total output energy divided by the branch probability. Thus the ordinary chain rules used in that subtraction are legitimate. The integral maps are CP on every finite ancillary space because their operator-valued integrands are positive, with total trace at most the input trace; trace-norm continuity extends them to the stated separable systems.
128: Quantum Pinsker in nats (obtain it by a Helstrom binary measurement and classical Pinsker) gives
129: || (√G⊗I)ω(√G⊗I)−(√Gω_X√G)⊗ξ_K ||₁≤√(2pℓ)≤√(2ℓ).
130: For P_L=1_{N≤L}, the compressed inverse has norm ||P_LG^(−1/2)||=3^(m/2)2^(L/2). Conjugating the preceding trace-class difference by this bounded operator therefore gives
131: ||P_LωP_L−P_Lω_XP_L⊗ξ_K||₁≤3^m2^L√(2ℓ).
132: Both states ω and ω_X⊗ξ_K have the same X energy. Applying the gentle estimate from §3 to each yields
133: ||ω−ω_X⊗ξ_K||₁≤3^m2^L√(2ℓ)+4√(E/(L+1)).
134: Partial trace bounds ||ω_K−ξ_K||₁ by that same expression. One final triangle inequality proves (6). For 0<ℓ<1 take L=floor(log(1/ℓ)/(4log2)); then 2^L√ℓ≤ℓ^(1/4) and L+1>log(1/ℓ)/(4log2), proving (7). If ℓ=0 first use (6) at fixed L, then let L→∞. ∎
135: The construction and information-loss budget are attributed to Li, not presented as a new equality mechanism. Finite-energy truncation of its explicit inverse filter supplies the paid logarithmic rate. This is a concrete full-scope technical reduction, and Q114 is **not nominated** as substantial.
136: ### What the doubling identity actually buys, and why unequal EPnI stops it
137: Let independent finite-energy A,B have output pair CE under U_η and linear deficit
138: L_η=S(C)−ηS(A)−(1−η)S(B).
139: Li's exact doubling identity, with an independent auxiliary pair (τ₂,τ₂), decomposes L_η into two nonnegative qEPI deficits and I(C_−;E_+|C_+). Read the commuting circuit in the other order, and put σ_CJ=(id⊗Φ)(ω_CE). The last conditional information equals
140: I(C;J)_σ−I(C_+;J)_(Φ⊗id)(σ) ≤L_η.
141: The equality follows by the chain rule and independence of the auxiliary C bath; no near-equality argument is needed for this identity. The two other terms are nonnegative by unconditional/conditionally independent qEPI, with the memory's conditional independence verified by the A-group/B-group product factorization. Therefore (6) applies to σ, with ℓ≤L_η and E_C≤E_A+E_B. It controls the **noisy** CE correlations. Removing Φ quantitatively would require another estimate: its characteristic multiplier e^(−5|w|²/4) is nonzero but has an unbounded inverse. Exact injectivity alone is not a rate. A finite-energy inversion/cutoff argument is plausible; it is not claimed proved here, and Cuesta's fourth-moment constants cannot simply be used after an uncontrolled approximation.
142: Even a complete solution of that inversion would not settle B. At the *exact* thermal minimizer A=τ_r, B=τ_b, put s=ηr+(1−η)b. Then Δ=0 but
143: L_η=n[g(s)−ηg(r)−(1−η)g(b)]>0                              (8)
144: for r≠b, since g''(x)=−1/[x(x+1)]<0. Also the cross-covariance of the two outputs is √(η(1−η))(b−r)I, up to the harmless sign convention for the discarded port, and is nonzero. Thus the desired unequal-temperature optimizers **do not have product outputs**. Equation (8) refutes the proposed inequality L_η≤CΔ^β for any finite C,β>0 even at optimizers; it does not refute B.
145: Using an unequal thermal auxiliary merely moves this problem: that auxiliary's output pair is correlated, so the clean local-noise factorization and zero baseline used by Li disappear. One would need a new identity comparing to the *correlated Gaussian reference output*, with an error bounded by Δ and a recoverable input-reference structure. Naively replacing qEPI by a conditional EPnI is not an available repair: I07 already records its precise quantum-memory counterexample, using De Palma–Trevisan's conditional optimality theorem. No conditional EPnI or commuting-limit statement is inferred here.
146: ## 5. Counterexample and limit tests with complete proofs
147: ### No energy-cap-free information-loss modulus
148: Q114 genuinely needs a common E. For one mode and a classical bit reference, let
149: ω^(k)_XK=½|0><0|⊗|0><0|+½|k><k|⊗|1><1|,   k≥64.
150: Then ||ω^(k)−ω_X^(k)⊗ω_K^(k)||₁=1 (four orthogonal diagonal blocks of magnitude 1/4), and I(X;K)=log2. Each state has finite energy k/2, but the energies are not uniformly capped.
151: Under Φ=T_{1/2,2}, vacuum has output thermal mean 1, while the kth Fock input has output number mean k/2+1 and variance 5k/4+2. To compute this, write n_out=(n_a+n_b+a†b+b†a)/2. In the product of a number state and a thermal state, cross terms with an odd ladder factor vanish, Var n_b=6 and <(a†b+b†a)²>=5k+2. Division by four gives 6/4+(5k+2)/4=5k/4+2.
152: Measure output photon number and classify as bit 1 if it is at least k/4. Markov bounds the vacuum error by 4/k. Chebyshev bounds the Fock error by (5k/4+2)/(k/4+1)²≤20/k+32/k². The average error e_k≤12/k+16/k²<1/2. Data processing and binary Fano give
153: 0≤ℓ_k=log2−I(Y;K)≤h₂(e_k)≤h₂(12/k+16/k²)→0.
154: Thus no function tending to zero with ℓ alone can bound the correlation distance over *all finite-energy states*. This counterexample concerns the stronger energy-free Q proposal, not B or fixed-E Q114.
155: ### Trace closeness does not imply thermal-relative-entropy closeness at bounded energy
156: Fix r,c₀>0 and let ρ_k=(1−c₀/k)τ_r+(c₀/k)|k,0,...,0><k,0,...,0| for integers k>c₀. Then ||ρ_k−τ_r||₁≤2c₀/k→0 and Trρ_kN=(1−c₀/k)nr+c₀ is uniformly bounded. The source's energy-entropy continuity gives S(ρ_k)→ng(r). Its exact thermal-relative-entropy identity then yields
157: D(ρ_k||τ_r)→h(r)c₀>0.
158: Means are zero. Their entropies are not required to equal ng(r), so this is a topology/transfer counterexample and **not** a counterexample to B. It explains why B114's trace result cannot be silently upgraded to relative entropy. The same rare tail has unbounded higher moments, explaining why finite energy cannot be called a fourth-moment cap.
159: ### Symmetry, endpoints and compactness
160: A displaced thermal is an exact optimizer, so a conclusion to the undisplaced thermal without centering is false. For two freely varying independent inputs, the same symplectic transformation on both ports commutes with the scalar beam splitter and preserves all three entropies; at equal entropies any equal-covariance Gaussian pair is already a linear-qEPI extremizer. These facts exclude an unjustified “only isotropic thermals” claim for the two-free-port problem. B fixes an isotropic bath and uses centering explicitly. At η=0 or 1 the entropy inequality has many trivial equality cases, so those endpoints cannot be included in B with its proposed extremal family.
161: A uniform energy cap makes the fixed-mode state set compact and its entropy continuous. Even if uniqueness were proved, compactness would supply only an unspecified inverse modulus. Neither a power rate nor a mode-uniform theorem follows. All displayed quantitative rates above arise from an actual oscillator spectral estimate or a specified cutoff/inverse-filter budget.
162: ## 6. Contribution and evidence disposition
163: **Author-proved technical claims:** B114 (1)–(5), its scoped regularized-minimizer rate, Q114 (6)–(7), the noisy-correlation consequence of the exact doubling identity, the energy-free and trace/KL counterexamples, and high-precision synthesis S. Their full proofs and parameter costs are above. No independent analytic review has yet occurred. The tests are exact finite diagnostics, not proofs of universal operator assertions.
164: **Unresolved:** B, the deficit-to-balance estimate for arbitrary near-minimizers, and a correlated-Gaussian-reference replacement for Route II. G was screened but not developed into a theorem. **Excluded:** GAD strong converse is already in current primary literature and in I07; arbitrary-partner additivity and the zero-key channel flag completion are already source operations; conditional EPnI is not a valid generic bridge. No completed partial result is promoted merely because it is absent verbatim from a source theorem title.

265: # I117 — Original I114 audit and unequal-temperature bosonic entropy stability
266: **Terminal disposition: original I114 analytic claims PASS at their original scopes, without statement repairs. Target B remains unproved and unrefuted. No substantial finding nominated; eight remain accepted.** This is an independent AI proof/disposition audit and a continuation, not human review or formal certification. In particular, the original report never asserted Target B. Its failure to solve B is preserved.
267: The new results are: an exact fixed-bath factorization with an explicit deficit transfer; uniqueness of the centered optimizer at the full finite-energy, internally entangled input scope; and two rare-tail constructions satisfying **both** the fixed entropy and fixed energy cap. They rule out a uniform strict thermal-relative-entropy contraction improvement and deficit-to-weighted-balance or deficit-to-ordinary-balance moduli, even qualitatively. An entropy-preserving rotation also forces any possible power exponent to be at most 1/2. They do **not** refute Target B, which permits smaller exponents. The factorization and uniqueness are technical consequences of accepted EPnI and the independently reconstructed linear-qEPI equality mechanism. The tail constructions are technical obstructions, not a ninth finding.

279: ## 2. Independent verdict on ORIGINAL B114
280: **(1) PASS; (4), including domains and gap, PASS.** For a finite-energy positive trace-one operator, all four products with a single ladder operator are trace class: for example `aρ=(a√ρ)√ρ`, with both factors Hilbert–Schmidt. Creation costs one additional photon. Their Hilbert–Schmidt extensions satisfy
281: \[
282:  \|a_j\rho\|_2^2=\operatorname{Tr}(N_j\rho^2)\le\operatorname{Tr}(N_j\rho),
283:  \qquad \|\rho a_j\|_2^2=\operatorname{Tr}((N_j+1)\rho^2)\le\operatorname{Tr}((N_j+1)\rho).
284: \]
285: These are positive-form traces, not cyclic permutations of undefined unbounded products. One proves them with bounded number cutoffs and takes the Hilbert–Schmidt limit. Positivity `ρ²≤ρ` bounds each sum.
286: In number-basis vectorization, right multiplication by `a` is creation on the second factor. Thus `D_j|ρ⟩=|R_j⟩` and `E_j|ρ⟩=|R_j†⟩`, including the order of the two terms. Both lists are needed; using only D would leave an infinite-dimensional kernel. The finite-mode squeezing transformation in I114 is a genuine unitary canonical transformation. One domain-level construction uses the Schrödinger representation: the real hyperbolic coordinate transformation and its inverse transpose on momenta are implemented by the associated unitary change of variables on L². Schwartz vectors are a core for the transformed oscillator forms. On that core the displayed cosh/sinh formulas hold; closure identifies the forms on the full space. This also identifies the exponential convention in I114: with `U=exp[θ(ab−a†b†)]`, `U†aU=a coshθ−b†sinhθ`. Hence
287:  \sum_j(D_j^*D_j+E_j^*E_j)=(2r+1)U^*N_{2n}U
288: as closed forms. Its gap above its unique vacuum is exactly `2r+1`; it is not inferred merely from commutators or a formal recurrence. The normalized vacuum has coefficients `(1−q²)^{n/2}q^{|p|}` on `|p,p⟩`, `q=r/(r+1)`. The finite-energy estimates above put `|ρ⟩` in this form domain. Projection gives `(2r+1)||ρ−cτ_r||₂²≤2Σ||R_j||₂²`. Finally `Tr τ_r²=(2r+1)^{-n}` and `||τ_r||∞=(r+1)^{-n}` give precisely `0≤c≤C_{r,n}`. No bounded inverse of ρ was used.
289: **(2) PASS.** For `P=1_{N≤K}`, `d_K=binom(K+n,n)` and `qσ=Tr σ(1−P)`, pure-state diagonalization gives `||σ−PσP||₁=√(4qσ−3qσ²)≤2√qσ`; convexity proves the mixed-state inequality. Then
290:  \|\rho-c\tau_r\|_1\le\sqrt{d_K}\,t+
291:  2\sqrt{E/(K+1)}+2c\sqrt{nr/(K+1)}.
292: The absolute trace `|1−c|` is bounded by this same expression. Adding it gives exactly (2), including the factors two and four. Applying a rank estimate directly to the uncompressed infinite operator would be invalid; I114 did not do that.
293: **(3) PASS, including t=0.** The cube `{0,…,K}^n` contains the total-number simplex, so `d_K≤(K+1)^n`. With `K+1=ceil(t^{-2/(n+1)})`, the rank term is at most `2^{1+n/2}t^{1/(n+1)}` and the tail term has exactly the other displayed coefficients. At zero the Hilbert–Schmidt identity gives `ρ=cτ_r`, and normalization fixes c. At large t the independent trace-distance cap is two.
294: **(5), metric duality and centered test density, PASS.** In the eigenbasis of faithful ρ the coordinate weight is
295:  w_{lr}=h(r)\ell(rp_l,(r+1)p_r)\le h(r)(2r+1)/2.
296: Its diagonal value is exactly `p_l`, since `h(r)ℓ(r,r+1)=1`. Therefore orthogonal projection off I is subtraction of the ρ-mean, and is contractive. The source actually admits finite eigenblocks centered by the full identity; I114's finite-block approximation is also in that space because its ρ-mean is zero. For `Z_M=Z−[TrρZ/TrρP_M]P_M`, with P_M containing Z's support, the squared F-norm of `I−P_M` is `Trρ(I−P_M)→0`; coefficients converge, so `Z_M→Z−TrρZ I` in F. Trace-class R gives convergence of its pairings, and centering gives `q(I)=0`. Thus
297:  |\operatorname{Tr}R_j^*Z|\le\|q_j\|_{F,*}\sqrt{h(r)(2r+1)/2}\,\|Z\|_2.
298: Finite eigenblock matrices are Hilbert–Schmidt dense. Taking the dual norm proves (5). There is no assertion that every finite-energy state has finite Q; this restriction matters, and the new sequence in §4 has finite but divergent Q.
299: **Regularized-minimizer rate and undoing displacement: PASS at the exact original scope.** The source defines `A_*²=Σw_iQ_i²`, `M_*²=Σw_i|μ_i|²` and proves `A_*²+M_*²≤2Cζ/ε` with fixed positive w_i, fixed auxiliary parameters, and uniformly bounded input energies. Displacement reduces energy, preserves spectrum and the F norm, and converts the centered ladder defect to R. Thus (3),(5) give `O(ζ^{1/[2(n+1)]})` for each centered input. If `D(α)=e^{iHα}`, purification and the spectral inequality `|e^{ix}−1|≤|x|` give
300:  \|D(\alpha)\tau_rD(\alpha)^*-\tau_r\|_1
301:  \le2\sqrt{\operatorname{Tr}\tau_rH_\alpha^2}
302:  =2\sqrt{2r+1}\,|\alpha|.
303: The generator has finite second moment on that purification. Since the means are `O(√ζ)`, undoing displacement preserves the asserted weaker exponent for `ζ≤1`. The input seventh moments used by the source are properties of its constructed Gibbs states, not assumptions on Target B's arbitrary inputs. Neither the rate nor the Hessian inequality applies to arbitrary nonnegative-deficit states. In fact the direct weighted-Q transfer proposed as a possible bridge is false, as §4 proves.
304: ## 3. Independent verdict on ORIGINAL Q114, doubling, counterexamples and S
305: **Q114 (6) and (7): PASS without repairs.** The heterodyne Fourier transform contributes `exp(−|w|²/4)` at `w/√2` and the prepared `τ_{1/2}` contributes `exp(−|w|²)`, so the total is exactly `χρ(w/√2)exp(−5|w|²/4)`. This is `T_{1/2,2}`. The inverse quadratic form at vacuum is `(3/2)^m exp(|z|²)`, finite for every z. Cauchy–Schwarz with `π_z^{-1/2}|0⟩` proves the stated positive vacuum minorant. Integrating its coefficient gives `G=3^{-m}2^{-N}`; it has no kernel but no positive uniform lower bound.
306: For an ancillary positive operator, the heterodyne operator-valued measure on the reference is positive, and its tensor product with each positive prepared kernel is positive. Integrating proves complete positivity on every finite ancillary dimension. These normal trace-bounded maps extend to trace class and separable references by finite-rank compression and trace-norm continuity. The branch-0 channel is
307:  \Psi_0(X)=\Lambda_0((I-G)^{-1/2}X(I-G)^{-1/2});
308: the inverse here is bounded, with norm at most `(1−3^{-m})^{-1/2}`, and the effect identity proves trace preservation. This is distinct from the unbounded G inverse later compressed.
309: Both branch probabilities are positive: p₁>0 by injectivity, p₀≥1−3^{-m}. The filter commutes with N, so branch input energy is ≤E/p_f. Its reference marginal satisfies `ω_K=Σp_fω_K^f`; entropy concavity gives `S(ω_K^f)≤S(ω_K)/p_f`. Total output energy is finite, and positivity bounds branch output energy by the total divided by p_f. Joint entropies are bounded above by sums of marginal entropies. Thus all the ordinary mutual informations and flagged chain rules are finite. The FK marginal is identical before and after the branch processing, but the conditional K marginal need not be ω_K. Subtracting the two chain-rule bounds gives `ℓ≥p I(ξ)`, exactly with `ξ_K`.
310: Pinsker, obtained by a Helstrom binary measurement and classical Pinsker in nats, then gives
311:  \|\sqrt G\,\omega\sqrt G-(\sqrt G\omega_X\sqrt G)\otimes\xi_K\|_1
312:  \le\sqrt{2p\ell}\le\sqrt{2\ell}.
313: The operator `B_L=P_LG^{-1/2}` is bounded with squared norm `3^m2^L`. Multiplying by it yields the compressed product comparison with ξ_K. Both compared full states have the same X energy. Their two gentle tails cost `4√(E/(L+1))`. Partial trace controls the change `ξ_K→ω_K` by the same bound, giving exactly `2·3^m2^L√(2ℓ)+8√(E/(L+1))`. Taking the specified floor gives (7), including its coefficient 16 and its natural logarithm. At ℓ=0 take L to infinity after fixing it. These arguments work for arbitrary separable K with finite entropy, not merely a classical or bounded-dimensional reference.
314: **Exact doubling/noisy-correlation consequence: PASS.** With four independent inputs and memory `M=A_+B_+`, the A-group/B-group tensor factorization gives `I(A_-;B_-|M)=0`. The chain rules and commuting passive rotations give, for linear weight η,
315:  L_\eta(A_1,B_1)+L_\eta(A_2,B_2)
316:  =L_\eta(A_+,B_+)+L_\eta(A_-,B_-|M)+I(C_-;E_+|C_+).
317: Every entropy is finite because total energy is finite. Both entropy deficits on the right are nonnegative; the needed conditional linear qEPI is proved in §5, with no conditional EPnI. Set the second pair to `(τ₂,τ₂)`, whose rotated outputs are independent τ₂ states. If `σ_CJ=(id⊗Φ)ω_CE`, the other circuit ordering gives
318:  I(C_-;J|C_+)=I(C;J)_\sigma-I(C_+;J)_{(\Phi\otimes id)\sigma}.
319: Independence of the added C thermal from CJ is essential here. Hence Q114 controls the noisy correlations with loss at most Lη. At the original unequal thermal pair, however, `Lη=n[g(s)−ηg(r)−(1−η)g(b)]>0` while Δ=0, and the output cross covariance is nonzero. This failure is preserved. It does not become a Δ estimate merely by subtracting a scalar from an inequality whose other terms also have nonzero baselines.
320: **Both original counterexamples: PASS at their stated scopes.** In the equal-weight vacuum/Fock-bit example the correlation distance is one and the information is log 2. Expanding the output number gives mean `k/2+1` and variance `(5k+8)/4`. The vacuum output mean is one. The Markov/Chebyshev decision rule has average error at most `12/k+16/k²`; Fano yields loss tending to zero. The energies k/2 diverge: this disproves only an energy-cap-free information-loss modulus. In the original thermal/Fock mixture with weight c₀/k, trace distance vanishes, energy tends to nr+c₀ and entropy tends to ng(r), giving relative entropy limit h(r)c₀. Its entropy was not fixed exactly, so it was correctly not called a Target B counterexample. Section 4 strengthens this obstruction while preserving the target constraints.
321: **Symmetries, endpoints, compactness: PASS.** Displacements preserve the two entropies and move the optimizer; common finite-mode symplectic transformations commute with the scalar two-port mixing but generally change the fixed isotropic bath. Passive invariance does not produce a distinct isotropic thermal state. At η=0,1 arbitrary inputs provide trivial equality, so uniqueness at those endpoints is false. At E=nr entropy-energy equality forces τ_r. Finite-energy caps give compactness and entropy continuity; centeredness is closed, since the cutoff estimate on first moments in §4 tends uniformly to zero. Even with the new uniqueness result this yields only an unspecified modulus, not a power. It is not offered as a solution to B.
322: **High-precision synthesis S: PASS, technical at full source-operation scope.** The actual fields/encoders are defined for every L≥1, not just the source's final convenient choice L=10000J. Choose the least integer L with `72J(3/4)^L≤2^{-k}`. It is deterministically computable by rational integer arithmetic in polynomial time and is O(k+log(n+1)). The source's dimensions are `h=ceil(log₂(L+1))`, `b=nL+h`, `K=b(L+1)`. The modulus is strictly larger than `L2^{nL}`, so the power-sum argument retains all multisets and the row bound is L. There is one smaller synthesis call per level. Summing its actual qubit/slot bounds gives exactly the two O-expressions in I114.
323: For compilation retain the stronger recurrence already proved in the source: `e_{j+1}≤C₀e_j^{3/2}`, `ℓ_{j+1}≤5ℓ_j`. Hardcode a sufficiently fine fixed net and a rational upper bound on C₀. With `b₀=C₀²e₀<1`, induction gives `C₀²e_j≤b₀^{(3/2)^j}`. Choosing j by integer upper bounds yields `ℓ=O((1+log(1/ξ))^{log5/log(3/2)})`, and the generator only needs this length, not a search for the word. Use per-slot error `ξ=2^{-k}/[4(S+1)]` and the phase-sensitive two-word construction; each word is approximated to ξ/2. The second word acts on a zero scratch bit and keeps the scalar phase as a coherent relative phase. Fixed-symbol controlled H,T,T† and reversible lookup are exact by the source's explicit gate constructions. Address length is `O(Q+log(S+1)+log(ℓ+1))`. Ideal-prefix telescoping applies each error only to clean scratch and bounds total compilation error by ε/4. With the skeleton error ≤ε/4, clean isometry error is ≤ε/2. Tensoring a reference leaves this operator norm unchanged; the pure-vector trace bound and partial trace give full diamond error ≤ε.
324: The quantifiers are **one polynomial-time skeleton generator, for every U some U-dependent Boolean truth table**. Oracle synthesis, storage and truth-table generation are not polynomially bounded. Coherent supplied labels add their bit length to resources and preserve the same blockwise norm proof. Ordinary channel composition alone would not check these requirements; the actual lower operations do. No efficient description of an arbitrary U was inferred.
325: **Contribution comparisons: PASS at the scopes actually asserted.** B114 uses a thermal oscillator gap plus energy truncation; Q114 uses Li's explicit instrument plus compressed inversion; both are technical. S is paid by the full accepted encoder and commutator-recursion operations. AD already handles arbitrary finite-dimensional CPTP partners and entangled signal inputs; its partner may still require capacity regularization. Fang v2's sandwiched CP-map multiplicativity is stronger and covers all Rényi orders and arbitrary entangled codes in the screened unassisted problem. This external exact duplicate is not inferred from ordinary Holevo additivity. ZK already includes tensor powers, its admitted completed/countable public-record protocols, conditioning at every positive success probability, and the 2m+1-dimensional absorbing-flag completion; the full Choi matrix can have a product flag corner even though another corner certifies entanglement. PR's full transfer gives δ^{1+1/β} from conditioning error O(τ^β); β=1/4 gives δ⁵, and its root construction gives β=1/2 only with κ_μ. Neither a commuting-operator result nor a distribution-uniform cubic threshold follows. G was unresolved, not disproved. None of these comparisons is a universal literature absence claim.
326: ## 4. Route I — paying localization and small-eigenvalue costs; a fixed-entropy obstruction
327: Fix the original parameters n≥2, r,b>0 unequal, 0<η<1 and E>nr. Write `s=ηr+(1−η)b`, `H=ng(r)`, and
328:  f(x)=n g\bigl(\eta g^{-1}(x/n)+(1-\eta)b\bigr),\qquad
329:  F(\sigma)=S(T_{\eta,b}\sigma)-f(S(\sigma)).
330: EPnI gives F≥0 for every finite-energy σ. On the target constraint, F(ρ)=Δ. This extension allows auxiliary variations without pretending their entropies stay fixed.
331: ### 4.1 Explicit cutoff and entropy budgets
332: Let P_K be the total-number cutoff, q=Trρ(1−P_K)≤E/(K+1)<1/2, and `ρ_K=P_KρP_K/(1−q)`. Then
333:  \|\rho-\rho_K\|_1\le2\sqrt q+q,
334:  \quad \operatorname{Tr}\rho_KN\le E/(1-q),
335:  \quad |S(\rho)-S(\rho_K)|\le B_E(q),
336: where
337:  B_E(q)=h_2(q)+qn g(E/(nq))+qn g(E/[n(1-q)]).
338: To prove the entropy bound, pinch into P_K and its complement. Recording the binary measurement changes entropy by a number in [0,h₂(q)] (the isometry recording its outcome has binary environment; subadditivity/Araki–Lieb give the upper bound). The pinched entropy is `h₂(q)+(1−q)S(ρ_K)+qS(ρ_Q)`. Bound the two conditional entropies by their thermal energy bounds; taking the absolute difference proves B_E. The zero-q values are continuous limits.
339: For clarity an explicit entropy continuity budget can be obtained without small-eigenvalue assumptions. If two states have energy ≤F₀ and half trace distance d≤1/8, set `M=ceil(F₀/d²)` and `D=binom(M+n,n)`. Both cutoff tails are ≤d². Their normalized compressed states have half distance at most `2d/(1−d²)≤3d`; this follows by compressing their difference and bounding the difference of their normalization traces by 2d. Finite-dimensional entropy continuity, followed by the preceding two tail bounds, gives
340:  \omega_{F_0}(d)=3d\log D+h_2(3d)+2B_{F_0}(d^2)
341: as an upper bound on the entropy difference. Here use the bound with actual compressed distance x≤3d, `x log D+h₂(x)`, increasing for x≤3/8; D=1 is harmless. Each term of B is increasing on the relevant q range or bounded by its displayed endpoint using the perspective concavity of g. Thus `ω_{F₀}(d)=O_{n,F₀}(d log(1/d))`. F₀=0 means both states are vacuum and is separate. This derives the output continuity cost used next, rather than assuming a hidden uniform Hessian bound.
342: Smooth inside P_K by `σ=(1−θ)ρ_K+θP_K/d_K`, θ=K^{-2}, K≥2. Its eigenvalue floor on that block is `m=θ/d_K`, its energy is ≤E/(1−q)+1/K, and its distance from ρ_K is ≤2θ. Its entropy changes by at most `h₂(θ)+θlog d_K`. Channel contraction and the preceding modulus, with a fixed enlarged output energy cap, give
343:  0\le F(\sigma)\le\Delta+C_{n,E,r,b,\eta}\,K^{-1/2}\log(K+1).
344:  \tag{L1}
345: For sufficiently large K the input entropies stay in a fixed compact interval about H>0, where f', f'' are bounded. The finitely many excluded K are irrelevant to this route. The input cutoff entropy error is smaller, O(log K/K); the output coherence/continuity error is the larger term in L1.
346: Centering is also paid. Since a_j lowers total number, `a_j−P_Ka_jP_K=a_j(1−P_K)`. Hilbert–Schmidt Cauchy–Schwarz gives
347:  \sum_j|\operatorname{Tr}\rho a_j(1-P_K)|^2
348:  \le q\sum_j\operatorname{Tr}\rho a_ja_j^*=q(E+n).
349: For centered ρ this bounds the squared mean of ρ_K by `q(E+n)/(1−q)²`; the diagonal smoothing has zero mean. Displacing σ by its own mean reduces its energy and leaves F unchanged. These auxiliary states are used only to test a route, never substituted for the original input in Target B.
350: For a traceless Hermitian block matrix A with `||A||₂=1`, two-sided positivity of `σ+tA` is guaranteed only for `|t|≤m/2`. At such points the input entropy Hessian has absolute value at most `2/m`. The output BKM quadratic form is no greater than the input one by differentiating relative-entropy data processing on this finite input block. Output logarithms are legitimate: `σ+tA≥(m/2)|0⟩⟨0|` implies
351:  T(\sigma+tA)\ge(m/2)\tau_{(1-\eta)b},
352:  \quad -\log T(\sigma+tA)\le C+|\log(m/2)|+h((1-\eta)b)N
353: as forms. Finite input blocks have all moments, and the thermal channel sends them to exponentially decaying output number tails; equivalently the characteristic polynomial times Gaussian gives this property. Resolvent differentiation, dominated by these logarithmic/energy bounds and the input BKM bound, justifies the entropy derivatives. Also `|DS(A)|≤√d_K |log(m/2)|`. Consequently along the allowed segment
354:  |D^2F(A,A)|\le H_K:={2(1+\sup|f'|)\over m}
355:  +\sup|f''|\,d_K\log^2(m/2).
356: Global F≥0 and Taylor's theorem give the actual boundary-aware estimate
357:  |DF_\sigma(A)|\le\inf_{0<t\le m/2}
358:  \{F(\sigma)/t+H_Kt/2\}.\tag{L2}
359: The square-root choice requires `F(σ)≤H_Km²/8`. In the present budget, `m≈K^{-(n+2)}` while L1 only bounds the excess by `Δ+O(K^{-1/2}log K)`. The uniform bound L1 does not meet that requirement at increasing K; the endpoint t=m/2 instead costs `2F(σ)/m+H_Km/4`. Thus this localization calculation supplies **no** vanishing gradient bound. This is an obstruction to this budget, not a proof that every actual cutoff realizes its worst error. A trace-ball constraint would additionally have a normal functional; dropping it is unjustified. A fourth-moment penalty on original ρ is unavailable. No infinite-dimensional Łojasiewicz assertion is used.
360: ### 4.2 Exact entropy repair of rare Fock tails
361: The following construction disproves the strongest natural missing transfer on **the original constraint set**. Choose any `0<c<E−nr`. For integers k tending to infinity let p=c/k, `v_k=|k,0,…,0⟩`, and define
362:  \rho_k(x)=(1-p)\tau_x+p|v_k\rangle\langle v_k|.
363: For every sufficiently large k there is `x_k∈(r/2,r)` such that
364:  S(\rho_k(x_k))=ng(r),\quad
365:  r-x_k=O(\log k/k),\quad
366:  \operatorname{Tr}\rho_k(x_k)N<E.
367:  \tag{T1}
368: Here is an exact entropy formula, avoiding any numerical root assertion. Put `a=(1-p)t_k(x)`, `t_k(x)=(x+1)^{-n}(x/(x+1))^k`, and
369:  J(a,p)=(a+p)h_2\bigl(p/(a+p)\bigr)
370:  =a\log(1+p/a)+p\log(1+a/p).
371: The classical label distinguishing the two components is ambiguous only at v_k. The entropy chain rule for their diagonal laws gives
372:  S(\rho_k(x))=(1-p)ng(x)+h_2(p)-J(a,p).\tag{T2}
373: At x=r, `J≤a[1+log(1+p/a)]` is exponentially small times O(k), whereas `h₂(p)−p ng(r)>0` eventually. At x=r/2, the upper mixture bound `(1-p)ng(r/2)+h₂(p)` is less than ng(r) eventually. Continuity in x (also direct from T2) and the intermediate value theorem give x_k. From concavity and the upper mixture entropy bound at that root,
374:  0\le ng(r)-ng(x_k)\le h_2(p),
375: so `r−x_k≤h₂(p)/(n h(r))`. The exact energy is `(1-p)nx_k+c<nr+c<E`. These states are faithful, centered, have no finite-support restriction, and satisfy every target hypothesis. Their use as counterexamples to a proposed universal transfer needs no entanglement: a diagonal subfamily already suffices.
376: They converge in trace norm to τ_r, because thermal states are trace-continuous in x on `[r/2,r]` by domination by a summable geometric law and p→0. Their energies tend to nr+c, not nr. The entropy identity gives
377:  D(\rho_k\|\tau_r)\longrightarrow h(r)c>0.\tag{T3}
378: The outputs converge in trace norm to τ_s with uniformly bounded energy, so their entropies converge and `Δ_k→0`. More explicitly mixture concavity/upper mixing entropy and
379: `S(T|v_k⟩⟨v_k|)≤n g((ηk+(1−η)nb)/n)` give `0≤Δ_k=O(log k/k)`. This is an upper bound, not a lower asymptotic and not a power-bound counterexample.
380: For the centered weighted defect Q at the **fixed parameter r**, one matrix entry already diverges. Let u=v_k−e₁ and v=v_k. Its squared contribution to the Hilbert dual norm is
381:  {k[(r+1)P_v-rP_u]^2\over
382:  h(r)\ell(rP_u,(r+1)P_v)}
383:  ={k[(r+1)P_v-rP_u]\over h(r)}
384:  \log{(r+1)P_v\over rP_u},\tag{T4}
385: where `P_v=p+(1-p)t_k(x_k)` and `P_u=(1-p)t_{k-1}(x_k)`. The test `|u⟩⟨v|` is centered, so restriction to centered tests changes nothing. On `[r/2,r]` the thermal probabilities decay uniformly exponentially, and x_k→r. Thus
386:  (r+1)P_v-rP_u=(r+1)c/k+O(e^{-a_0k}),
387:  \quad \log{(r+1)P_v\over rP_u}=k h(x_k)+O(\log k),
388: and T4 divided by k tends to `(r+1)c`. In particular `Q(ρ_k)^2≥((r+1)c/2)k` eventually. For each fixed k its full dual sum is finite: away from finitely many edges incident to v_k, probabilities are a common multiple of the thermal law at x_k, so the sum is a convergent geometric first-moment sum; the exceptional edges have positive finite probabilities. Each state even has all moments, but those moments have no common cap (`Trρ_kN²≥ck`).
389: Consequently **no** function m(d)→0 can bound Q(ρ) by m(Δ) on the original target set. In particular the direct proposed `Q≤CΔ^β` bridge is false for every β>0. Likewise no bound `D(ρ||τ_r)≤m(Δ)` holds there. Ordinary Hilbert–Schmidt R is a different defect; T4 alone says nothing against a transfer to that weaker defect. The next, separate construction closes that loophole. Neither construction contradicts the original B114 theorem.
390: ### 4.3 Entropy-preserving rotation: ordinary balance obstruction and exponent ceiling
391: Keep `0<c<E−nr`. Write `p₀=(r+1)^{-n}`, `p_k=p₀(r/(r+1))^k`, `d_k=p₀−p_k`. For sufficiently large k≥2 let U_k be a real rotation in the span of vacuum and v_k, identity elsewhere, with
392:  \sin^2\theta_k={c\over k d_k},\qquad
393:  \widehat\rho_k=U_k\tau_rU_k^*.
394: Its diagonal differs from τ_r only by subtracting c/k at vacuum and adding c/k at v_k. Its only new off-diagonal pair has magnitude `d_k sinθ_k cosθ_k`. Consequently the state is faithful, centered (no new matrix entry connects number states differing by one photon in one mode), and
395:  S(\widehat\rho_k)=ng(r),\quad
396:  \operatorname{Tr}\widehat\rho_kN=nr+c<E,\quad
397:  \|\widehat\rho_k-\tau_r\|_1=2\sqrt{cd_k/k}.
398:  \tag{T5}
399: The entropy equality is exact unitary invariance. The trace norm is the sum of the two absolute eigenvalues of the traceless 2×2 difference, namely `d_k|sinθ_k|`. Thus these are actual original-scope states, not approximately corrected inputs.
400: For the ordinary balance residual at fixed r, its `(v_k−e₁,0)` entry is `(r+1)√k (widehatρ_k)_{v_k,0}`: the corresponding entry of ρa is zero. Therefore
401:  \sum_j\|R_j(\widehat\rho_k)\|_2^2
402:  \ge(r+1)^2\bigl(cd_k-c^2/k\bigr)
403:  \longrightarrow(r+1)^2cp_0>0.\tag{T6}
404: All these products are defined by finite energy as in the original audit. This proves failure of any vanishing deficit-to-ordinary-balance modulus once the deficit convergence below is checked. It does not contradict B114: that is a sufficient bound on trace distance in terms of R, not a converse, and R is not trace-continuous on an energy cap.
405: Here the deficit has a sharper elementary upper bound. Let `p=c/k`, and let the global phase twirl be the average of `exp(iϕN)`. It removes the sole new off-diagonal pair and leaves the diagonal state
406: `barρ_k=τ_r+p(|v_k⟩⟨v_k|−|0⟩⟨0|)`. The thermal attenuator commutes with this twirl. Entropy concavity therefore gives `S(T widehatρ_k)≤S(T barρ_k)`. For large k, p<p₀ and
407: `σ_k=(τ_r−p|0⟩⟨0|)/(1−p)` is a state. We have `barρ_k=(1−p)σ_k+p|v_k⟩⟨v_k|` and `τ_r=(1−p)σ_k+p|0⟩⟨0|`. Apply the upper mixture entropy bound to the first identity after T and entropy concavity to the second. Subtracting yields
408:  0\le\widehat\Delta_k
409:  \le h_2(p)+p\{S(T|v_k\rangle\langle v_k|)-S(T|0\rangle\langle0|)\}
410:  \le h_2(c/k)+{cn\over k}g\left({\eta k+(1-\eta)nb\over n}\right)
411:  =O(\log k/k).\tag{T7}
412: All mixture entropies are finite by their explicit energies. The global twirl can be obtained by finite convex averages and energy-entropy continuity, so no differential entropy is involved. The lower bound is stipulated EPnI applied to the original fixed-entropy state. Uniqueness in §5 also makes these deficits strictly positive, since the centered states are not τ_r.
413: Equations T5 and T7 show that **no Target B estimate can hold with α>1/2**, for any of its fixed allowed parameter choices: the ratio of distance to Δ^α is bounded below by a constant times `k^{α−1/2}/(log k)^α`, which diverges. They leave all `0<α≤1/2` open. They also give `D(widehatρ_k||τ_r)=h(r)c` exactly. The construction is a technical energy-preserving-budget test using an entropy-preserving two-level unitary, not a full counterexample to B and not a substantial nomination.
414: ## 5. Route II — exact baseline subtraction, factorization, and uniqueness
415: ### 5.1 Thermal and correlated reference identities
416: For any original target input with e=TrρN, thermal relative entropy gives the exact identities
417:  D_{\rm in}=h(r)(e-nr),\qquad
418:  D_{\rm out}=\eta h(s)(e-nr)-\Delta,
419:  \qquad \Delta=c_*D_{\rm in}-D_{\rm out},
420:  \quad c_*={\eta h(s)\over h(r)}\in(0,1).\tag{R1}
421: The energy relation uses the independent centered thermal bath; arbitrary internal input entanglement is allowed. Positivity of D gives e≥nr. To check c_*<1, `s>ηr`, h is decreasing, and strict concavity of log gives `η log(1+1/(ηr))<log(1+1/r)`. EPnI is exactly the sharp contraction `Dout≤c_*Din` on this entropy slice. The rare-tail sequence gives `Dout/Din→c_*`, so a uniform strict coefficient smaller than c_* is impossible **even centered with fixed entropy and fixed energy cap**. This is the actual thermal-relative-entropy obstruction; subtracting the unequal-temperature scalar baseline alone does not fix it.
422: For the full beam-splitter output Ω and the correlated Gaussian reference `Γ=U_η(τ_r⊗τ_b)U_η*`, unitary invariance gives `D(Ω||Γ)=Din`. Its marginals are τ_s and `τ_t`, `t=(1−η)r+ηb`. The logarithm of Γ is a quadratic Hamiltonian with a cross term proportional to `h(r)−h(b)`; it is not the sum of marginal logarithms. Define `K=logΓ−log(τ_s⊗τ_t)`. All its expectations are finite because its cross ladder terms are bounded in quadratic-form expectation by total energy. Direct expansion gives
423:  D(\Omega\|\Gamma)=D(\Omega_C\|\tau_s)+D(\Omega_E\|\tau_t)
424:  +I(C;E)_\Omega-\operatorname{Tr}\Omega K.\tag{R2}
425: At Γ, `TrΓK=I(C;E)_Γ=n[g(s)+g(t)−g(r)−g(b)]`. Subtracting that baseline in R2 yields the exact remainder
426:  D_{\rm in}=D_C+D_E+(I_\Omega-I_\Gamma)-\operatorname{Tr}(\Omega-\Gamma)K.
427: Neither of the last two terms has a favorable sign separately. In particular it is not a sum of nonnegative deficits that Δ controls. This is the correlated-reference calculation, with its full missing term retained. No product-output assumption is imposed on unequal-temperature extremizers.
428: ### 5.2 Exact transfer to an equal-temperature channel on the SAME input
429: **Proposition F117.** Set
430:  u={\eta+\min\{1,s/r\}\over2},\qquad t=\eta/u,
431:  \qquad c={s-ur\over1-u}.
432: Then `η<u<1`, `0<t<1`, c>0, and
433:  T_{\eta,b}=T_{u,c}\circ T_{t,r}.\tag{F1}
434: For every target input, with `δ=S(T_{t,r}ρ)−ng(r)≥0`,
435:  0\le\delta\le\Delta/\kappa,
436:  \qquad \kappa={u\,h(uM+(1-u)c)\over h(r)}>0,
437:  \quad M={tE+(1-t)nr\over n}.\tag{F2}
438: **Proof.** The scalar channel characteristic formula proves composition: the signal coefficient is ut=η and the added photon number is `u(1−t)r+(1−u)c=s−ηr=(1−η)b`. The vacuum half-photon contributions also agree because the attenuations multiply. Thus this is equality of channels on arbitrary inputs, not just Gaussian states. EP gives δ≥0. If `x=g^{-1}(S(T_{t,r}ρ)/n)`, energy and entropy-energy bounds give r≤x≤M. Apply EP to the second factor:
439:  \Delta\ge n[g(ux+(1-u)c)-g(s)].
440: The derivative of the right-hand thermal entropy as a function of input entropy is `u h(ux+(1−u)c)/h(x)`, bounded below on [r,M] by κ. Integrating from r to x proves F2. All states retain finite energies. ∎
441: This does not choose a new physical bath for the target or replace ρ by a nearby state. The original fixed channel is exactly decomposed, and the same ρ is the first input. It does not by itself establish a stability theorem at equal temperature; in particular it is **not** a replacement of Target B by an asserted solved special case. It explains why the positive original Li baseline can be bypassed by a different circuit, rather than incorrectly bounded in the original circuit.
442: ### 5.3 The external linear-qEPI operation, proved at the needed scope
443: Here is a proof of the needed conditional **linear** qEPI for arbitrary finite-energy bosonic A,B and finite-entropy memory M with `I(A;B|M)=0`. It reconstructs De Palma–Trevisan's integral-information argument; the heat asymptotic is established explicitly, so the external theorem is not an additional stipulated premise.
444: Let `N_v` be additive Gaussian displacement noise adding v photons per mode, and write `H_X(v)=S(X|M)_{(N_v⊗id)ρ}`. Finite energy bounds S(X); finite S(M) bounds joint entropy. Noise increments equal the conditional mutual information with their classical Gaussian displacement label Z, since displacements leave the conditional entropies of the labelled branches unchanged:
445:  H_X(v+w)-H_X(v)=I(X;Z|M)\ge0.
446: Increasing v is an additional channel on X; data processing makes each fixed-w increment decrease. Hence H_X is concave. At v=0 it is continuous: strong continuity of displacements gives trace convergence; lower semicontinuity of joint entropy gives the lower limit, and lower semicontinuity of `D(ρ_XM||τ_a⊗ρ_M)` together with exact energy `e+nv` gives the upper limit through the identity
447: `S(X|M)=h(a)e+nlog(a+1)−D(ρ_XM||τ_a⊗ρ_M)`. This also proves continuity at all finite v. Concavity gives local absolute continuity and finite derivatives almost everywhere away from zero; continuity at zero makes the improper derivative integral recover the increment there as well.
448: For the large-v asymptotic, for v>1 the channel has the explicit heterodyne representation
449:  N_v(\rho)=\int Q_\rho(z)D(z)\tau_{v-1}D(z)^*\,d^{2n}z.
450: Indeed the heterodyne transform contributes `e^{-|w|²/2}` and `τ_{v-1}` contributes `e^{-(v-1/2)|w|²}`, giving `χρ(w)e^{-v|w|²}`. Positivity of the kernels proves the ancillary version. If the input has energy e, output entropy is between `ng(v−1)` by concavity and `ng(v+e/n)` by its energy. In the joint output, Y and M are independent given the classical heterodyne label Z. Data processing from the classical Z to its conditional M state gives
451:  I(Y;M)\le I(Y;Z)=S(Y)-ng(v-1).
452: Continuous classical registers can be interpreted through relative-entropy integrals or finite measurable partitions followed by monotone refinement; the displayed Holevo quantity is finite by the preceding entropy bounds. Therefore
453:  ng(v-1)\le H_Y(v)\le ng(v+e/n),
454:  \qquad H_Y(v)=n\log v+n+o(1).
455: For the Stam step, jointly displace A by √η Z and B by √(1−η) Z, so the retained output is displaced by Z. Conditional independence holds in every labelled branch. The chain identity gives
456:  I(AB;Z|M)=I(A;Z|M)+I(B;Z|M)
457:  -I(A;B|M)+I(A;B|MZ)
458:  \le I(A;Z|M)+I(B;Z|M).
459: Output data processing and the noise-increment identity imply
460: `H_C(w)−H_C(0)≤H_A(ηw)−H_A(0)+H_B((1−η)w)−H_B(0)`. Now evolve A and B by independent noise N_v; conditional independence persists by conditional-mutual-information data processing, and C evolves by N_v. Taking right derivatives, or difference quotients at common differentiability points, shows that
461: `H_C(v)−ηH_A(v)−(1−η)H_B(v)` is nonincreasing. Its large-v limit is zero by the asymptotic just proved. Its value at zero is therefore nonnegative, proving the required inequality. This proof uses no finite fourth moment and includes arbitrary quantum M under the displayed finiteness assumptions. The unconditional case is M trivial.
462: ### 5.4 Full uniqueness, and its precise quantitative limit
463: **Theorem U117 (technical uniqueness).** For fixed finite n≥1, r,b>0, 0<η<1, a finite-energy input with `S(ρ)=ng(r)` has `S(T_{η,b}ρ)=ng(s)` if and only if it is a displacement of τ_r. In particular a centered exact optimizer is τ_r. No energy cap beyond finiteness, faithfulness, mode-product hypothesis, or higher moment is required. This includes the original unequal-temperature parameters.
464: **Proof.** F1–F2 imply `S(T_{t,r}ρ)=ng(r)`; thus the linear deficit for `(ρ,τ_r)` is zero. Apply the exact doubling identity with auxiliary `(τ₂,τ₂)`. The preceding conditional linear qEPI makes its remainders nonnegative, so the noisy mutual-information loss is zero. Q114, whose instrument proof was independently checked above, makes `(id⊗Φ)ω_CE` a product. Weyl characteristic functions remove Φ exactly: its multiplier `exp(−5|w|²/4)` is never zero and its argument rescaling is onto. Thus ω_CE itself is a product. This exact cancellation asserts no quantitative inverse bound.
465: Because one input is known thermal, no external Darmois–Skitovich theorem is needed to finish. In complex Weyl coordinates, put a=√t, d=√(1−t), R=r+1/2. Product outputs give, for all x,y∈Cⁿ (take output arguments x/a and −y/d),
466:  \chi_\rho(x+y)=\chi_\rho(x)\chi_\rho(y)
467:                      e^{-2R\operatorname{Re}(x\cdot\bar y)}.
468: Consequently `φ(x)=e^{R|x|²}χρ(x)` is continuous, φ(0)=1, and φ(x+y)=φ(x)φ(y). It has no zeros: split any x into sufficiently many equal small increments. Also φ(−x)=conjugate φ(x) and φ(x)φ(−x)=1, so it takes values on the unit circle. On a neighborhood of zero choose its continuous argument; additivity there makes that argument real-linear (first on rational multiples and sums, then by continuity), and subdivision extends this to all x. Hence φ(x)=exp(iℓ(x)) for a real linear functional ℓ. This is exactly a displacement phase; finite first moments identify it with the input mean. Thus ρ is a displaced τ_r by Weyl uniqueness. Conversely displacement covariance and thermal mixing prove equality. ∎
469: There is also an explicit, but incomplete for B, quantitative consequence. Let Ψ=T_{t,r}, let ω be its two-port output from `(ρ,τ_r)`, and σ=(id⊗Φ)ω. Equations F2 and the doubling identity give Q114's bound for
470:  \|\sigma-\sigma_C\otimes\sigma_J\|_1
471:  \le\inf_{L\ge0}\left\{2\,3^n2^L\sqrt{2\Delta/\kappa}
472:                 +8\sqrt{(E+nr)/(L+1)}\right\},
473: capped at two. This compares the correct **intermediate** equal-temperature circuit, not the correlated original unequal-temperature circuit. To derive a power bound on ρ one still needs a quantitative reversal and stability of its characteristic equation with all tails controlled. The displayed inverse-filter method gives a logarithmic cost. Cuesta's fourth-moment constants cannot be made uniform by our cutoff, and the rare-tail sequence rules out the weighted-Q and strict relative-entropy shortcuts. No unknown compactness modulus, Gaussian-only result, or nearby auxiliary state is substituted for B.
474: ## 6. Contribution assessment and terminal obligations
475: U117 proves uniqueness, a consequence not asserted in the pinned EPnI introduction, but its full-scope proof reduces to scalar channel composition, EPnI, Li's explicit doubling/instrument operations, conditional linear qEPI, and an elementary thermal characteristic equation. The linear operation was independently reconstructed rather than stipulated. This is a concrete technical reduction, not a substantial new mechanism. F117's explicit constant is useful bookkeeping, not a separate finding. T1–T7 pay the original entropy and energy constraints and falsify both weighted and ordinary deficit-to-balance transfers, as well as a strict relative-entropy contraction gap. T5–T7 also exclude exponents above 1/2. They remain technical obstructions and do not refute B.
476: The original report's failures remain failures: no original entropy-to-defect transfer; no control of original unequal-temperature Lη by Δ; no quantitative removal of the noisy reference supplied by injectivity; no finite-energy fourth-moment bound; no distribution-uniform cubic game rate. The new work improves the first diagnosis from “unpaid” to **false** for both its weighted-Q and ordinary Hilbert–Schmidt-R formulations, and establishes exact optimizer uniqueness. It leaves open a transfer to a suitably weaker, bounded-test defect or an argument that uses additional nonnegative doubling remainders to obtain a power rate. No exponent α_n>0 or valid countersequence violating every such exponent has been proved.
477: The complete Target B, with the original fixed n,E,r,b,η, centered arbitrary internally entangled inputs and the fixed independent thermal bath, is therefore **UNPROVED AND UNREFUTED in this investigation**. All proved new claims above have complete analytic arguments; finite diagnostics below are separate evidence. No nomination is forced.
