550: ## 2. Independent audit of ORIGINAL I117
551: ### 2.1 Cutoff, smoothing, entropy and centering; L1–L2
552: **PASS without repairs.** Put `q=Tr rho(1-P_K)` and `rho_K=P_K rho P_K/(1-q)`. Gentle compression and normalization cost `2sqrt(q)+q`; commutation of `P_K` with N gives energy at most `E/(1-q)`. Pinching has entropy increase `d_pin` in `[0,h_2(q)]`, so, exactly,
553: `S(rho)-S(rho_K)=h_2(q)-d_pin+q[S(rho_Q)-S(rho_K)]`.
554: The two conditional thermal energy bounds yield precisely the displayed `B_E(q)`. This bounds both signs; a continuity statement alone would not pay this budget. At q=0 the terms use their continuous limits. On `q<=1/2`, each energy perspective used to replace a tail by an upper endpoint is increasing: for example the derivative of `q g(c/q)` is `log(1+c/q)>0`; the derivative of `q g(c/(1-q))` is positive. This justifies the endpoint substitutions in the original modulus.
555: For states of energy at most F and half trace distance d, the chosen `M=ceil(F/d^2)` makes both tails at most `d^2`. Their normalized compressions have half distance at most `2d/(1-d^2)<=3d`. The finite-dimensional estimate `x log D+h_2(x)` is increasing for `x<=3/8`, `D>=1`. Two pinching errors then give exactly `omega_F(d)`. For F>0, `log binom(M+n,n)=O_n(log(1/d))`; thus its declared order is correct. F=0 is the separate vacuum case. There is no uniform input Hessian hidden in this argument.
556: Smoothing by `theta=K^-2` has block floor `m=theta/d_K`, energy addition at most `theta K=1/K`, and entropy cost at most `h_2(theta)+theta log d_K`. Output trace contraction with the preceding modulus costs `O(K^-1/2 log K)`, while input entropy truncation costs only `O(log K/K)`. On a fixed neighborhood of `H=ng(r)>0`, f' and f'' are bounded. This proves the original L1, including its nonnegativity from EPnI and its dependence only on fixed parameters.
557: The centering identity also passes. Since `a_j-P_K a_j P_K=a_j(1-P_K)`, Cauchy–Schwarz applied to `(a_j* sqrt(rho))` and `(1-P_K)sqrt(rho)` gives
558: `sum_j |Tr rho a_j(1-P_K)|^2 <= q(Tr rho N+n)`.
559: After division by `(1-q)^2`, this is the original mean bound; diagonal smoothing has zero mean. Displacement reduces energy by the squared mean and preserves both entropies. This does not substitute a centered approximation for the original rho in B.
560: For a Hermitian traceless A with `||A||_2=1`, its operator norm is at most one, hence the admitted two-sided interval is indeed `|t|<=m/2`. The input entropy Hessian is bounded by `2/m`. On the finite input block, differentiation of relative-entropy data processing bounds the output BKM form by that input form. The vacuum minorant `T(sigma+tA)>=(m/2)tau_(1-eta)b` supplies the stated logarithmic form bound; finite input blocks give polynomial times Gaussian characteristic functions and exponentially decaying number tails. Resolvent differentiation is therefore legitimate. Cauchy–Schwarz gives `|DS(A)|<=sqrt(d_K)|log(m/2)|`. The original H_K and Taylor estimate L2 follow.
561: Minimizing `F/t+H_K t/2` unconstrained uses `t=sqrt(2F/H_K)`, admissible exactly when `F<=H_K m^2/8`. Here `m` is of order `K^(-n-2)`, and `H_K m^2` is of that same order, up to a smaller logarithmic correction. L1's supplied error is much larger. The original conclusion is correctly a failure of this **budget**, not a counterexample to all localization arguments. No small-eigenvalue or boundary term was dropped.
562: ### 2.2 Entropy-repaired Fock tails T1–T4
563: **T1, T2, T3, T4 and every stated obstruction: PASS without repairs.** With `p=c/k`, the only ambiguous component label is at `v_k`. The exact ambiguity is
564: `J(a,p)=(a+p)log(a+p)-a log a-p log p`,
565: which gives T2. Uniformly on `[r/2,r]`, the thermal probability at v_k is bounded by a geometric sequence; `J<=a[1+log(1+p/a)]` is exponentially small times O(k). The sign at x=r is positive eventually, and the upper mixture bound at r/2 is strictly below H. The intermediate-value root is actual, not a numerical approximation. The upper mixing inequality at the root yields `H-ng(x_k)<=h_2(p)`, whence `r-x_k<=h_2(p)/(n h(r))`. The energy is `(1-p)nx_k+c<nr+c<E` and tends to `nr+c`. Centering, faithfulness and exact entropy all hold.
566: Thermal trace continuity plus p→0 gives convergence to tau_r. The thermal relative-entropy identity then proves T3, while the output mixing bound and its energy entropy bound give `0<=Delta_k=O(log k/k)`. No lower asymptotic for this deficit was asserted.
567: For the edge `(u,v)=(v_k-e_1,v_k)`, the logarithmic-mean identity gives exactly T4, with its numerator sign matched to the logarithm. The numerator before multiplication by k is `(r+1)c/k+O(exp(-a_0 k))`; the logarithm is `k h(x_k)+O(log k)`. Thus the displayed contribution divided by k tends to `(r+1)c`. The off-diagonal test is centered. All other nonexceptional edges form a geometric first-moment sum, so Q is finite at each k but diverges along the sequence. This is a counterexample to every vanishing weighted-Q or input-relative-entropy transfer on the original constraint set. It says nothing against trace stability, and is not a proof about ordinary R.
568: ### 2.3 Entropy-preserving rotation T5–T7 and exponent ceiling
569: **T5, T6, T7 and `alpha<=1/2`: PASS without repairs.** The two eigenvalues of the difference in the rotated block are `+-d_k sin(theta_k)`. Its trace norm is therefore `2sqrt(c d_k/k)`; its energy increase is exactly c. For k>=2 the only off-diagonal entries do not connect neighboring number states, so every annihilator mean is zero. Entropy is unchanged by the unitary, and strict positivity is preserved.
570: The `(v_k-e_1,0)` entry of rho a is zero, whereas that of a rho is `sqrt(k)rho_(v_k,0)`. Squaring gives precisely `(r+1)^2(c d_k-c^2/k)`. Thus ordinary R stays away from zero. The phase twirl removes just the new coherence, and its covariance with T plus entropy concavity makes the output entropy no smaller. Subtracting the two mixture entropy inequalities for the common state `(tau_r-p|0><0|)/(1-p)` proves T7, including the vacuum subtraction before it is discarded. Every state used in these inequalities has finite energy. Finite twirls converge with a common energy cap, justifying entropy convergence.
571: Together, `d_k->p_0>0` and `Delta_hat<=C log k/k` exclude every `alpha>1/2`. Even without strict positivity, a zero deficit at a nonthermal state would already contradict such a bound; U117 additionally establishes positivity. No exponent in `(0,1/2]` is excluded. The exact relative entropy `h(r)c` and failure of ordinary-R transfer are consistent with B114's one-way sufficient estimate.
572: ### 2.4 R1–R2 and F1–F2
573: **R1 and R2: PASS without repairs.** Entropy is fixed and output energy is `eta e+(1-eta)nb`, giving `D_in=h(r)(e-nr)` and `D_out=eta h(s)(e-nr)-Delta`. Strict scalar log concavity and `s>eta r` give `0<c_*<1`. T1–T3 have a strictly positive limiting D_in, so their ratio really tends to c_* and refutes a uniformly smaller coefficient on the same constraints.
574: For R2, `log Gamma` is the conjugate of two thermal logarithms and hence is quadratic, including its cross term. Every cross expectation is finite by quadratic-form Cauchy–Schwarz and the total-energy bound. Expanding the three relative entropies gives the stated identity, with the minus sign before `Tr Omega K`. At Gamma the expectation equals its mutual information. Subtracting the reference leaves both the mutual-information difference and the cross-Hamiltonian expectation difference; neither can be erased as nonnegative.
575: **F1 and F2, arbitrary internally entangled input scope: PASS without repairs.** `s/r>eta`, so `eta<u<min(1,s/r)`; hence `0<t=eta/u<1` and `c=(s-ur)/(1-u)>0`. The Weyl signal factor is ut=eta and the added photon noise is `u(1-t)r+(1-u)c=(1-eta)b`. The half-photon contribution also agrees. This proves equality of channels, rather than agreement only on Gaussian states.
576: For any target input, EPnI and the energy bound put `x=g^-1(S(T_t,r rho)/n)` in `[r,M]`. Differentiation in input entropy gives `u h(ux+(1-u)c)/h(x)`, bounded below by the displayed positive kappa. Integration proves F2. M is finite and greater than r for E>nr. For an arbitrary finite-energy input in U117 one may take its actual energy or any larger cap. Displacements do not invalidate the energy formula because the bath is centered. The original bath is never freely changed: the physical channel is exactly factored, with rho itself at the first input.
577: ### 2.5 Conditional linear qEPI, including the infinite-dimensional steps
578: **PASS without repairs.** Here are explicit checks of the potential analytical failure points. All marginal and joint quantum entropies involving finitely many finite-energy modes and finite-entropy M are finite, by subadditivity and Araki–Lieb. Conditional entropy can consequently be subtracted without an infinity-minus-infinity convention.
579: For a continuous displacement label Z, use the classical-quantum direct integral. Its information is a relative-entropy integral, not an expression containing the differential entropy of Z. With Z independent of M,
580: `I(X;Z|M)=S(XM)_average-integral S(XM)_z`,
581: since the M marginal of every branch agrees. Gaussian averaging has finite average energy. Each branch has the same joint entropy, while the averaged state has finite entropy. The formula is finite and follows by expanding the relative entropy to the average; unbounded logarithms are obtained by monotone spectral truncations of their positive parts. Equivalently, increasing finite measurable partitions converge to the classical-quantum relative entropy, and the finite conditional entropy expressions converge to the integrals. Conditional independence in a labelled branch is not assumed for a coarse partition: its limiting integral is the quantity that vanishes.
582: The heat increment is thus the stated nonnegative information. Adding independent heat to X is a local channel and diminishes this same fixed-w increment. The midpoint inequality follows by comparing consecutive equal increments; continuity makes midpoint concavity ordinary concavity. At zero, heat is trace-norm continuous even with a separable reference, by strong continuity on finite-rank approximants and trace contraction. Joint entropy lower semicontinuity supplies one direction. For any fixed a>0,
583: `S(X|M)=h(a) e+n log(a+1)-D(rho_XM || tau_a tensor rho_M)`
584: and the exact energy increase nv plus relative-entropy lower semicontinuity supply the other. The reference marginal does not move. No continuity of an unbounded energy functional on the whole energy cap was used; along this heat path its value is known exactly.
585: A finite concave continuous function is locally absolutely continuous on `(0,infinity)`. Since the heat entropy is increasing, its derivative is nonnegative almost everywhere. For `0<epsilon<T`, the fundamental theorem holds on `[epsilon,T]`. Monotone convergence of its derivative integral and continuity at zero extend the identity to `[0,T]`. Linear combinations of these functions therefore admit the improper derivative integration used in I117. This checks the endpoint rather than treating almost-everywhere differentiability alone as sufficient.
586: For v>1, the heterodyne channel representation has characteristic multiplier `exp(-v|w|^2)`: the measurement contributes `exp(-|w|^2/2)` and the prepared `tau_(v-1)` contributes `exp(-(v-1/2)|w|^2)`. In its ancillary form the conditional M states are obtained from the positive operator-valued heterodyne density. Given Z, Y and M are a product. Classical-to-quantum data processing implies
587: `I(Y;M)<=I(Y;Z)=S(Y)-ng(v-1)`.
588: The right-hand side is finite since Y has energy `e+nv`; the equality follows from the finite Holevo integral. Subtracting from S(Y) gives the conditional lower bound `ng(v-1)`, while the upper bound is `ng(v+e/n)`. Their difference from `n log v+n` tends to zero. This proves exactly the needed heat asymptotic, without Gaussian extremality or extra moments.
589: For shared noise, the chain identity is
590: `I(AB;Z|M)=I(A;Z|M)+I(B;Z|M)-I(A;B|M)_average+I(A;B|MZ)`.
591: The last term is zero in the labelled branches. The negative term is evaluated in the averaged state and may be nonzero; it has the favorable sign. Data processing to C gives the finite-increment Stam inequality. Independent heat on A and B preserves their zero conditional mutual information and evolves C by the same heat time. At common differentiability points this gives `H_C'<=t H_A'+(1-t)H_B'`. The weighted difference is therefore nonincreasing, and its limit is zero. Integration back to zero proves the conditional linear qEPI used by doubling. This argument covers arbitrary quantum M with finite entropy and arbitrary internal entanglement in either port.
592: ### 2.6 U117, noisy consequence, and original I114 disposition
593: **Full U117 and its explicit noisy consequence: PASS without repairs.** F117 reduces a zero physical deficit to zero linear deficit for `(rho,tau_r)`. The different-pair doubling identity with `(tau_2,tau_2)` has three nonnegative terms by §2.5, the explicit A-group/B-group conditional independence, and strong subadditivity. Its information-loss term is zero. The Q114 erasure instrument then makes `(id tensor Phi)omega_CE` a product at the stated separable-reference scope.
594: In this step the branch reference marginal is the filtered one. The positive filter has eigenvalues `3^-n 2^-N`; compression, Pinsker, two gentle tails and the final reference-marginal correction are exactly what prove Q114. The branch-zero inverse is bounded, whereas the branch-one inverse is used only on a fixed number cutoff. Finite branch entropies follow from their conditional energy bounds and from entropy concavity on the reference. None of these checks requires a classical memory or finite reference dimension.
595: At equality, Weyl cancellation removes Phi because its multiplier is nonzero and its argument map is onto. For `a=sqrt(t)`, `d=sqrt(1-t)`, output arguments `(x/a,-y/d)` give
596: `chi_rho(x+y)=chi_rho(x)chi_rho(y) exp(-2(r+1/2)Re(x conjugate(y)))`.
597: The function `exp((r+1/2)|x|^2)chi_rho(x)` is a continuous multiplicative character, has no zeros by subdivision, and has modulus one by conjugate symmetry. A local continuous argument is additive; rational approximation and subdivision extend it to a real linear functional on all real phase space. It is exactly a displacement phase. Finite first moments identify that displacement with the mean. Conversely displaced thermals attain equality by channel covariance. This proves the full finite-energy uniqueness, including uncentered inputs, with no Darmois–Skitovich theorem assumed.
598: At nonzero deficit, doubling and F2 give the exact Q114 bound with loss at most `Delta/kappa` and C energy at most `E+nr`. Its constants, infimum over L, and cap at two pass. It controls the noisy **intermediate** equal-temperature output, not the original correlated unequal-temperature output. Injectivity was used only for equality; no quantitative inverse was asserted.
599: **Original I114 no-repair pass retained.** In particular B114's closed squeezing form has gap `2r+1`, both annihilator lists are required, and finite energy puts the vectorized state in their form domains. Its trace-normalization/cutoff constants, centered thermal metric duality and undoing of displacement apply at their original scopes. The source stationarity controls only its constructed minimizers. Q114 and its two scoped counterexamples, symmetry/endpoints, and synthesis S retain the original pass and technical disposition. S already follows from the source's all-L encoders, one-call recursion, commutator refinement, phase-sensitive lookup and clean-isometry-to-diamond comparison with a target-dependent truth table. Nothing in this audit promotes it or the AD/PR/ZK screens to a new finding.
600: **U117 contribution: technical.** Its complete reduction is scalar channel composition and EPnI, followed by reconstructed conditional linear qEPI, Li's exact doubling and instrument operations, and the known-thermal characteristic equation. The pinned EPnI title's failure to mention uniqueness is irrelevant to this assessment. The reduction works at the full arbitrary-input scope; it is not a Gaussian-only reduction. T1–T7 are genuine constraint-respecting technical obstructions. Their original failure conclusions remain intact.
601: ## 3. Route I — bounded Weyl tests, paid frequency tails, and an explicit modulus
602: This route completes a quantitative bridge beyond the original noisy correlation statement. It does not turn Q or ordinary R into a small-deficit quantity.
603: ### 3.1 A general noisy-product-to-input lemma
604: Fix finite n>=1, r>0, `0<t<1`, and a centered input rho with energy at most E. No entropy constraint is needed in this lemma. Let
605: `omega=U_t(rho tensor tau_r)U_t*`, `sigma=(id tensor Phi)omega`, `Phi=T_(1/2),2`,
606: and suppose `||sigma-sigma_C tensor sigma_J||_1<=epsilon`, `0<epsilon<=exp(-4)`. Define explicit constants
607:  R=r+\tfrac12,\quad B=2E+n+R,\quad
608:  A=\frac5{8(1-t)}+\frac{R(1-2t)^2}{4t(1-t)},\quad D=4(A+1),\quad J=8(E+nr)+8n,
609:  H_0^2=JD+\frac{2^{n+3}(n+3)!}{n!D^n}
610:                   \left(\frac BD+\frac12\right)^2,
611:  \qquad H_1=2^{n/2}H_0+2\sqrt E+2\sqrt{nr},
612:  \qquad \gamma=\frac1{2(n+1)}.
613: **Lemma W120 (author-proved technical):**
614:  \|\rho-\tau_r\|_1\le
615:  \min\{2,H_1[\log(1/\epsilon)]^{-\gamma}\}. \tag{W1}
616: There is no finite-support, faithfulness, fourth-moment, Gaussian-input or mode-product assumption.
617: **Bounded-test defect.** Use complex Weyl operators `D(z)` with `chi_tau_r(z)=exp(-R|z|^2)`. In sigma test C at `u=z/(2sqrt(t))` and J at `sqrt(2)v=-z/sqrt(2(1-t))`. A product of Weyl operators has norm one, so its expectation defect is at most epsilon. Removing the **scalar** noise multiplier at these finite test arguments costs `exp(5|v|^2/2)`, not a global channel inverse. The remaining thermal second-port factor in the joint characteristic function is
618: `exp(-R(1-2t)^2 |z|^2/[4t(1-t)])`.
619: The product-marginal thermal factor differs from this by `exp(-R|z|^2/2)`. Therefore, for every z,
620:  |\chi_\rho(z)-\chi_\rho(z/2)^2e^{-R|z|^2/2}|
621:  \le\epsilon e^{A|z|^2}. \tag{W2}
622: All amplification as the test radius grows is displayed in A.
623: **Centering and dyadic propagation.** Write `D(z)=exp(iH_z)`. The annihilator in direction z, divided by |z|, is one normalized mode, whose number expectation is at most E. The elementary vector inequality for its creation and annihilation parts gives `Tr rho H_z^2<=(4E+2n)|z|^2`. Centering makes `Tr rho H_z=0`. Integrating the scalar bound `|exp(ix)-1-ix|<=x^2/2` against its spectral measure gives
624: `|chi_rho(z)-1|<=(2E+n)|z|^2`.
625: Also `|1-exp(-R|z|^2)|<=R|z|^2`. For `d(z)=|chi_rho(z)-exp(-R|z|^2)|`, W2 and `|chi_rho|<=1` yield
626: `d(z)<=2d(z/2)+epsilon exp(A|z|^2)`.
627: After m iterations this is at most `B|z|^2/2^m+(2^m-1)epsilon exp(A|z|^2)`. Choose the integer m with `epsilon^-1/2<=2^m<2epsilon^-1/2`. For every radius Q and every `|z|<=Q`,
628:  d(z)\le(BQ^2+2)e^{AQ^2}\sqrt\epsilon. \tag{W3}
629: Only a second moment and bounded tests enter; no derivative of W2 is taken. At zero epsilon one may instead send m to infinity at fixed z, recovering exact centered uniqueness directly.
630: **Frequency tails at finite energy.** In the convention here Weyl Plancherel is
631: `||X||_2^2=pi^-n integral |chi_X(z)|^2 dz`.
632: For completeness, in the Schrödinger representation the characteristic function at real displacement (q,p) is the ordinary Fourier transform in the midpoint variable of the kernel `K(x-q/2,x+q/2)`. Ordinary Fourier Plancherel and the unit-Jacobian midpoint/difference change of variables give `integral |chi_X(q,p)|^2 dq dp/(2pi)^n=integral |K(x,y)|^2 dx dy`. Since `(q,p)=sqrt(2)(Re z,Im z)` up to a harmless rotation, this is the displayed normalization. Finite-rank Schwartz kernels are dense in Hilbert–Schmidt space, proving the identity for all Hilbert–Schmidt X.
633: For a finite-energy state nu, the four single-ladder products are Hilbert–Schmidt, with `||a_j nu||_2^2<=Tr nu N_j` and `||nu a_j||_2^2<=Tr nu(N_j+1)`. Consequently
634:  \pi^{-n}\int |z|^2|\chi_\nu(z)|^2 dz
635:  =\sum_j\|[a_j,\nu]\|_2^2\le4\operatorname{Tr}\nu N+2n. \tag{W4}
636: To justify the unbounded commutator identity, first take `P_K nu P_K`. Its characteristic function is Schwartz and cyclicity gives `chi_[a_j,nu](z)=-z_j chi_nu(z)`. Its left/right ladder products converge in Hilbert–Schmidt norm: use `a P_K=P_(K-1)a`, `P_K a=a P_(K+1)`, and the Hilbert–Schmidt convergence of two-sided cutoffs on a nu and nu a. Closedness of multiplication by z_j in L2 passes the equality to the limit. This proof never asserts finite fourth moment.
637: Apply W4 to rho and tau_r and use the factor-two square inequality. The frequency tail of their difference outside radius Q is bounded by `J/Q^2`. The ball's normalized volume is `Q^(2n)/n!`. Combining with W3 gives, for every Q>0,
638:  \|\rho-\tau_r\|_2^2
639:  \le\frac{Q^{2n}}{n!}(BQ^2+2)^2\epsilon e^{2AQ^2}
640:           +\frac J{Q^2}. \tag{W5}
641: Let `lambda=log(1/epsilon)>=4`, `Q^2=lambda/D`. Then `epsilon exp(2AQ^2)<=exp(-lambda/2)`, and `BQ^2+2<=(B/D+1/2)lambda`. The elementary series bound `exp(lambda/2)>=(lambda/2)^(n+3)/(n+3)!` gives exactly `||rho-tau_r||_2^2<=H_0^2/lambda`.
642: **Number tails and trace norm.** For every integer K>=0, gentle compression and the energy cap give
643:  \|\rho-\tau_r\|_1\le\sqrt{\binom{K+n}{n}}\,
644:                        \|\rho-\tau_r\|_2
645:        +\frac{2(\sqrt E+\sqrt{nr})}{\sqrt{K+1}}. \tag{W6}
646: Take `K+1=ceil(lambda^(1/(n+1)))`. The rank is at most `(K+1)^n`, and `K+1<=2lambda^(1/(n+1))`. Substitution proves W1 with the displayed H_1. Both growing cutoffs, both tails, the frequency amplification, the dyadic iteration and their constants have now been paid. ∎
647: ### 3.2 The original constraint set: an explicit iterated-logarithmic theorem
648: Use F117's **same input** parameters u,t,c,kappa; use t in W120. Set
649:  K_0=4\,3^n+16\sqrt{(E+nr)\log2},\qquad
650:  z_0=\max\{e^{16},K_0^4\},\qquad \gamma=1/[2(n+1)].
651: **Theorem M120 (author-proved technical).** Under every original hypothesis of B, if `0<Delta/kappa<=exp(-z_0)`, then
652:  \boxed{\quad\|\rho-\tau_r\|_1
653:  \le 4^\gamma H_1\,[\log\log(\kappa/\Delta)]^{-\gamma}.\quad} \tag{M1}
654: At Delta=0 the distance is zero. For other deficits the bound two applies. An often more usable explicit bound is W1 with any upper bound `epsilon<=exp(-4)` obtained from
655:  \epsilon\le\inf_{L\ge0}
656:  \left\{2\,3^n2^L\sqrt{2\Delta/\kappa}
657:               +8\sqrt{(E+nr)/(L+1)}\right\}. \tag{M2}
658: **Proof.** F2 and doubling give M2 by the already audited instrument estimate. For `d=Delta/kappa<1` and `z=log(1/d)`, Q114's explicit choice yields
659: `epsilon<=2sqrt(2)3^n exp(-z/4)+16sqrt((E+nr)log2/z)<=K_0/sqrt(z)`.
660: The last inequality uses `sqrt(z)exp(-z/4)<=sqrt(2)` for z>0. We may use the larger upper bound `epsilon_0=K_0/sqrt(z)` in W120. When `z>=z_0`, `log(1/epsilon_0)>=log(z)/4>=4`. W1 proves M1. At zero use U117 or W2 with arbitrarily small epsilon. No assertion that actual noisy correlation equals its upper bound is needed. ∎
661: The first logarithm is lost in the inverse-filter/energy budget; the second is lost in the bounded-frequency removal of Gaussian noise and in the recurrence. M1 is **not** B with a small exponent. It is an explicit full-scope modulus, and therefore stronger than merely citing compactness, but remains asymptotically weaker than every positive power of Delta.
662: ### 3.3 Additional nonnegative doubling remainder and why it does not close a power
663: The plus-pair remainder was not needed for M1. It gives another actual quantitative restriction. Put `r_+=(r+2)/2`, `A_+=Phi rho`, `B_+=tau_(r_+)`, and `x=g^-1(S(A_+)/n)`. Let
664: `V=max{r_+,(E/2+n)/n}` and `x_min=g^-1([ng(r)+ng(2)]/(2n))>0`.
665: Unconditional linear qEPI gives `x>=x_min`; energy gives `x<=V`, and `x_min<=r_+<=V`. On `[0,V]`, `-g''(x)>=1/[V(V+1)]` (interpreted by a limit at zero). Strong concavity therefore gives
666:  L_t(A_+,B_+)\ge
667:  \frac{nt(1-t)}{2V(V+1)}(x-r_+)^2.
668: This remainder is at most `delta<=Delta/kappa`. Since h decreases,
669:  |S(\Phi\rho)-ng(r_+)|
670:  \le n h(x_{\min})
671:        \sqrt{\frac{2V(V+1)\Delta}{\kappa nt(1-t)}}. \tag{M3}
672: This is a square-root constraint on a smoothed entropy at the same input, derived from an additional nonnegative remainder. It supplies no control of the unsmoothed energy excess: the original rare-tail families keep a positive excess while this smoothed entropy difference tends to zero. We have not proved an inequality converting M3 and the conditional remainder into a polynomial-in-cutoff trace budget. Treating M3 alone as a trace-distance estimate would require a new argument.
673: ## 4. Route II — direct entropy-growth coercivity and a constraint-respecting tail test
674: This route works with the equal-temperature semigroup on the same rho. It does not use bounded inversion of a Gaussian channel. It identifies the exact coercive quantity, pays a short-time trace comparison, and tests a potential no-power construction quantitatively.
675: ### 4.1 An exact positive entropy-growth measure, without unjustified derivatives
676: Let `P_v=T_(exp(-v)),r`, `D_v=D(P_v rho||tau_r)`, and `F(v)=exp(v)D_v`, for any finite-energy rho. Conditional linear qEPI with trivial memory implies, for every finite-energy state sigma and `0<q<1`,
677:  J_q(\sigma):=S(T_{q,r}\sigma)-qS(\sigma)-(1-q)ng(r)
678:              =qD(\sigma\|\tau_r)-D(T_{q,r}\sigma\|\tau_r)\ge0. \tag{D1}
679: The equality is the exact energy identity, so no unequal-temperature baseline was omitted. Thus F is nonincreasing. It is continuous: semigroup trace continuity, its exact continuous energy, and energy-constrained entropy continuity suffice. It defines a nonnegative atomless Stieltjes measure `mu_rho=-dF` on compact time intervals. For `T=-log t`,
680:  J_t(\rho)=e^{-T}\mu_\rho([0,T]). \tag{D2}
681: On the original entropy slice J_t is precisely the transferred entropy deficit delta. More generally, for any partition `0=v_0<...<v_m=T`, telescoping gives the exact positive sum
682:  J_t(\rho)=\sum_{i=1}^m e^{-(T-v_i)}
683:       J_{e^{-(v_i-v_{i-1})}}(P_{v_{i-1}}\rho). \tag{D3}
684: This proves a fully scoped integral remainder without assuming differentiability of entropy for arbitrary finite-energy inputs. On smooth finite-moment faithful trajectories, the familiar density, when justified, is `e^v(I_r-D_v)` where `I_r=-D_v'`. The source's generator pairing is proved at its special regularized states; it is not an automatic proof of that differentiability on our entire state set.
685: Even on states where the production identity is justified, the centered formula is `I_r=h(r)Q^2`. The remainder is **`h(r)Q^2-D_v`**, not `h(r)Q^2`. Discarding the subtraction would repeat a disproved shortcut. The energy cap bounds D_v, but integrating that bound leaves a fixed positive term; it does not make Q small as delta tends to zero.
686: ### 4.2 Paid short-time trace comparison and the missing coercivity
687: For every input of energy at most E,
688:  \|P_v\rho-\rho\|_1
689:  \le2\arccos(e^{-v/2})\sqrt{(2r+1)E+nr}
690:  \le\pi\sqrt{((2r+1)E+nr)v}. \tag{D4}
691: To prove this, purify rho and tau_r, and implement the beam splitter by `exp(-i theta G)`, `theta=arccos(exp(-v/2))`, `G=i sum(a_j* b_j-a_j b_j*)`. Its squared expectation in the product input is
692: `Tr (rho tensor tau_r)G^2=(2r+1)Tr rho N+nr`.
693: Indeed anomalous bath second moments vanish, and the two normal bath moments are r and r+1 times the mode Kronecker delta. This uses only second moments even if rho is internally entangled. The joint purification lies in the form domain of G squared. Spectral calculus gives a vector displacement at most `theta sqrt(<G^2>)`, the pure-state trace bound costs two, and partial trace contracts. Finally `theta<= (pi/2)sin(theta)` and `sin^2(theta)=1-exp(-v)<=v`. This proves D4 on the full energy cap.
694: One sufficient quantitative obligation is now concrete. If for centered finite-energy states sigma with energy at most E one could prove a uniform production remainder
695: `I_r(sigma)-D(sigma||tau_r)>=c ||sigma-tau_r||_1^p`,
696: with a legitimate measure/integrated formulation for nonsmooth states, D2–D4 would yield a power. In fact, put `d=||rho-tau_r||_1`, `C_E=pi sqrt((2r+1)E+nr)`, and `h=min(T,d^2/(4C_E^2))`. Centering and the energy cap persist under P_v. D4 gives distance at least d/2 on `[0,h]`. The proposed measure bound would imply
697: `delta>=exp(-T)c h(d/2)^p`,
698: and hence a bound with exponent `1/(p+2)`, with constants depending only on the fixed parameters. This is a **conditional reduction with a precisely unpaid premise**, not a theorem claiming that production coercivity holds. It does not demand vanishing Q or R, nor a strict multiplicative improvement `D_out<ct D_in`.
699: A naive convexity route to that premise fails. J_q is nonnegative and vanishes on every displaced tau_r. For opposite nonzero displacements, their midpoint is centered and not a thermal state: its characteristic function has a nonconstant cosine factor. The equality mechanism in §2.6 applies to any zero J_q with the known bath, without an entropy-slice assumption, and forces a displaced tau_r. Thus this midpoint has strictly positive J_q although both endpoints have zero J_q. J_q is not convex on the state space. A globally nonnegative Hessian difference cannot be assumed.
700: On a finite-number block with spectral floor m, the exact entropy Hessian difference along a traceless A is `q B_sigma(A,A)-B_Tsigma(TA,TA)`. Data processing gives only `B_Tsigma<=B_sigma`, which does not give a sign after multiplying the input by q<1. A thermal-line Taylor argument must retain the negative portions outside a small neighborhood. The original cutoffs make `m=K^-2/binom(K+n,n)`; local derivative costs at least `1/m=K^2 binom(K+n,n)`, and logarithmic gradients can cost `sqrt(binomial(K+n,n))|log m|`. There is no cutoff-independent spectral neighborhood. These are the actual small-eigenvalue and integration obligations, not a missing appeal to compactness.
701: ### 4.3 A different exact-constraint no-power test: separated displaced thermal tails
702: Choose `0<c<E-nr`. For `0<p<1/2`, let a_p be the real first-mode displacement with
703: `|a_p|^2=c/[p(1-p)]`.
704: For x in `[r/2,r]` define
705:  \rho(p,x)=(1-p)D(-pa_p)\tau_xD(-pa_p)^*
706:        +pD((1-p)a_p)\tau_xD((1-p)a_p)^*. \tag{D5}
707: Its mean is exactly zero and its energy is exactly `nx+c`. It is faithful and is not restricted to finite Fock support. Write `chi_in(p,x)` for the Holevo information of this binary ensemble, so
708: `S(rho(p,x))=ng(x)+chi_in(p,x)`.
709: The means of its heterodyne outcomes differ by a_p; the variance of the first real coordinate is `(x+1)/2`. A threshold at the midpoint of these two means has each conditional error at most `exp(-|a_p|^2/[4(x+1)])`, by the exponential Markov bound for a one-dimensional Gaussian. For small p, this is less than one half uniformly in x. Binary Fano and data processing therefore give
710:  h_2(p)-h_2\!\left(e^{-c/[4(r+1)p(1-p)]}\right)
711:        \le\chi_{\rm in}(p,x)\le h_2(p). \tag{D6}
712: The identical calculation after the **original physical channel** has component temperature `s_x=eta x+(1-eta)b` and mean separation `sqrt(eta)a_p`. Hence its Holevo information satisfies
713:  h_2(p)-h_2\!\left(e^{-\eta c/[4(s+1)p(1-p)]}\right)
714:        \le\chi_{\rm out}(p,x)\le h_2(p). \tag{D7}
715: For fixed p the entropy is continuous in x by the common finite-energy bound. At x=r the Holevo information is strictly positive: otherwise both distinct displaced components equal their mixture by relative-entropy positivity. At x=r/2, the upper bound `ng(r/2)+h_2(p)` is below H for small p. There is therefore an actual `x_p in (r/2,r)` with **exact** entropy H. The resulting rho_p obeys every Target B hypothesis and has energy `<nr+c<E`.
716: Let `h_p=h_2(p)`. The error terms in D6–D7 are `o(h_p^m)` for every fixed positive m, since `h_2(exp(-a/p))=O(p^-1 exp(-a/p))` for a>0. Taylor's theorem on `[r/2,r]`, using `|g''|<=1/[(r/2)(r/2+1)]`, gives
717:  r-x_p=\frac{h_p}{n h(r)}+O(h_p^2),\qquad
718:  \Delta_p=(1-c_*)h_p+O(h_p^2),\quad
719:  c_*=\frac{\eta h(s)}{h(r)}<1. \tag{D8}
720: All constants here depend only on the fixed n,r,b,eta,c. The second Taylor remainder is bounded on `[eta r/2+(1-eta)b,s]`, whose left endpoint is positive. Thus D8 is a two-sided asymptotic, not merely an upper bound. In particular `Delta_p` is bounded above and below by positive fixed multiples of `p log(1/p)` eventually.
721: The trace distance has order sqrt(p). For the upper bound, changing the rare component costs at most 2p. Moving the main thermal by `beta=-pa_p` costs at most `2sqrt(2r+1)|beta|`. Also the thermal family is trace-Lipschitz on `[r/2,r]`: differentiation of its diagonal probabilities gives
722: `||d tau_x/dx||_1=E_tau_x |N-nx|/[x(x+1)]<=2n/(x+1)<=2n`.
723: Integration and D8 prove `||rho_p-tau_r||_1=O(sqrt(p))`. For a lower bound, test the first-mode Weyl operator at z=i. Its thermal characteristic function is real, whereas
724: `Im chi_rho_p(i)=exp(-(x_p+1/2))[-(1-p)sin(2p|a_p|)+p sin(2(1-p)|a_p|)]`.
725: The second summand has magnitude at most p and the first is `-2sqrt(cp)+O(p^(3/2))`. Since `x_p->r`, the absolute expectation difference is at least a positive fixed multiple of sqrt(p) for small p. The operator norm is one, so
726:  \|\rho_p-\tau_r\|_1=\Theta(\sqrt p),\qquad
727:  \Delta_p=\Theta(p\log(1/p)). \tag{D9}
728: This fully paid centering/entropy/energy construction is a **failed no-power candidate**. For `alpha<1/2`, the ratio `||rho_p-tau_r||_1/Delta_p^alpha` tends to zero; it also tends to zero at alpha=1/2. It only duplicates the already known exclusion of alpha>1/2. Making the displaced rare component travel to distance of order p^-1/2 does not make its binary mixing entropy disappear. The positive coefficient `1-c_*` in D8 is the exact unequal-temperature baseline subtraction that the proposed counterexample must overcome. No conclusion about all other possible sequences follows from this calculation.
729: ## 5. Contribution assessment and terminal quantitative obligation
730: All original I117 audited claims pass without repairs. U117 remains a full-scope technical consequence of the accepted and reconstructed operations, and original I114 remains proof-passed and technical. The new M120 conclusion controls the **original input's trace distance** under every original hypothesis. Its proof uses the original instrument bound, a known-thermal specialization of the characteristic functional equation, elementary dyadic iteration, Hilbert–Schmidt Fourier tails, and a number cutoff. This is more than tuning a Q114 cutoff constant: it supplies the previously missing finite-energy quantitative reversal to the actual input. Nevertheless, the full mechanism is a concrete combination of the existing doubling/instrument and phase-space stability operations with a fixed known Gaussian factor. It is assessed **technical**, not nominated as a substantial finding merely because its exact formula is absent from a title.
731: Cuesta's phase-space split and Parseval tail operation are credited at their actual scope. We have not imported its fourth-moment local estimates. Here the second input is known thermal and centering fixes the remaining character; these special facts permit W2–W3. W4 supplies a direct domain-safe version of a finite-energy frequency tail. W6 is the same elementary energy/rank mechanism as B114. None of these facts supplies a positive power of the entropy deficit.
732: Route II gives a different exact entropy-growth formulation and D4, and rejects an actual centered fixed-entropy fixed-energy displaced-tail proposal by its two-sided deficit asymptotic. Its remaining obligation is a trace-coercive remainder for D2/D3 (or an equivalent integrated statement) with constants uniform on the finite-energy centered set. Source generator identities only at constructed Gibbs minimizers do not pay it. In Route I the corresponding obligation is a polynomial-in-cutoff bounded-test/doubling estimate whose errors remain absorbable when both frequency and number cutoffs grow; exponential Gaussian test amplification and `2^L` inverse-filter growth currently prevent that conclusion.
733: Target B, with some `0<alpha_n<=1/2`, is **UNPROVED AND UNREFUTED in this investigation**. No sequence defeating every positive exponent was produced. No balance-defect shortcut, injectivity-to-bounded-inverse inference, fourth-moment assumption, finite-support substitution, Gaussian-input substitution, or compactness-as-rate claim enters the positive theorem. Eight accepted findings remain eight.

794: ## 2. Independent audit of the ORIGINAL new I120 claims
795: ### 2.1 W120: bounded tests and domains
796: **W120, including W1–W6: PASS without repairs.** The following are checks of the original argument, not replacements of a failed premise.
797: Write `R=r+1/2`, `B0=2E+n+R` (the original B), and
798: `A=5/[8(1-t)]+R(1-2t)^2/[4t(1-t)]`, `D=4(A+1)`, `J=8(E+nr)+8n`.
799: For `omega=U_t(rho tensor tau_r)U_t*`, `sigma=(id tensor Phi)omega`, `Phi=T_(1/2),2`, test C at `u_z=z/(2 sqrt(t))` and J at `sqrt(2)v_z`, where `v_z=-z/(2 sqrt(1-t))`. The joint signal argument is z. The auxiliary channel multiplies by `exp[-(5/2)|v_z|^2]`. The joint thermal exponent is `R(1-2t)^2|z|^2/[4t(1-t)]`; subtracting it from the product-marginal exponent gives `R|z|^2/2`. A product of Weyl operators has norm one, so noisy correlation at most epsilon gives exactly
800: `|chi_rho(z)-chi_rho(z/2)^2 exp(-R|z|^2/2)|<=epsilon exp(A|z|^2)`.
801: Only a nonzero scalar at a specified finite test argument was divided out. There is no bounded inverse channel in this step.
802: For `D(z)=exp(iH_z)`, the normalized annihilation direction is a single mode with number expectation at most E. On its form domain the triangle/Cauchy–Schwarz bound gives `Tr rho H_z^2<=(4E+2)|z|^2<=(4E+2n)|z|^2`. Finite energy ensures finite first moments, and centering removes the linear term. Spectral integration of `|e^{ix}-1-ix|<=x^2/2` therefore gives the original quadratic near-zero bound. With `d(z)=|chi_rho(z)-exp(-R|z|^2)|`, modulus at most one gives
803: After m steps the initial error is at most `B0|z|^2/2^m` and the sum at most `(2^m-1)epsilon exp(A|z|^2)`. For `epsilon^-1/2<=2^m<2epsilon^-1/2`, on `|z|<=Q` this is at most `(B0 Q^2+2) exp(AQ^2)sqrt(epsilon)`. This iteration requires no differentiation of the approximate equation and no fourth moment.
804: Here is the full unbounded-operator check. For a density nu of finite energy, `a_j sqrt(nu)` and `a_j* sqrt(nu)` are Hilbert–Schmidt, with squared norms `e_j` and `e_j+1`. Their products with the bounded sqrt(nu), and their adjoints, define the four closed left/right ladder products of nu; they are even trace class, hence Hilbert–Schmidt. In the eigenbasis of nu, or by the positive quadratic-form inequality `nu^2<=nu`,
805: `||a_j nu||_2^2<=e_j`, `||nu a_j||_2^2<=e_j+1`.
806: This construction defines the closure of `nu a_j` on Dom(a_j), not an everywhere-defined unbounded cyclic product. For the total-number projection P_K,
807: `a_j P_K nu P_K=P_(K-1)(a_j nu)P_K`,
808: `P_K nu P_K a_j=P_K(nu a_j)P_(K+1)`.
809: These converge in Hilbert–Schmidt norm to the indicated products, because strongly convergent orthogonal projections converge on both sides of any Hilbert–Schmidt operator. The adjoint formulas cover the other products. Finite Fock matrices have Schwartz characteristic functions; cyclicity on their finite ladder extensions gives `chi_[a_j,nu_K]=-z_j chi_nu_K`. Weyl Plancherel and closedness of multiplication by z_j in L2 pass this identity to the limit. Thus
810: `pi^-n integral |z|^2 |chi_nu(z)|^2 dz=sum_j ||[a_j,nu]||_2^2<=4 Tr nu N+2n`.
811: The stated Plancherel normalization follows by the midpoint/difference kernel transformation in the Schrödinger representation and ordinary Fourier Plancherel with measure `dq dp/(2pi)^n`; `dq dp=2^n d(Re z)d(Im z)`. Density of finite-rank Schwartz kernels extends it to all Hilbert–Schmidt operators. Applied to rho−tau_r, the factor-two square inequality gives the original frequency tail `J/Q^2`. No ladder product is passed using trace-norm convergence alone.
812: The normalized frequency-ball volume is `Q^(2n)/n!`, so the original W5 is
813: `||rho-tau_r||_2^2 <= Q^(2n)(B0 Q^2+2)^2 epsilon exp(2AQ^2)/n! + J/Q^2`.
814: Let `lambda=log(1/epsilon)>=4`, `Q^2=lambda/D`. Then `2A/D<=1/2`, `B0 Q^2+2<=(B0/D+1/2)lambda`, and the `(n+3)`rd positive exponential-series term bounds the first summand by
815: `[2^(n+3)(n+3)!/(n!D^n)](B0/D+1/2)^2/lambda`.
816: Consequently the original
817: `H0^2=JD+[2^(n+3)(n+3)!/(n!D^n)](B0/D+1/2)^2`
818: is sufficient. For the separate number cutoff K, gentle compression of each positive state costs at most `2sqrt(Tr nu(1-P_K))`; `N>=K+1` on the complement. Cauchy–Schwarz on the compressed difference therefore gives exactly
819: `||rho-tau_r||_1<=sqrt(binomial(K+n,n))||rho-tau_r||_2+2(sqrt E+sqrt(nr))/sqrt(K+1)`.
820: The rank is at most `(K+1)^n`, since nonnegative n-tuples of sum at most K lie in the corresponding cube. Taking `K+1=ceil(lambda^(1/(n+1)))` yields W1 with
821: `gamma=1/[2(n+1)]`, `H1=2^(n/2)H0+2sqrt E+2sqrt(nr)`.
822: Both growing cutoffs are paid, as are their dimensions and tails. This proves the full centered finite-energy statement for n>=1, without an entropy constraint, Gaussian input, faithfulness, finite support, or mode-product assumption. E=0 causes no domain problem; the state is vacuum. The cap two is the universal trace bound. At epsilon=0, the dyadic argument at fixed z directly gives the centered thermal state.
823: ### 2.2 M120 and the extra smoothed entropy remainder
824: **M120, its full original constraint scope and thresholds: PASS without repairs.** The exact same-input factorization above permits use of W120 with the actual rho. The inherited instrument estimate gives, for integer L>=0,
825: `epsilon <= 2*3^n*2^L sqrt(2Delta/kappa)+8sqrt((E+nr)/(L+1))`.
826: The audited explicit choice of L implies, for `z=log(kappa/Delta)>0`,
827: `epsilon<=2sqrt(2)3^n exp(-z/4)+16sqrt((E+nr)log2/z)<=K0/sqrt z`,
828: where `K0=4*3^n+16sqrt((E+nr)log2)`. The last bound uses the global maximum `sqrt z exp(-z/4)<=sqrt2`. With `z0=max(e^16,K0^4)` and `z>=z0`, the upper bound epsilon_0 obeys `log(1/epsilon_0)>=log z/4>=4`. Applying W1 with that upper bound, rather than equating it to the actual correlation, proves the original `4^gamma H1[log log(kappa/Delta)]^-gamma`. Delta=0 uses U117 (or the zero-error equation); larger deficits use two. Both logarithms are real losses, not a small positive power.
829: **M3: PASS without repairs.** For `r_+=(r+2)/2`, `A_+=Phi rho`, `B_+=tau_(r_+)`, let `x=g^-1(S(A_+)/n)`, `V=max(r_+,E/(2n)+1)`, and `x_min=g^-1((g(r)+g(2))/2)>0`. Linear qEPI and energy give `x_min<=x,r_+<=V`. Applying EPnI to the plus pair and subtracting its linear entropy baseline leaves the Jensen gap of g. Since `-g''>=1/[V(V+1)]` between x and r_+, this gap is at least `nt(1-t)(x-r_+)^2/[2V(V+1)]`. The plus remainder is at most delta<=Delta/kappa. The derivative bound `h<=h(x_min)` on that interval proves exactly M3. None of these inequalities uses a product structure within a port. It is a restriction on a smoothed entropy, not an energy or unsmoothed trace estimate.
830: ### 2.3 D1–D4, the conditional implication, and nonconvexity
831: **D1, D2, D3 and continuity: PASS without repairs.** For any finite-energy sigma, all entropies and relative entropies to a positive-temperature thermal state are finite. The channel energy identity gives
832: `J_q(sigma)=S(T_q,r sigma)-qS(sigma)-(1-q)H=qD(sigma||tau_r)-D(T_q,r sigma||tau_r)>=0`.
833: The inequality is the inherited quantum linear qEPI. Thus `F(v)=e^v D(P_vrho||tau_r)`, `P_v=T_(e^-v),r`, is nonincreasing. Semigroup trace continuity and the exact continuous energy `nr+e^-v(e-nr)`, together with finite-energy entropy continuity, prove continuity of F, including v=0. Its negative Stieltjes differential defines a nonnegative atomless measure on compact intervals; the endpoint convention assigns no atom at zero. With `T=-log t`, `J_t=e^-T mu([0,T])`. On any finite partition, direct telescoping proves D3 with weights `e^{-(T-v_i)}`. This is valid without differentiability or absolute continuity of F. When a production density exists it is `e^v(I_r-D_v)`. The subtraction is indispensable.
834: On a centered regular state where the generator pairing is justified, the normalization in I120 gives `I_r=h(r)Q²`; consequently the remainder is `h(r)Q²-D_v`. Bounding D_v by a fixed energy constant and integrating leaves a fixed term, not a quantity vanishing with delta. The original refusal to discard D_v passes.
835: **D4, including arbitrary internal entanglement: PASS without repairs.** The generator `G=i sum_j(a_j* b_j-a_j b_j*)` is the self-adjoint direct sum of finite matrices on fixed total-number sectors of the two ports. For a product of arbitrary finite-energy rho and tau_r, normal bath contractions are `Tr tau_r b_j* b_k=r delta_jk` and `Tr tau_r b_j b_k*=(r+1)delta_jk`; anomalous contractions vanish. They kill all cross-mode and anomalous input terms, irrespective of internal entanglement. Finite truncations and the squared norm of G applied to a purification give
836: `<G^2>=(2r+1)e+nr`.
837: This finite value places the purification in Dom(G), the form domain of G². It does not require Dom(G²) or a fourth moment. Spectral calculus bounds its unitary displacement by `theta sqrt(<G²>)`, pure-state trace distance by twice that, and partial trace contracts. With `theta=arccos(e^-v/2)`, `theta<=(pi/2)sin theta` and `sin²theta<=v`, the original bound follows with `C_E=pi sqrt((2r+1)E+nr)`.
838: **Conditional production implication: PASS without repairs as a conditional statement; proposed coercivity itself unresolved.** Its legitimate measure premise can be stated as
839: `dmu_rho(v)>=c e^v ||P_vrho-tau_r||_1^p dv`
840: on `[0,T]`, uniformly on the centered energy cap. Mean evolves as `e^-v/2` times the input mean, and energy remains at most E because E>nr. If `d=||rho-tau_r||_1`, D4 implies distance at least d/2 until `h0=min(T,d²/(4C_E²))`. Since d<=2,
841: `delta>=e^-T c h0(d/2)^p >= e^-T c 2^(-p-2) min(T,C_E^-2) d^(p+2)`.
842: Thus, **if the premise is proved**, Target B follows with exponent `1/(p+2)` and constant
843: `[2^(p+2)e^T/(kappa c min(T,C_E^-2))]^(1/(p+2))`.
844: I120 did not claim the premise or a uniform production domain; it correctly identified the unpaid obligation. Source generator pairings at its regular minimizers cannot be applied to every state here.
845: **Nonconvexity and Hessian diagnosis: PASS without repairs.** J_q vanishes on displaced tau_r. The equal mixture of opposite nonzero displacements is centered and has a cosine factor in its characteristic function, hence is not a displaced thermal. The same equality mechanism used by U117 applies to zero J_q at arbitrary finite entropy, without fixing the input entropy slice, and forces a displaced thermal. The midpoint therefore has J_q>0, contradicting convexity. This argument is on the full state space, not a claim that the entropy slice is convex. The Hessian is `q B_sigma(A,A)-B_Tsigma(TA,TA)`; data processing only bounds its second term by `B_sigma`, not `q B_sigma`. The original finite-block spectral-floor and gradient costs are correctly stated as local costs, not a global sign theorem.
846: ### 2.4 D5–D9: actual constraints and asymptotics
847: **D5, D6, D7: PASS without repairs.** Fix `0<c0<E-nr`, `a_p²=c0/[p(1-p)]` in the first real mode, and mix displaced tau_x with weights 1−p,p and displacements −p a_p,(1−p)a_p. The weighted mean is exactly zero and the displacement-energy contribution exactly c0. The state is faithful, with energy `nx+c0`. Its entropy is `ng(x)+chi_in`, an exact finite binary Holevo formula.
848: For heterodyne measurement, the first real-coordinate variance is `(x+1)/2`; the midpoint threshold, although not necessarily the optimal decision rule for unequal priors, has both conditional errors at most `exp[-a_p²/(4(x+1))]`. Thus its total error has the same upper bound. For small p it is below 1/2, so monotonicity of binary entropy, binary Fano and classical measurement data processing give D6 uniformly for x in `[r/2,r]`. Under the actual physical channel the component temperature is `s_x=eta x+(1-eta)b`, separation `sqrt(eta)a_p`; the same argument gives D7 with exponent `eta c0/[4(s+1)p(1-p)]`. This is a quantum Holevo lower bound obtained from a specified measurement, not a classical replacement of quantum entropy.
849: **Exact entropy repair and D8: PASS without repairs.** For each fixed p, entropy is continuous in x under a common finite-energy bound. At r the Holevo information is strictly positive, since its vanishing forces both distinct displaced components equal to their mixture. At r/2, the binary entropy upper bound makes the entropy strictly below H for small p. The intermediate-value root x_p is consequently actual and lies in `(r/2,r)`. It preserves centering, faithfulness, exact entropy H, and energy `<nr+c0<E`.
850: Put `h_p=h_2(p)`. Both discrimination errors contribute `O(p^-1 exp(-a/p))=o(h_p^m)` for every fixed m>0 and fixed a>0. On `[r/2,r]`, `|g''|<=1/[(r/2)(r/2+1)]`, so the input entropy identity first gives `r-x_p=O(h_p)` and then
851: `r-x_p=h_p/[nh(r)]+O(h_p²)`.
852: The output Taylor interval has positive left endpoint `eta r/2+(1-eta)b`, so its second derivative is uniformly bounded too. Hence
853: `Delta_p=(1-c_*)h_p+O(h_p²)`, `c_*=eta h(s)/h(r) in (0,1)`.
854: Strictness follows, for example, from `s>eta r` and the strict inequality `eta log(1+1/(eta r))<log(1+1/r)`. Constants depend only on the fixed parameters and c0. This is genuinely two-sided: eventually Delta_p lies between positive multiples of p log(1/p).
855: **D9 and its failed no-power conclusion: PASS without repairs.** Replacing the rare component costs at most 2p. Moving the main component costs `2sqrt(2r+1)|p a_p|`. The thermal derivative has trace norm
856: `E_tau_x |N-nx|/[x(x+1)]<=2n/(x+1)<=2n`,
857: so temperature repair adds O(h_p). The upper trace bound is O(sqrt p). For a lower bound, the imaginary part of the z=i first-mode Weyl expectation has main term `-2sqrt(c0 p)+O(p^(3/2))`, rare term of magnitude at most p, and positive prefactor tending to `exp[-(r+1/2)]`. A norm-one test therefore gives a fixed positive multiple of sqrt p. Thus trace is Theta(sqrt p), deficit Theta(p log(1/p)); the ratio to Delta^alpha tends to zero for every alpha<=1/2 and diverges for alpha>1/2. This family is not a counterexample to Target B.
858: ## 3. Attempt A — resolve the production measure before seeking coercivity
859: This attempt combines the same-input EPnI semigroup with the exact Stieltjes remainder. It obtains a fully nonsmooth decomposition, and tests a commonly tempting regularization step on an actual admissible state. It does not prove the trace-coercive inequality.
860: ### 3.1 A positive decomposition requiring no entropy derivative
861: For the original rho define `x(v)=g^-1(S(P_vrho)/n)` and `V=E/n>r`. Energy and EPnI imply `r<=x(v)<=V`. Applying EPnI between v and v+h gives
862: `x(v+h)>=r+e^-h(x(v)-r)`.
863: Consequently `Y(v)=e^v(x(v)-r)` is continuous and nondecreasing, with Y(0)=0. Continuity uses the same energy/entropy argument as D1. On any compact interval it is bounded and of bounded variation. Let `nu=dY`, a positive atomless Stieltjes measure. The identity `x=r+e^-v Y` implies, as measures,
864: `dx=(r-x)dv+e^-v dnu`.
865: Set `f(v)=n[g(x(v))-g(r)]`. The exact thermal relative-entropy formula gives
866: `F(v)=h(r)(e-nr)-e^v f(v)`, so `mu=d(e^v f)`.
867: The chain rule for a continuous bounded-variation function and a C1 function on `[r,V]` has no jump correction. It follows either by Riemann–Stieltjes sums and uniform continuity of g', or the usual finite-measure chain rule. The product rule therefore yields the exact decomposition
868: `dmu(v)=n h(x(v)) dnu(v)+e^v k_r(x(v)) dv`,
869: `k_r(x)=n[g(x)-g(r)+(r-x)h(x)]`.
870: Both terms are nonnegative. Indeed `k_r(r)=0` and `k_r'(x)=n(x-r)/[x(x+1)]`, giving the explicit bounds
871: `n(x-r)²/[2V(V+1)] <= k_r(x) <= n(x-r)²/[2r(r+1)]`.
872: Thus, for the same fixed T,
873: `delta=e^-T {n integral_[0,T] h(x(v)) dnu(v)+integral_0^T e^v k_r(x(v))dv}`.  (A1)
874: This is a universal technical consequence on the original finite-energy slice. It retains the entire `I_r-D` measure, including any singular continuous part, and never posits a production density. Constants `h(V)<=h(x)<=h(r)` and the displayed quadratic bounds are uniform in rho. It also yields `0<=f(v)=e^-v mu([0,v])<=e^{T-v}delta` for `0<=v<=T`.
875: The second summand of A1 sees only the entropy photon number. At v=0 it vanishes for every admissible rho, even nonthermal ones. The first summand is the residual photon-number growth measure; replacing it by a coercive norm bound is precisely the difficult quantum inequality, not an algebraic consequence of A1. Neither Pinsker applied to `D_v=h(r)e^-v(e-nr)-f(v)` nor the quadratic lower bound on k_r controls the input trace: the energy excess is not small on this slice. No sign in A1 removes that term.
876: ### 3.2 An admissible obstruction to moment-based production smoothing
877: Positive OU time cannot be used to assert finite fourth quadrature moments for every state in this problem. Here is an exact-constraint example, distinct from the displaced-tail sequence.
878: Let J be a positive-integer variable with probabilities `C j^-3`, `C=1/sum_(j>=1) j^-3`. It has finite mean and entropy, and infinite second moment. Let U be independent uniform on `{0,...,m-1}` and put K=mJ+U. The map `(J,U)->K` is injective. Choose a fixed integer m large enough that `S(J)+log m>H`. Let zeta be the first-mode diagonal mixture of |K><K|, with all other modes vacuum. Then `S(zeta)>H`, `e_zeta<infinity`, and `Tr zeta N_1²=infinity`.
879: For small fixed epsilon>0, consider `(1-epsilon)tau_x+epsilon zeta`, x in `[r/2,r]`. Concavity makes its entropy at r strictly larger than H. The mixture upper bound makes its entropy at r/2 smaller than H once
880: `epsilon S(zeta)+h_2(epsilon)<H-ng(r/2)`.
881: Also choose `epsilon e_zeta<E-nr`. Finite-energy entropy continuity gives an exact root x in `(r/2,r)`. The resulting rho is centered, faithful, has entropy exactly H and energy at most `nr+epsilon e_zeta<E`, but infinite second number moment. It is admissible for the original arbitrary-input theorem; admissibility does not require every example itself to be entangled.
882: For a diagonal first-mode input and independent thermal bath, the normally ordered output factorial second moment is
883: `q² E[N_1(N_1-1)]+4q(1-q)r E[N_1]+2(1-q)²r²`.
884: To interpret the infinite term, first use an individual Fock input and expand the beam-splitter polynomial; bath anomalous contractions vanish and all three remaining quantities are nonnegative. Then average over its number distribution using monotone convergence of nonnegative sums. At every q>0 the output factorial moment is infinite. Thus every finite positive OU time retains the infinite second number moment. This blocks a proof that inserts the source's high-moment generator domain merely by evolving for a short time. It does **not** show that entropy production itself is undefined, refute measure coercivity, or defeat any power of Delta.
885: ### 3.3 What remains unpaid
886: A1 is not a quantitative stability theorem. To complete this route one must prove, for the actual quantum semigroup and centered finite-energy states, a lower bound on its measure by a positive trace power, or another integrated estimate strong enough to combine with D4. Ordinary log-Sobolev/entropy contraction only establishes the nonnegativity already used. A finite-block Hessian argument must control its negative terms; it cannot invoke convexity. Using the original smoothing floor `m_K=K^-2/binomial(K+n,n)` brings derivative cost `m_K^-1`, while gentle/entropy errors are of order `K^-1/2 log K`. No absorbable estimate follows from multiplying those budgets. These are scoped method failures, not a proof that a different coercive remainder cannot exist.
887: ## 4. Attempt B — vary the erasure instrument and use all doubling remainders
888: The fixed bath in Target B remains tau_b. Here only the auxiliary pair in the same-input doubling proof varies. This is a concrete attempt to make its inverse filter polynomial while retaining the extra nonnegative terms; it exposes a cost tradeoff rather than a new no-power state.
889: ### 4.1 Exact variable-bath instrument
890: Choose any auxiliary temperature `B_aux=2y+1>1`, y>0. Balanced mixing defines `Phi_B=T_(1/2),B_aux`. Its heterodyne measure-and-prepare representation prepares `D(z/sqrt2)tau_y D(z/sqrt2)*`. The vacuum inverse quadratic form of this prepared thermal is
891: `(y+1)^n exp(|z|²/(2y))`.
892: Indeed in one mode `<alpha|tau_y^-1|alpha>=(y+1) exp(|alpha|²/y)`; tensoring and `alpha=z/sqrt2` give the formula. Hence one can subtract the positive vacuum component with coefficient `(y+1)^-n exp[-|z|²/(2y)]`. The associated heterodyne effect is
893: `G_y=g_y q_y^N`, `q_y=2y/(2y+1)`,
894: `g_y=[(y+1)(1+1/(2y))]^-n`.
895: To check the integral, in each number basis element the coherent-state radial integral with the extra Gaussian factor a=1/(2y) equals `(1+a)^(-k-1)`; tensoring supplies the stated factor and no omitted half photon. The complementary measure-and-prepare map is completely positive. Also `0<G_y<I`, so the branch-zero inverse is bounded; the branch-one inverse is bounded only on P_L. Its squared cutoff norm is
896: `C_(y,L)=g_y^-1 q_y^-L=[(y+1)(1+1/(2y))]^n(1+1/(2y))^L`.  (B1)
897: At y=1/2, this recovers precisely `3^n 2^L`.
898: Apply the different-pair doubling identity to `(rho,tau_r)` and `(tau_Baux,tau_Baux)`. The auxiliary linear deficit is zero. Write its exact three nonnegative summands as `P_B+C_B+ell_B=delta`: plus-pair deficit, conditional minus-pair deficit given the plus systems, and information loss. Nonnegativity uses the quantum conditional linear qEPI and strong subadditivity, with the independent A/B port groups, not conditional EPnI. All systems have finite energy, and the finite-entropy memory and instrument branches have finite conditional energies. The branch reference marginal must be the filtered one. Repeating Q114's Pinsker, inverse filter, two gentle tails, and marginal correction at precisely this reference gives, with `F=E+nr`,
899: `epsilon_B=||(id tensor Phi_B)omega - omega_C tensor Phi_B(omega_E)||_1`
900: `<=2 C_(y,L) sqrt(2 ell_B)+8sqrt(F/(L+1))`.  (B2)
901: This follows from the same proved operations, not an external quantitative recovery theorem. The exact C marginal energy is `t e+(1-t)nr<=F`; replacing it by F is a uniform upper bound.
902: ### 4.2 All growing cutoffs and the tradeoff
903: The same Weyl test now loses
904: `A_y=(2y+3/2)/[4(1-t)]+R(1-2t)^2/[4t(1-t)]`.
905: The second term comes from the original thermal port and is unchanged. For any valid upper bound `0<epsilon_B<=e^-4`, the dyadic estimate and both W120 cutoffs give, without optimizing away a constant,
906: `d <= sqrt(binomial(K+n,n)) { Q^(2n)(B0 Q²+2)² epsilon_B exp(2A_y Q²)/n! + J/Q² }^(1/2)`
907: `     +2(sqrt E+sqrt(nr))/sqrt(K+1)`,  (B3)
908: where `d=||rho-tau_r||_1`, `J=8(E+nr)+8n`, `B0=2E+n+R`, K>=0, Q>0, and the separate filter cutoff L>=0 appears in B2. Thus the filter cutoff, frequency cutoff and number cutoff are all visible. If the upper bound in B2 exceeds e^-4, this formula supplies no small-deficit conclusion via W120; one may only use the cap two.
909: Taking y comparable to L makes B1 polynomial in L, but increases A_y linearly in L. This cannot be ignored. More generally, the coefficient of `ell_B^(1/4)` in the characteristic-function bound contains the exponential
910: `exp{ A_y Q² + (L/2)log(1+1/(2y)) }`.
911: For y>=1/2, `log(1+1/(2y))>=1/(2y+1)>=1/(4y)`. Therefore its exponent is at least
912: `y Q²/[2(1-t)] + L/(8y) >= Q sqrt L/[2 sqrt(1-t)]`.  (B4)
913: For 0<y<1/2 it is at least `(L/2)log2`. The remaining prefactor in B1 is positive and does not cancel this loss. To make the explicit error terms in B2 and B3 polynomially small in a desired trace tolerance, L and K must grow polynomially, and `sqrt(rank P_K)/Q` must also be small; Q must grow. B4 prevents this particular composition of bounds from acquiring a polynomial signal budget by optimizing y. In addition the filter tail is amplified when inserted into B3. This is a lower bound on a cost in a derived upper bound, **not** a lower bound on the actual deficit or a universal impossibility theorem for all bounded tests.
914: ### 4.3 The additional remainders do not yet cancel that cost
915: Let `r_B=(r+B_aux)/2`, `V_B=E/(2n)+B_aux/2`, and `x_B=g^-1(S(Phi_Brho)/n)`. Original EPnI actually gives `r_B<=x_B<=V_B` on this entropy slice. Exactly as for M3,
916: `P_B>=nt(1-t)(x_B-r_B)²/[2V_B(V_B+1)]`.
917: Thus, writing `z_B=S(Phi_Brho)-ng(r_B)>=0`,
918: `z_B<=n h(r_B) sqrt[2V_B(V_B+1)P_B/(nt(1-t))]`.  (B5)
919: This uses the real plus remainder, rather than discarding it. But the exact relative-entropy identity is
920: `D(Phi_Brho||tau_rB)=h(r_B)(e-nr)/2-z_B`.
921: It only yields the unconditional smoothed trace estimate `||Phi_Brho-tau_rB||_1<=sqrt[h(r_B)(E-nr)]`. That bound tends to zero as B_aux tends to infinity for **every** admissible input. Hence its improvement with auxiliary temperature is not evidence that the original input is close to tau_r. The positive conditional remainder C_B cannot be replaced by a scalar entropy-photon-number inequality with arbitrary quantum memory. The available linear conditional inequality supplies its sign and its inclusion in delta, but no bound that absorbs B4.
922: The attempted combination therefore stops at a precise obligation: an inequality converting `P_B+C_B+ell_B` to bounded input-test errors with polynomial cutoff constants, or an equivalent estimate `d<=C K^a delta^beta+C'K^-1/2` with fixed finite a and positive beta and all additional tails absorbed. B1–B5 do not give it. No constraint-respecting all-power-defeating sequence was constructed. The original displaced-tail family remains exactly the failed candidate audited in §2.4; changing the auxiliary bath is not a new physical counterexample.
923: ## 5. Primary-source comparison and contribution assessment
924: The source readings are versioned, frozen and recorded with actual ranges and endings in `../sources/i123_thermal_production_stability/read-manifest.json`. They are operation-level comparisons, not title searches or automatic stipulations.
925: * **Pinned EPnI**, [sections at the accepted commit](https://github.com/openai/math/tree/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/The-entropy-photon-number-inequality-September-24-2026/build/sections): the finite-energy independent-port theorem allows arbitrary entanglement within each port. The complete relevant introduction, preliminaries, minimization, metric and stationarity sections were read. Its faithful regularized minimizers have the moment/logarithmic control needed for its generator pairings; those are not arbitrary energy-cap states. Its thermal metric gap and stationarity calculations do not control `I_r-D` on our original entropy slice. A1 instead uses the stipulated finite-energy theorem and BV calculus, avoiding that domain transfer.
926: * **Li**, [2609.40061v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.40061v1): the different-pair rotation, all three nonnegative remainders, branch conditional independence, explicit erasure instrument, changed reference marginal and exact characteristic cancellation were read through their relevant proofs and Appendix A.4. The text already permits an auxiliary thermal temperature greater than one. B1 is its variable-temperature instrument worked out quantitatively, not an independent new erasure mechanism. Its equality cancellation is not a bounded inverse.
927: * **De Palma–Trevisan**, [1706.00440v2](https://arxiv.org/html/1706.00440v2): integral conditional information, shared noise, conditional independence, heat integration and asymptotics at finite energy/finite memory entropy supply the operations reconstructed in I117. They yield nonnegative linear remainders, not the missing trace-coercive remainder. The quantum memory is retained.
928: * **Cuesta**, [1902.05298v1](https://arxiv.org/html/1902.05298v1): Theorem 9 and its quantitative proof through Appendix V use local phase-space equations, a growing ball, Parseval and frequency tails, with higher-moment assumptions and explicit fourth-moment dependence in the differentiated local error. These complete operations matter for contribution assessment. W120 specializes the second port to a known thermal and uses centering plus dyadic subdivision to avoid differentiating an approximate defect; its HS domain argument pays a second-moment frequency tail. It does not import Cuesta's constants or higher-moment theorem. The previously recorded literal factor defect in the external proof is retained, not used to erase its valid operations.
929: * **Carlen–Maas**, [1609.01254v3](https://arxiv.org/html/1609.01254v3): the Bose oscillator and the production-contraction/metric argument were read through their endings. The Bose application uses intertwining and monotone operator metrics for production contraction and entropy decay; its discussion distinguishes this from proving geodesic convexity in that infinite-dimensional setting. With beta=h(r), its rates `e^(beta/2),e^(-beta/2)` are our generator multiplied by `2sinh(beta/2)`. Normalizing gives ordinary `I_r>=D`, already encoded by D1. Joint convexity of a metric pairing is not convexity of J_q and supplies no stated centered trace remainder. No general finite-energy differentiability theorem is imported from its formal Bose formulas.
930: * **Huber–König–Vershynina**, [1606.08603v2](https://arxiv.org/html/1606.08603v2): the regularity discussion and one-mode OU production calculation, Lemmas 8–9 and their complete displayed proofs, were read. Their proof explicitly assumes regularity for unbounded differentiations. It decomposes production into upward/downward Fisher terms and energy, deriving scoped sufficient bounds and examples for the proposed decay estimate. It is not an arbitrary internally entangled multimode `I_r-D` stability result. The present analysis does not assume its regularity by finite energy or positive time.
931: * **De Palma–Huber**, [1803.00470v2, §9](https://arxiv.org/html/1803.00470v2): the full conditional OU contraction proof combines conditional entropy inequalities with the linear energy evolution and a fixed thermal logarithm. It is a quantum conditional argument, not a classical substitution. Its conditional-information framework requires finite quantum energy and finite memory entropy; the displayed application is one mode. Its conclusion is a relative-entropy contraction rate, not a trace remainder after subtracting that rate. We use neither an unproved strict improvement nor its statement outside those prerequisites.
932: * **Beigi–Rahimi-Keshari**, [2311.09572v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2311.09572v2): the meta inequality, its rearrangement and diagonal optimization proof, its finite-mean domain appendix, single-mode sharp p-log-Sobolev derivation and the multimode argument through Theorem 10 were read, including the relevant appendix endings. The single-mode theorem optimizes over finite-mean states, with p=1 interpreted by a limit and possible extended production values; it is not a finite high-moment theorem in disguise. The multimode sharp Gaussian consequence has its stated Gaussian restriction. The general multimode p=2 bound instead uses weighted diagonal decompositions, interpolation, classical tensorization only on diagonal operators, and a spectral-gap tightening step, with the explicit dimension-dependent constant in Theorem 10. That general quantum result is not being discarded, but it does not lower-bound the sharp physical `I_r-D` remainder by centered trace distance. Weighted-Lp hypercontractivity requires membership in the indicated weighted spaces; finite mean does not justify every negative thermal power.
933: * **Becker–Rouzé–Salzmann**, [2610.06764v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.06764v1), current October 2026 comparison: the precise entropy-production domain `Dom(L) intersect {Gamma^-1 sigma<=rho<=Gamma sigma}`, the endpoint/gap hypotheses, and the complete bounded-generator and finite-core Gibbs-weight comparison proofs were read. The stated global entropy decay is distinct from the domain on which the logarithmic pairing is defined. Their finite-core comparison requires form-domain inclusion, form comparison and a positive gap; its constant contains the reference log difference and inverse gap. The norm perturbation theorem requires a consistent family of bounded weighted-Lp perturbations and a Trotter hypothesis. Neither operation asserts a remainder for our original thermal attenuator under a mean-energy constraint. Their nonlinear-jump sampler applications are different dynamics and are not substituted for P_v. No theorem from this fresh external paper is used as a proof input here.
934: Fresh metadata responses fixed the versions above; acquisition errors, output truncations and recovery ranges are retained in the failure log. No new PDF extraction was needed. The selected-source lexical results are exact but are not a complete semantic literature search. The inherited complete 13,223-entry tree and 10,373 original-position mapping are preserved and checked; a title's failure to mention a theorem is not evidence of novelty.
935: **Independent contribution assessment:** W120/M120 are correct full-scope technical bridges, not substantial nominations on this evidence. Their mechanism combines existing doubling/instrument recovery, a known-thermal phase-space equation, elementary dyadic propagation, Hilbert–Schmidt Fourier tails and an energy/rank trace cutoff. The second-moment domain argument and same-input reversal are real mathematical work; they do not change the overall technical disposition. M3, D1–D4 and the exact D5–D9 tail asymptotics also remain technical. A1 and the exact-constraint moment obstruction are new proofs in this report at their stated scopes; B1–B5 are a quantitative specialization and a scoped budget failure. None is a universal Target B power theorem or a no-power counterexample. There is no ninth finding here.
936: ## 6. Evidence, validation, and terminal disposition
937: The owned inventory is exactly this report and the two I123 roots named in the brief. `references.json` freezes the task, original report, parent receipt, instructions, upstream identity and ledgers. The original recursive record is reused as a single frozen record, not recursively expanded into copied package trees. `inherited.json` binds the original I114/I117/I120 package seals, inventories and references. Only the four permitted live ledgers may drift; every snapshot remains immutable.
938: The verifier first checks all owned sealed bytes, all inherited immutable bytes and exact inventories, all original corpus bytes and Git blob identities, the 776 existing PDF-view bindings, exact lexical occurrence multisets, titles and selected inventories, range/hash/line provenance, and deterministic HTML conversions. It traverses the original corpus once and does not call nested corpus verifiers. Only after these checks does it run the finite diagnostics. It has no network, write, subprocess, bytecode, repair or reseal path. Preseal validation precedes the single final seal; the preseal mode itself writes nothing. Read ranges distinguish decoded JSON briefs, actual splitlines, LF counts, and HTML views. Failed or truncated acquisition/read attempts are not silently represented as complete reads.
939: python3 -B frontier/experiments/i123_thermal_production_stability/verify.py
940: The finite rational checks cover normalization and centering identities, the scalar BV decomposition algebra, the auxiliary instrument constants and fixed-bath specialization, the cost tradeoff, and cutoff/conditional-power arithmetic, alongside inherited diagnostics. They certify neither universal analytic proofs nor substantiality.
941: **Final recommendation:** retain all original I120 passes without repairs and the inherited I117/I114 passes at exact scope. Target B remains unproved and unrefuted for every possible positive exponent at most one half. Both concrete attempts identify unpaid quantum coercivity obligations. Park the thermal-stability branch until a new mechanism controls the production measure or the combined doubling remainders with an absorbable polynomial budget. Further rounds spent only improving iterated-log constants are not recommended.
942: frontier/experiments/i123_thermal_production_stability/verify.py
943: Finite exact rational/formal coefficient diagnostics only; no universal proof, rate, or substantiality certification
