1438: ## 2. Independent original I126 audit, without repairs
1439: ### R126 topology and equivalence — PASS without repairs
1440: Let \(K=\overline{(0,1)^2}\). The dense unit-ball tests include \(f_1=1\). For the original metric
1441: d_m(\lambda,\lambda')=\sum_{j\ge1}2^{-j}\min(1,|\lambda(f_j)-\lambda'(f_j)|),
1442: convergence implies convergence of each test integral. The constant test bounds total masses; uniform approximation then extends convergence to all of \(C(K)\). Conversely weak convergence gives each summand's convergence and the geometric tail is uniform. Positivity and compactness of \(K\) are essential here. This is the weak topology of finite measures, including total mass, not vague convergence on the open square.
1443: Here is the fixed-radius continuity argument in detail. For a nonempty compact planar set \(C\), its distance function is Lipschitz. At a differentiability point outside \(C\), a nearest point gives a direction along which the directional derivative is \(-1\); the Lipschitz bound then forces gradient norm one. A differentiable function has gradient zero at almost every density point of any one of its level sets: otherwise a fixed cone has a nonzero first-order change and excludes a positive density fraction of the level set. Rademacher's theorem and the density theorem therefore imply that \(\{z:\operatorname{dist}(z,C)=r\}\) has zero area for **every** \(r>0\), not just almost every radius. Ordered-curve convergence implies Hausdorff trace convergence, and distance functions differ uniformly by at most the Hausdorff distance. Indicators of the radius-\(r\) tubes thus converge almost everywhere. Tubes of curves in the fixed square lie in a common bounded enlargement. Dominated convergence against \(f\circ\Pi\), with the fixed factor \(r^{-1/3}\), proves weak continuity of \(T_r\). Clamping the full plane tube preserves this reasoning and its total mass; it is not the tube intersected with the open square.
1444: Write \(P_{n,\xi}\) for the **separately normalized** quenched curve law, \(K_{n,\xi}\) for the joint curve/counting law, and \(F_r(\gamma)=(\gamma,T_r\gamma)\). On each configuration the two joint readouts have the identical ordered curve. Their product-metric displacement is precisely \(d_m(\nu_n^c,T_r\gamma_n)\). Consequently
1445: d_{BL}(K_{n,\xi},(F_r)_*P_{n,\xi})
1446: \le\phi_{n,\xi}[d_m(\nu_n^c,T_r\gamma_n)].
1447: Markov over the original iid-sign environment and the triangle inequality give R1 with the original thresholds \(3a,a,a,a\) and term \(R_{n,r}/a\). No partition function weights the environment. For fixed \(r\), continuity of \(F_r\) on curve space induces continuity on probability laws. Apply the deterministic subsequence criterion for convergence in environmental probability to the stipulated quenched curve theorem. The other law, \((F_r)_*Q\), tends to the joint same-sample SLE/Minkowski law as \(r\downarrow0\). This proves sufficiency in exactly the order \(n\to\infty\) at fixed \(r\), then \(r\downarrow0\).
1448: For necessity the discrepancy \((\gamma,\lambda)\mapsto d_m(\lambda,T_r\gamma)\) is bounded continuous. Quenched joint convergence in probability makes its conditional expectations converge in probability; boundedness converts this to convergence of environmental means. Their limit is \(E d_m(\mu_\gamma,T_r\gamma)\), which tends to zero by bounded convergence in probability. This proves both directions of R126. It requires no environmental variance estimate or independent reconstruction of the coupling. R1 is **PASS without repairs**. The claims about limit order, full boundary convention, total-mass topology, and absence of an expectation conclusion are also **PASS without repairs**.
1449: ### R2 — FAIL for the literal endpoint-only cutoff class
1450: The original sentence requires only \(\chi_\delta\in C(K)\), equal to zero in the width-\(\delta\) strip and one outside the width-\(2\delta\) strip. It does not state \(0\le\chi_\delta\le1\). Its asserted positivity argument needs \(1-\chi_\delta\ge0\). For example, choose a transition point \(z\) with \(\chi_\delta(z)=2\), and deterministic positive measures \(\lambda=\lambda'=\delta_z\). The interior discrepancy is zero for every test, the proposed boundary budget is \(-2\), and the right side of R2 is \(2^{-J}-2<0\), while the metric discrepancy is zero. Thus the measure inequality invoked in the proof does not follow from the stated cutoff conditions. This diagnoses the unrestricted cutoff formulation; it is not a counterexample to G in the FK model.
1451: If “cutoff” is read by convention as a function valued in \([0,1]\), the intended algebra is correct. Under the requested strict no-repair reading, I do not silently supply that condition and pass the original endpoint-only statement. Separately, **R129-cutoff** is proved with the explicit hypothesis \(0\le\chi_\delta\le1\): for \(\|f_j\|_\infty\le1\),
1452: | (\lambda-\lambda')(f_j)|\le
1453: | (\lambda-\lambda')(\chi_\delta f_j)|+
1454: (\lambda+\lambda')(1-\chi_\delta).
1455: Cap the left side by one, sum the first \(J\) weights, use Cauchy–Schwarz for the interior term and bound the remaining metric tail by \(2^{-J}\). This proves precisely the R2 bound for this explicitly smaller class. It suffices to have each interior \(L^2\) error vanish at fixed \(\delta,j\) in the order \(n\to\infty,r\downarrow0\), and boundary first moments vanish as \(\delta\downarrow0\) after those limits, then send \(J\to\infty\). Bounded total mass in probability follows from the test one and R126; no global boundary second moment is necessary. This clarification is technical and does not pay any disordered estimate.
1456: ### E126 and deterministic shifts — PASS without repairs
1457: The finite law may have arbitrary dependent coordinates and may be supported on a proper subset of the binary cube. Tilts preserve that support. The score is \(X_i-E_sX_i\), and
1458: \(\partial_i A=E_sX_i\), \(\partial_i^2A=\operatorname{Var}_sX_i\le1/4\). In particular the reference normalizer cannot be omitted. Convexity, \(A(0)=0\), and centered independent fair signs give \(E_\xi A(a\xi)\ge0\). Conditioning on all signs other than \(i\) gives
1459: E_{\xi_i}[a_i\xi_i\partial_iA]=
1460: \frac{a_i}{2}\int_{-a_i}^{a_i}\partial_i^2A\,dt\le a_i^2/4.
1461: Subtracting the nonnegative mean log normalizer proves \(E D(P_s\Vert P_0)\le\sum a_i^2/4\). Classical half-TV satisfies \(2TV^2\le D\), by log-sum followed by the binary second derivative \(1/[p(1-p)]\ge4\). Hence E1 and both E2 inequalities have their stated constants \(1/8\). Measurable deterministic readouts contract TV even when their outputs include the entire curve and an unbounded finite mass; this is a statement about laws, not expectations of that mass.
1462: For deterministic \(b\), differentiation of \(t A'(tb)-A(tb)\) along the line yields
1463: D(P_b\Vert P_0)=\int_0^1t\operatorname{Var}_{tb}(b\cdot X)dt
1464: \le\|b\|_1^2/8.
1465: The range of \(b\cdot X\) is at most \(\|b\|_1\), including mixed signs. Pinsker gives \(TV(P_b,P_0)\le\|b\|_1/4\); apply E1 relative to \(P_b\) and the triangle inequality. No covariance-matrix independence is assumed.
1466: ### E3 physical application and UI — PASS without repairs
1467: The sampled square has \(2n(n+1)-2n=2n^2\) edges, after removing precisely the top and left wired boundary edges. The physical critical-point operation establishes the magnetization threshold and then the product equation for the two odds. Self-duality alone is not the premise. Thus the reference law really is the pure critical q=2 law on this graph and its exact density is \(\exp(\theta\sum_e\xi_eX_e-A(\theta\xi))\). E3 follows with \(E TV^2\le n^2\theta^2/4\) and \(E TV\le n\theta/2\).
1468: For deterministic \(\varepsilon_n\) in the admissible interval and \(n\theta(\varepsilon_n)\to0\), Markov and contraction transfer the full pure occupation theorem with its one constant \(c_0\). This proves the joint quenched-in-probability law, with total mass and the same continuum curve mark. The exact graph, all full medial traversals, massless connectors, and clamp remain unchanged. This is an actual shrinking-disorder theorem, not fixed G.
1469: Put \(K_0=\tfrac12\log(1+\sqrt2)\) and \(K_\pm=\beta_c(\varepsilon)(1\pm\varepsilon)\). The product equation gives \(K_-\le K_0\le K_+\). For \(\varepsilon\le1/2\), \(\beta_c\le2K_0\) and \(K_-\ge K_0/3\). With \(F'(K)=2/(1-e^{-2K})\), the mean value bound is exactly
1470: \theta(\varepsilon)\le\frac{4K_0}{1-e^{-2K_0/3}}\varepsilon.
1471: Every configuration has at most \(Cn^2\) counted full traversals, so normalized mass is at most \(Cc_0n^{1/3}\). TV consequently bounds its expectation difference by \(C'c_0\theta_n n^{4/3}\). The stronger sufficient rate in I126 is correct and is not claimed necessary. The pure interior measures are uniformly integrable from their \(L^2\) replacement estimates; the boundary first-moment bound lets the strip error tend to zero, giving UI for the full pure mass. For the disordered mass tails use the same deterministic bound times TV, then handle finitely many initial meshes separately. Thus \(\theta_n n^{4/3}\to0\) transfers annealed UI. Weak finite-measure convergence alone does not do so.
1472: Uniformity for deterministic boundary partitions does not justify restarting the fair-sign proof under an exploration posterior. Conditioning on a rare event can cost its reciprocal probability. Those limitations, and the statement that the fixed-disorder volume bound is eventually trivial, are **PASS without repairs**. Neither limitation refutes G.
1473: ## 3. Exact fixed target and complete source-operation comparison
1474: Throughout the attempts, fix sufficiently small **positive** \(\varepsilon\), independent of \(n\). Use the sampled square graph and marked corners just audited, iid fair signs \(\xi_e\), \(J_e=1+\varepsilon\xi_e\), actual \(\beta_c(\varepsilon)\), and
1475: \phi_{n,\xi}(w)=Z_{n,\xi}^{-1}2^{k(w)}
1476: \prod_{e\in E_n}(\sqrt2e^{\theta\xi_e})^{w_e}.
1477: The desired G is one \(0<c_\varepsilon<\infty\), independent of \(n\), for which
1478: \mathcal L_{\phi_{n,\xi}}\left([\gamma_n],c_\varepsilon n^{-5/3}
1479: \sum_{\text{full medial traversals }e}\delta_{\Pi(z_e)}\right)
1480: \longrightarrow\mathcal L(\gamma,\mu_\gamma)
1481: in environmental probability, in bounded ordered-curve times weak positive finite-measure topology on the closed square. \(\gamma\) is SLE\(_{16/3}\); \(\mu_\gamma\) is its whole-plane tubular Minkowski measure on the **same sample**, using \(r^{-1/3}\Pi_*1_{\{d(z,\operatorname{tr}\gamma)<r\}}dA\). Initial/terminal half-edges and connectors have no mass. Counting vertices, only interior edges, a capacity clock, or a partition-function-weighted environmental law would change the target.
1482: The read manifest records the complete eight sections of the pure occupation companion, the complete eight sections of the disordered FK companion, and the pure/local-map/moments/variance/trajectory/crossings/criticality operations of its weak-disorder Ising companion. All were read through their relevant endings. This includes the lower calculations used in calibration and the final driver deduction, not just theorem statements.
1483: In the pure proof, radial stopping circuits first cancel microscopic nesting normalizers, including complex perturbations, with relative control of both full and thresholded masses. The angular proof obtains a summable Laplacian remainder: mixed small/large spectral-zero bands are suppressed by the width tail, while unmixed bands have a cubic cancellation. Potential inversion retains all four parity classes. Neutral electric correlations then have a power error, and analytic continuation plus a positive construction proves a **nonzero** special-charge amplitude. The one-arm and preliminary two-arm exponents alone are not the calibration.
1484: The outward coupling minorizes arbitrary band data after a good past, retaining the probability of that past's start-connected extensions inside its exterior mixture. It divides by the continuation denominator only after a matching two-arm bound. The common band has exactly two through-strands and fixed transmitted primal/dual regions. Hence the exterior distinguished-strand decision really agrees. Signed charge factors are controlled by positive reference tilts and moment bounds. In calibration, using the same mesoscopic \(r,R\) for \(N\in[n,2n]\) produces a **relative** error \(a_N/a_n=1+O(n^{-c})\); its dyadic sum converges and gives a positive finite amplitude on the entire mesh sequence. Disk calibration then identifies the continuum constant. The final occupation proof cancels its intermediate-scale normalization in the separated-pair conditional estimate, treats near pairs down to mesh, and separately bounds boundary first moments. Its last step identifies the whole-plane tube on the same ordered SLE sample. These operations already provide the pure theorem; they are not new here.
1485: The disorder map is an exact addition/integration of local Gibbs variables, including dummy coins and complete comparison stars. It retains the common scalar in every boundary condition, and the same physical random bond on every sheet. The projected charge \(q_s\) is defined by a distant **energy** test. Preservation of that charge for normalized finite insertions is not preservation of a two-arm mark. For two separated groups its normalized addition has denominator \(1+c_{AB}\), with leading \(c_{AB}\simeq\kappa_\mathrm{en}\ell^{-2}T_AT_B\). The covariance with that denominator supplies the negative quartic term in the variance flow. No marked numerator has yet appeared in that computation.
1486: With the source's chosen fixed localization constants, its actual tuned trajectory has
1487: X_k\le A\sqrt{v_k},\quad Y_k\le Bv_k,\quad |m_k|\le Dv_k,
1488: \qquad -C_2v_k^2\le v_{k+1}-v_k\le-cv_k^2.
1489: \tag{3.1}
1490: For small \(v_0>0\), reciprocal increments are bounded above and below by positive constants: divide the last differences by \(v_kv_{k+1}\), using \(v_{k+1}\ge v_k(1-C_2v_0)\). Thus \(v_k\asymp(v_0^{-1}+k)^{-1}\). In particular \(\sum v_k\) and \(\sum\sqrt{v_k}\) diverge, whereas \(\sum v_k^{3/2}<\infty\). This is a deduction along the actual flow, not a replacement by a guessed ODE. Its bulk quartic coefficient contains a fixed-localization error tending to zero when that localization is enlarged; such an error is not automatically a summable scale error or an exact limiting coefficient.
1491: The crossing operation multiplies **conditional exterior suprema** on disjoint enlarged environment neighborhoods before averaging iid primitives; it does not multiply arbitrary conditional quenched crossing probabilities. Pivotal geometry supplies a factor \((s_k/r)^c\), making the sum \(\sum X_k(s_k/r)^c\) vanish for a macroscopic test even though \(\sum X_k\) diverges. The criticality proof uses this local comparison, torus partition ratios with the same scalars, barriers, and sharpness to identify the physical threshold. The FK polygon operation also stops at fixed physical scale, where only finitely many residual potentials remain. Straight sides, free sides modulo spin flip, corners, and boundary changes all have their specified transfer estimates.
1492: The subsequent FK slit proof keeps a fixed environment after a countable-test extraction, deletes near punctures in the order mesh then physical radius, and places a fixed collared polygon on a finite cover. It compares shared edges and induced boundary partitions on that cover, not independent disorder on sheets. Finally the driver proof divides a four-point rare probability only while the short interval is fixed, takes the mesh limit, replaces it by a bounded martingale test, and only then shrinks the interval. Two tests identify drift zero and quadratic variation \(16/3\); tip approximation and target no-return give the full ordered curve. This ending pays the curve theorem but contains no division by a microscopic hit probability and no traversal normalization.
1493: ## 4. Attempt one: transport the distinguished mark and compute its response
1494: The starting construction is exact on the target graph. Let \(H\) be the event that a specified full medial segment belongs to the distinguished \(a\)-to-\(b\) strand, or its two-arm continuation event with the exterior strand selector retained. Its indicator is a function of the FK configuration; conditioning on an exterior pairs its ports rather than replacing the distinguished strand by an arbitrary loop. One can equally take a nonnegative mark \(h\le1\), obtained by conditioning that indicator. No mark changes the environmental prior.
1495: ### M129 — normalized microscopic response
1496: For the pure law on this exact finite graph write \(p=P_0(H)>0\), \(p_i=P_0X_i\), \(p_i^H=P_0(X_i\mid H)\), and \(d_i=p_i^H-p_i\). Define \(P_s\) by the exact log-odds tilt. Finite differentiation gives
1497: \partial_i\log P_s(H)=E_s(X_i\mid H)-E_sX_i,
1498: \partial_i^2\log P_s(H)=\operatorname{Var}_s(X_i\mid H)-\operatorname{Var}_sX_i.
1499: \tag{M1}
1500: Consequently the leading terms under the original independent signs are
1501: \frac{E_\xi P_{\theta\xi}(H)}p
1502: =1+\frac{\theta^2}{2}\sum_i(1-2p_i)d_i+O_n(\theta^4),
1503: \tag{M2}
1504: E_\xi\log\frac{P_{\theta\xi}(H)}p
1505: =\frac{\theta^2}{2}\sum_i\big[(1-2p_i)d_i-d_i^2\big]+O_n(\theta^4),
1506: \tag{M3}
1507: \operatorname{Var}_\xi\left(\log\frac{P_{\theta\xi}(H)}p\right)
1508: =\theta^2\sum_i d_i^2+O_n(\theta^4).
1509: \tag{M4}
1510: All four statements apply to the actual physical family by inserting \(\theta=\theta(\varepsilon)\); they do not center at a different temperature.
1511: Proof: \(\log(P_s(H)/p)=\log E_0(e^{s\cdot X}\mid H)-\log E_0e^{s\cdot X}\). Differentiate both log normalizers. Since \(X_i^2=X_i\), the second derivative of the probability itself divided by \(p\) is
1512: \(\operatorname{Var}_H X_i-\operatorname{Var}_0X_i+d_i^2=(1-2p_i)d_i\). Independence of the signs kills mixed second derivatives; simultaneous sign reversal kills odd Taylor degrees. Squaring the linear term of the logarithm proves M4. Taylor's formula is valid on every fixed finite graph. For \(M|\theta|\le1\), derivatives along \(\theta\xi\) are bounded by universal constants times powers of \(M\): log derivatives are cumulants of a variable of range at most \(M\), also under conditioning on \(H\). The relative-probability derivatives additionally have a factor at most \(e^{2M|\theta|}\). Thus one may bound the displayed remainders by \(CM^4\theta^4\) in that range, uniformly over positive-probability \(H\). This explicit volume dependence prohibits exchanging the fixed-graph expansion with \(n\to\infty\) at fixed \(\theta\). ∎
1513: This calculation exhibits a genuine distinction. In a self-dual translation-invariant pure plane reference with each edge marginal \(1/2\), a perturbation on any **fixed finite** edge set has zero M2 coefficient for every event of positive probability. M3 instead has coefficient \(-\tfrac12\sum d_i^2\). On the square with mixed boundary data the factor \(1-2p_i\) must be retained; self-duality is not permission to set all finite-square marginals to \(1/2\). Moreover a negative mean logarithm is not a negative exponent of the mean probability or a statement about total traversal mass. Fluctuations in M4 account for the difference at this order. These are checks against an incorrect anomalous-dimension inference, not a derivation of a slowly varying factor in G.
1514: On a cover, index disorder by physical bonds \(b\), and set \(Y_b=\sum_{e:\,\operatorname{proj}(e)=b}X_e\). The exact response uses \(s_bY_b\), not independent signs for the summands. The second coefficient in the relative probability is then
1515: \frac12\sum_b\left\{
1516: \operatorname{Var}_H Y_b-\operatorname{Var}_0Y_b+
1517: (E_HY_b-E_0Y_b)^2\right\}.
1518: \tag{M5}
1519: Mixed covariances between different occurrences of the same bond remain. The Bernoulli simplification in M2 applies only to a single physical occurrence, not to \(Y_b\). This keeps precisely the common-sheet distinction of the source operations.
1520: ### M129-local — a summable Taylor remainder, with its rare-mark cost
1521: Here is a usable estimate at one actual scale. Let \(\mu\) be any finite pure conditional reference specification, including its reference coins and its exterior port pairing; let \(0\le h\le1\), \(p=\mu h>0\). Write \(\mu_h=h\mu/p\), \(\Delta_hF=\mu_hF-\mu F\). For a real random potential \(F\), which may be dependent across its fixed finite stencil, suppose
1522: \(E\|F\|_\infty^{40}\le X^{40}\), \(X\le1\). Then
1523: E\log\frac{\mu(h e^F)}{p\mu(e^F)}
1524: =E\Delta_hF+\frac12E(\operatorname{Var}_{\mu_h}F-
1525: \operatorname{Var}_\mu F)+O(X^3),
1526: \tag{M6}
1527: and
1528: E\frac{\mu(h e^F)}{p\mu(e^F)}
1529: =1+E\Delta_hF+\frac12E[\mu_hF^2-\mu F^2]
1530: -E[(\mu F)\Delta_hF]+O(X^3+X^{40}/p).
1531: \tag{M7}
1532: The constants are absolute; no independence of \(F\)'s terms and no lower bound on \(p\) is hidden. For random reference/mark pairs the same pathwise expansion holds, but the last error is bounded by \(CX^3+C E[1_{\{\|F\|>1\}}/p]\) rather than \(CX^{40}/p\), unless a deterministic lower bound on \(p\) is available.
1533: To prove M6, differentiate \(\log\mu_h e^{tF}-\log\mu e^{tF}\), \(0\le t\le1\). Its third derivative is the difference of third centered moments and is bounded by \(16\|F\|^3\). Taylor with integral remainder and Lyapunov's inequality give \(O(X^3)\) without exponential moments. For M7, on \(\|F\|\le1\) Taylor-expand the quotient. Its third derivative is bounded by \(C\|F\|^3\); multiplying the two expansions produces the negative denominator term shown. On the complementary event the actual quotient is at most \(1/p\). The omitted constant, linear, and quadratic polynomials have expected absolute values at most \(CX^{40}\) there, since \(\|F\|^j1_{\{\|F\|>1\}}\le\|F\|^{40}\) for \(j\le2\). Markov gives the remaining \(X^{40}/p\). This proves both estimates. ∎
1534: For a **fixed bounded scale stencil** of the source trajectory, Minkowski's inequality gives \(X\le C\sqrt{v_k}\). Hence the third-order remainder in M6 is summable along the actual trajectory, and M7 has summable remainder \(O(v_k^{3/2}+v_k^{20}/p)\) when \(p\) is bounded below. This is a paid remainder estimate, not a claim that the first and second terms cancel. The linear term can contain the deterministic \(O(v_k)\) mean and stable interaction. In M7 the covariance and the negative denominator term are also of order \(v_k\). For a mark selected by past random fields, even the mean-zero part of the linear term need not average to zero.
1535: To specify what must be transported, one exact integration of an interior block \(U\), with all relevant potentials in \(F\), changes the mark to
1536: h'(\chi_{U^c})=
1537: \frac{\mu_0(h e^F\mid\chi_{U^c})}
1538: {\mu_0(e^F\mid\chi_{U^c})},
1539: F'(\chi_{U^c})=\log\mu_0(e^F\mid\chi_{U^c}).
1540: \tag{M8}
1541: The full integral of \(h e^F\) is unchanged. Its unmarked and marked integrals must both be carried through the local-addition decorations and all discarded scalar factors. For a connectivity event, retaining the finite connectivity states and the distinguished port selector makes M8 exact. Iterating it yields a bounded conditional mark but does **not** make it the pure two-arm indicator at the next scale, nor prove locality of that mark. A common unmarked scalar cancels in an unmarked boundary ratio; the marked numerator still contains \(h'\).
1542: I applied M6–M8 to the structure of the source's projected-charge addition. Its energy projection controls the response to a far energy field and supplies the bulk variance flow; it does not evaluate the differences \(\mu_hF-\mu F\) or \(\operatorname{Var}_{\mu_h}F-\operatorname{Var}_\mu F\) for this transported strand mark. In particular the negative quartic bulk-variance contribution cannot be relabeled a marked anomalous dimension. M2 shows a bare second-order annealed cancellation in the balanced plane reference, but M5, the generated \(O(v_k)\) interactions, and the transported mark all remain in the finite-square scale calculation. I do **not** have a proof that their full order-\(v_k\) coefficient vanishes, or that it is nonzero.
1543: The missing estimate is now more precise than nonsummability of \(\sqrt{v_k}\): identify the full marked relative scale response, including its linear mean/stable part and normalized quadratic part, with an absolutely summable error under the correct two-arm conditional laws. An order-\(v_k\) term of fixed nonzero sign would have a divergent harmonic sum. Cancellation of **all** such terms plus a uniformly controlled \(O(v_k^{1+\alpha})\), \(\alpha>0\), remainder would give a convergent positive product, because finitely many initial factors are strictly positive by finite energy. Neither alternative is proved here for that response. The local Taylor remainder alone is not the full adjacent-scale error.
1544: Even a nonzero first-moment coefficient would not refute G without typical-mass or tail control. No estimate derived here makes \(n^{-5/3}\#\gamma_n\) vanish/diverge in probability or fail tightness. No logarithmic renormalization theorem is claimed. This attempt stops at the marked transfer and order-\(v_k\) cancellation problem; it has not constructed a fixed-disorder counterexample.
1545: ## 5. Attempt two: normalized replacement with the actual posterior
1546: Let \(\pi(\xi)=2^{-M}\) and let \(Q(d\xi,dw)=\pi(d\xi)\phi_\xi(dw)\). For a configuration history event \(A\) of positive joint probability,
1547: \pi_A(\xi)=\frac{\pi(\xi)\phi_\xi(A)}{Q(A)}.
1548: \tag{P1}
1549: Given \(\xi,A\), the remaining configuration has its usual FK conditional law. After marginalizing \(\xi\), it is a mixture with the posterior P1. In a switch-revealing transcript, exposed edge factors and the remaining partition function occur in \(\phi_\xi(A)\), divided by the **full original** \(Z_\xi\). The remaining partition function cannot erase that denominator. Formula P1 also applies to hitting a disk or a specified strand segment, whether or not that event is a stopping history.
1550: ### P129 — one-coordinate posterior and an actual graph example
1551: Fix all other signs. Write \(\phi_-\) for sign \(i=-1\), let \(t=e^{2\theta}-1\), \(p_i^-=\phi_-X_i\), and \(p_i^{-,A}=\phi_-(X_i\mid A)\). Bayes and the normalized single-edge tilt give
1552: \frac{\pi_A(\xi_i=+1\mid\xi_{-i})}{\pi_A(\xi_i=-1\mid\xi_{-i})}
1553: =\frac{1+t p_i^{-,A}}{1+t p_i^-}.
1554: \tag{P2}
1555: Thus the log odds have absolute value at most \(2\theta\), and more precisely at most \(t|p_i^{-,A}-p_i^-|\). Every scalar in P2 comes from separately normalizing the two environments. The same signs are used on repeated sheets; there \(1+tX_i\) is replaced by \(e^{2\theta Y_i}\), so the Bernoulli formula must not be reused.
1556: On the exact \(n=1\) square, wiring top and left contracts their three vertices. The bottom-right vertex is joined to this wire by the two sampled edges. For their odds \(u,v\), the FK configuration weights, after division by a common factor two, are \(2,u,v,uv\). If the first edge is revealed open, then
1557: L(u,v)=\phi_{u,v}(X_1=1)=\frac{u(1+v)}{2+u+v+uv},
1558: \qquad \partial_v L=\frac{u}{(2+u+v+uv)^2}>0.
1559: \tag{P3}
1560: Taking \(u=\sqrt2e^{\pm\theta}\) and \(v=\sqrt2e^{\pm\theta}\), averaging over the still unobserved first sign shows \(\pi_A(\xi_2=+1)>1/2\) for every \(\theta>0\). Moreover \(L(u,v_+)/L(u,v_-)\) depends strictly on \(u\): after removing its positive constant factor, its derivative has numerator \(v_--v_+<0\). Hence the two latent signs are dependent under this posterior. This is an exact calculation in the prescribed physical model, not an auxiliary counterexample or an interface-limit claim. It is a single-edge reveal; no special stopping-line property is assumed.
1561: The entropy cost of the posterior is also explicit:
1562: D(\pi_A\Vert\pi)=E_{\pi_A}\log\frac{\phi_\xi(A)}{Q(A)}
1563: \le\log\frac1{Q(A)}.
1564: \tag{P4}
1565: If signs are exposed successively, the chain rule writes this entropy as the sum of expected binary conditional divergences from a fair sign. For each prefix, its likelihood ratio is the quotient of sums over remaining signs of \(\phi_{+,\mathrm{rest}}(A)\) and \(\phi_{-,\mathrm{rest}}(A)\). The pointwise ratio lies in \([e^{-2\theta},e^{2\theta}]\), so the quotient of sums does too. For binary log odds \(\ell\), differentiation of its divergence from a fair sign gives \(\ell p_\ell(1-p_\ell)\); integrating yields \(D\le\ell^2/8\). Thus
1566: D(\pi_A\Vert\pi)\le \min\{\log(1/Q(A)),\ M\theta^2/2\}.
1567: \tag{P5}
1568: P5 is a bound for biased dependent posteriors, derived from their likelihood, not E126 reapplied to them. It does not vanish in the required rare-hit regime. In particular it provides no uniform conditional Pinsker budget after normalizing a microscopic hit.
1569: ### P129-replacement — exact annealed expansion of the whole marked discrepancy
1570: There is a more directed way to use the symmetry of each original sign. For any finite binary law \(P\), any bounded real readout \(F\), and one coordinate of marginal \(p=PX_i\), direct division of the two partition functions gives
1571: \frac12(P_{+\theta e_i}F+P_{-\theta e_i}F)-PF
1572: =b(p,\theta)\operatorname{Cov}_P(F,X_i),
1573: \tag{P6}
1574: b(p,\theta)=
1575: \frac{(\cosh\theta-1)(1-2p)}
1576: {1+2p(1-p)(\cosh\theta-1)}.
1577: Indeed \(P_{t e_i}F=PF+(e^t-1)\operatorname{Cov}_P(F,X_i)/[1+p(e^t-1)]\); averaging the two signs and multiplying the denominators proves P6. This identity is exact at finite \(\theta\), for arbitrary dependence, and retains both normalization factors.
1578: Order the sampled edges deterministically. Put \(s^{(i-1)}=(\theta\xi_1,\ldots,\theta\xi_{i-1},0,\ldots,0)\). Iterating P6 under the **original** independent signs yields
1579: E_\xi P_{\theta\xi}F-P_0F
1580: =\sum_{i=1}^M E_{\xi_1,\ldots,\xi_{i-1}}
1581: \left[b(P_{s^{(i-1)}}X_i,\theta)
1582: \operatorname{Cov}_{P_{s^{(i-1)}}}(F,X_i)\right].
1583: \tag{P7}
1584: There is no posterior-independence assumption: the signs here are added before sampling or conditioning the configuration. After some tilts, the next marginal need not be \(1/2\), even if all original pure marginals were. Therefore the first-coordinate cancellation does not telescope to zero.
1585: Use the actual bounded readout
1586: F_{n,r,c}(w)=d_m\left(cn^{-5/3}\sum_{e\text{ traversed}}\delta_{\Pi z_e},
1587: T_r\gamma_n(w)\right)\in[0,1].
1588: P7 is an exact expression for \(R_{n,r}(c)-P_0F_{n,r,c}\). It therefore retains the ordered curve, its own counting mark, the whole-plane tube and total mass. A sufficient new estimate would make its right side tend to the appropriate calibrated replacement error. For \(c=c_0\), making the absolute sum tend to zero in the R126 limit order would prove fixed G with that constant; for a different \(c\), the pure discrepancy is retained and must be calibrated too.
1589: The elementary absolute bound still costs volume: \(|b(p,\theta)|\le\cosh\theta-1\) and \(|\operatorname{Cov}(F,X_i)|\le1/4\) for \(0\le F\le1\), giving \(M(\cosh\theta-1)/4\). P7 exposes the possible gain as a product of imbalance \(|1-2p_i|\) and the actual marked influence, but neither factor has a summable bound under the partly tilted laws or hit-conditioned laws in this investigation. The geometric squared-influence estimates in shrinking-window literature concern specific crossings and mixing rates, not \(F_{n,r,c}\)'s microscopic count under fixed disorder.
1590: One could try the source's conditional-supremum reversal instead. A fixed polygonal crossing has a pivotal edit penalty from several long passages. For a microscopic traversal indicator the edit can change the counted local piece without such a macroscopic pivotal event. Summing a bounded probability of edits over \(O(n^2)\) candidate pieces and multiplying by \(n^{-5/3}\) leaves an \(n^{1/3}\) loss before any scale summation. A two-arm probability and a calibrated conditional number of traversals would have to replace that crude count. Conditioning to obtain those two arms produces P1, and using the local comparison envelope then costs its expectation under that posterior, not under iid primitives. Dividing an absolute envelope by \(Q(H)\) merely restates the rare-hit loss. The source's finite-cover and fixed-short-interval ending avoids this division; it does not bound it.
1591: Accordingly P1–P7 pay the posterior, normalization, and an exact replacement expansion. They do not prove the interior \(L^2\) or boundary first-moment estimates of R129-cutoff, nor even the bounded replacement R at fixed disorder. Repeating E126, changing the order of limits, or dropping total mass would conceal this gap. Attempt two does not close G.
1592: ## 6. Literature, contribution, and disposition
1593: Fresh primary-source acquisition checked the following versions. None is used as an unproved model premise. Read ranges and deterministic HTML views are bound in the evidence.
1594: * Jacobsen–Le Doussal–Picco–Santachiara–Wiese, [0809.3985v1](https://arxiv.org/html/0809.3985v1), September 23, 2008, was read through its ending. Its replica/Coulomb-gas calculation says the curve-detecting operator has no first-order disorder correction and computes a second-order correction; its numerical work concerns q=3. This is relevant evidence for testing cancellation, not a rigorous fixed-q=2, fixed-disorder discrete occupation theorem. The calculation distinguishes moments of random correlations. We import neither its operator identification nor a lattice error estimate from its perturbation theory. In particular its first-order cancellation does not evaluate M6–M8 on the exact square.
1595: * Avérous–Mahfouf, [2509.08938v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.08938v1), September 10, 2025: the main hypotheses, complete normalized-derivative appendix A.1, and natural-centering Gaussian comparison through the proofs of Lemmas 4.3–4.4 were read. Their stochastic interpolation pays drift and quadratic variation using geometric mixing estimates in shrinking windows. This supplies a serious comparison for P6–P7's mechanism, but not the fixed-disorder marked influence budget. The torus setup and parameter centering cannot silently replace the prescribed square or physical critical tuning. Displayed external formulas are not algebraic premises of M/P, which were derived independently.
1596: * Holden–Li–Sun, [1804.07286v3](https://arxiv.org/html/1804.07286v3), March 29, 2021: the complete §3 measure-identification operation was read through its ending. It uses local two-arm calibration, near/off-diagonal estimates, conditional couplings, and a separate treatment of non-Jordan domains. It is a precedent for same-sample occupation replacement in percolation, not a disordered FK normalization theorem. The coupling and moment work cannot be replaced by two marginal limits.
1597: The raw current metadata and versioned bodies are retained, including HTML conversion conventions; acquisition succeeded for these six requests. Earlier search truncations and recovered reading ranges are recorded separately. No PDF re-extraction was required. These searches are not an exhaustive literature or priority determination. The external perturbative cancellation is a reason **not** to claim a logarithmic obstruction from the negative mean-log term alone.
1598: The contribution assessment is separate from the proof audit. R126 is a standard marked-law replacement principle made precise for normalized quenched laws. E126 is an elementary curvature/entropy comparison, and E3 is a valid technical transfer in a very small shrinking window. The complete pure lower operations already supply their microscopic geometric input. M129, M129-local and P129 are exact finite-response, truncation, and posterior calculations; the actual two-edge square example makes the posterior issue concrete. They are paid technical partials, not a substantial fixed-disorder theorem and not a new continuum construction. R129-cutoff is only a precise version of the intended cutoff bound.
1599: For G the remaining obligation can be stated without altering any model choices: produce one deterministic positive finite calibration and prove normalized replacement R, or its explicit interior/boundary sufficient estimates. Route one additionally identifies the specific local coefficient that must be evaluated: the whole order-\(v_k\) marked response after common-sheet transport and all normalizers, with uniform summable relative errors. Route two identifies the missing control of the same mark's influence or replacement under the actual posterior P1. Neither a bulk energy charge, a vanishing macroscopic comparison, nor a continuum Hausdorff gauge resolves these obligations.
1600: **Recommendation to the parent:** retain the no-repair passes itemized in §2, record the narrow R2 cutoff formulation issue explicitly, preserve I123 and the inherited I120/I117/I114 scopes, and keep thermal Target B parked. Park fixed-disorder G as unproved/unrefuted after these two attempts. Reopen only for a marked microscopic comparison with a paid normalization/tail budget, or an actual typical-mass obstruction. Do not nominate a logarithmically renormalized theorem, an expectation-only statement, SAW, or a map side target from this report.

1715: ## 1. Original I129 audit, without repairs
1716: Before this audit, all decoded fields of the required I129 task (SHA-256 `96547471b969b143cc84a80c6efdc12897315973c2ab322c4ae5ee18cbdd9147`) and its mandatory recursive I126/I123/I120 tasks were frozen and read, including their 902/750/602/1011-line briefs, reports, finalized parent responses and objections. Raw original reports and the I129 parent receipt are frozen separately. Truncated displays and recovery ranges are recorded; byte retention alone is not semantic reading.
1717: **R129-cutoff: PASS without repairs.** For positive finite measures on the closed square and the explicitly required `0<=chi<=1`, the decomposition into `chi f` and `(1-chi)f` gives the stated nonnegative boundary budget. The first J metric weights total `1-2^-J<=1`, the remaining weights total `2^-J`, and Cauchy–Schwarz gives each interior L2 term. This is valid under every separately normalized conditional law for which the displayed moments exist, and then after averaging; it is not a proof of those moments under disorder. The order remains mesh, tube radius, boundary strip, finite-test cutoff. Preserve **literal endpoint-only R2 FAIL**: chi(z)=2 and lambda=lambda'=delta_z give boundary budget -2 and a negative asserted upper bound. The usual meaning of “cutoff” may include [0,1], in which case the intended algebra is valid; that convention does not erase the strict-statement distinction.
1718: **Actual trajectory and summability: PASS without repairs.** In the source's actual chosen trajectory, let `a=C2`, `b=c` and choose its small initial variance with `a v0<=1/2`. Monotonicity and `v_(k+1)>=v_k(1-a v0)` imply
1719: `b <= 1/v_(k+1)-1/v_k <= a/(1-a v0) <= 2a`.
1720: Thus `1/(v0^-1+2ak)<=v_k<=1/(v0^-1+bk)`. This proves the asserted comparability with constants depending only on the fixed trajectory parameters, including positivity; it is not an ODE substitution. Harmonic and square-root sums diverge, whereas `sum v_k^(3/2)<=v0^(3/2)+2 sqrt(v0)/b`. The fixed localization error does not become a summable error merely because its constant can be made small. The source trajectory section was read through its ending.
1721: **M1–M4: PASS without repairs.** For a fixed positive-probability configuration event H, put `g(s)=log E(exp(s.X)|H)-log E exp(s.X)`. Finite differentiation gives the difference of means and variances in M1. At zero, `g_i=d_i`, `g_ii=(1-2p_i)d_i-d_i^2`; the relative-probability second derivative is `g_ii+g_i^2=(1-2p_i)d_i`. Independent fair signs eliminate cross-coordinate quadratic terms and global sign reversal eliminates every odd degree. Squaring the log expansion and subtracting its squared mean proves M4, with quadratic coefficient `sum d_i^2`. This verifies each normalization and sign, including the negative square in the mean log and its absence from the mean probability.
1722: Here is a uniform fixed-volume remainder check. For `Y=xi.X`, its range has length at most M under both reference laws. The first four derivatives of each log moment-generating function are cumulants and have bounds `C_j M^j` independent of P(H). Also `|g(t)|<=2M|t|`. On `M|t|<=1`, the fourth derivatives of `exp(g(t))`, `g(t)` and `g(t)^2` are bounded by `C M^4`; averaging their even Taylor expansions yields the claimed remainder in M2–M4. The square of the mean in M4 is also `O(M^4 t^4)`. The constants are universal, the small parameter is M theta, and no mesh-uniform fixed-disorder expansion follows. The identities hold with a deterministic fixed conditional reference and fixed event/mark; using random history-conditioned signs as independent signs would change their hypotheses. Balanced plane marginals cancel the M2 coefficient on a fixed finite perturbation set; mixed finite-square marginals cannot be set to one half by assertion. The mean-log coefficient is not a mass exponent.
1723: **M5: PASS without repairs.** Differentiate using the physical-bond variables `Y_b=sum_(copies of b) X_e`. The same cumulant argument gives the displayed variance difference plus squared mean difference. Terms `2 Cov(X_e,X_f)` for two occurrences of the same bond remain inside `Var Y_b`. Independent-sign averaging removes cross terms between different physical bonds only. The Bernoulli simplification does not apply to a multi-occurrence Y_b. Bounds for a cover must count the total occurrences, rather than silently retaining M as the number of independent signs.
1724: **M6: PASS without repairs.** Write `K=||F||_infinity` and `g(t)=log mu_h e^(tF)-log mu e^(tF)`. Every tilted third centered moment has absolute value at most `8K^3`, so `|g'''|<=16K^3`. Taylor's integral remainder costs at most `8K^3/3`; Lyapunov gives `E K^3<=X^3`. No exponential moment or positive lower bound on p is required. This pathwise argument also works for a random reference and mark satisfying the stated almost-sure conditions, with the moment bound taken under their actual joint law. An unconditional moment hypothesis does not assert the same constant after arbitrary further conditioning.
1725: **M7: PASS without repairs.** On K<=1 the quotient is `mu_h e^F/mu e^F`; differentiating at zero gives linear term `Delta_h F` and quadratic term `(mu_h F^2-mu F^2)/2-(mu F)Delta_h F`. Its third derivative along tF is at most `C K^3` for 0<=t<=1, since both tilted expectations are probabilities and the ratio is at most e^2 there. On K>1 the actual quotient is at most 1/p, and the degree-at-most-two polynomial has magnitude at most `C(1+K+K^2)`. Consequently the error is at most `C X^3+C X^40/p` for deterministic p, as stated. For a random p the actual bound is `C X^3+C E[1_(K>1)/p]`; no independence permits replacing this by `X^40/Ep`. The latter expectation can be infinite, in which case the displayed bound gives no finite control. For a fixed bounded stencil with source 40th-moment norm `C sqrt(v_k)`, M6 has a summable remainder; M7 additionally requires the stated lower bound on p for the claimed scale summability. A posterior or growing stencil needs its own moment budget. None of these facts cancels the retained order-v terms.
1726: **M8: PASS without repairs.** With `y=chi_(U^c)`, the tower property gives `mu_0(h e^F)=mu_(0,out)(h'(y)e^(F'(y)))` and `mu_0(e^F)=mu_(0,out)e^(F'(y))`. Positivity of the denominator and `0<=h<=1` give `0<=h'<=1`. The identity holds for each fixed environment and every supported exterior state, and iterates when all normalizers, connectivity states and distinguished selectors are retained. It proves neither locality nor a pure next-scale indicator. Conditioning on transported marks is not energy-charge projection.
1727: **P1–P2: PASS without repairs.** Bayes under `Q(dxi,dw)=pi(dxi)phi_xi(dw)` gives the posterior with full original normalization. On common finite support, a configuration event of positive joint probability has positive probability under every environment. Holding the other signs fixed, `dphi_+/dphi_-=(1+tX_i)/(1+t p_i^-)` with `t=e^(2theta)-1>=0` gives P2 exactly. The logarithm is bounded in absolute value by 2theta; its derivative in either Bernoulli mean has magnitude at most t, giving the sharper bound. These are configuration-only events, including specified histories/hits. If an additional observation directly reveals environmental signs, P1 with phi_xi(A) for a configuration-only A is not that posterior. The original makes no such extension. On a cover the sufficient statistic is Y_i, so the Bernoulli identity cannot be reused.
1728: **P3: PASS without repairs, for every theta>0.** Contracting precisely the wired three vertices gives two parallel sampled edges and weights `2,u,v,uv`. For the event X1=1, differentiation gives `partial_v L=u/(2+u+v+uv)^2>0`. Since `v_+>v_-`, averaging over either positive first-sign prior weight strictly biases the second sign upwards. More explicitly the posterior odds of the second sign given u are
1729: `[(1+v_+)/(1+v_-)] [(2+v_-+u(1+v_-))/(2+v_++u(1+v_+))]`.
1730: The derivative of the nonconstant quotient has numerator `v_--v_+<0`, with a positive squared denominator. Distinct u values therefore yield distinct conditional odds, proving posterior dependence, not merely unequal marginals. At theta=0 the strict effects disappear; that endpoint was not asserted. The all-positive-odds calculation is valid for every theta>0; its physical application is restricted to the physical family's admissible epsilon values.
1731: **P4–P5: PASS without repairs.** `phi_xi(A)<=1` proves the entropy bound `log(1/Q(A))`. For a prefix of exposed environmental signs under this posterior, average the two likelihoods over the same unexposed fair prior. The pointwise likelihood ratio lies in `[e^(-2theta),e^(2theta)]`, so the quotient of sums lies there too. This does not assert posterior independence. For `q_l=e^l/(1+e^l)`, the divergence from the fair bit has derivative `l q_l(1-q_l)`; integrating from zero, also for negative l with the reversed integration direction, yields `D<=l^2/8<=theta^2/2`. The chain rule proves the factor M, and hence the minimum in P5. These bounds may be volume-sized and do not pay a rare-hit comparison.
1732: **P6–P7: PASS without repairs.** Direct normalization gives `P_(t e_i)F-PF=(e^t-1)Cov(F,X_i)/(1+p(e^t-1))`. Its symmetric average simplifies to the displayed b, including the denominator `1+2p(1-p)(cosh(theta)-1)`. This holds at p=0,1 as well (covariance zero), for any bounded real F and arbitrary coordinate dependence. Successive independent signs introduced before sampling give the exact telescoping in P7. For 0<=F<=1, `Cov(F,X_i)=p(1-p)(E(F|X_i=1)-E(F|X_i=0))` has absolute value at most 1/4; thus the crude volume constant is exactly `M(cosh(theta)-1)/4`. The actual readout is bounded although its counting mass is not uniformly bounded in n, and retains the same configuration's curve and whole-plane tube. This is a bounded metric comparison, not a comparison of unbounded mass expectations. Reapplying the sign average under P1 would be invalid and is not done in the original.
1733: **Independent complete-operation assessment.** These are finite log-partition differentiation, even-sign Taylor calculus, a 40th-moment truncation, conditional expectation, Bayes/entropy chain rules, and exact coordinate replacement. Each operation above is fully paid at its original scope. The pure occupation input supplies microscopic calibration, outward distinguished-strand coupling and boundary/near-pair control in the pure model; the disorder operations supply the unmarked flow and fixed-scale curve comparison. The new finite operations do not supply the transported marked scale coefficient or the calibrated posterior influence estimate. Their contribution remains technical. The marked order-v coefficient is **not evaluated**; neither logarithmic renormalization nor typical-mass failure is proved. G remains unproved/unrefuted and parked.
1734: Preserve the I126 no-repair passes for R126, R1, finite-measure topology, E126 and deterministic shifts, and E3 on its exact square. Joint-law transfer uses `n theta_n->0`; annealed expectation/UI transfer has the separate stronger sufficient rate `theta_n n^(4/3)->0`, not a necessary condition or consequence of weak convergence. Preserve I123 A1, its exact-entropy finite-energy heavy tail and persistence at every finite OU time, and B1–B5. These are inherited scopes, not fresh thermal proof audits. All earlier literal failures, including the external Cuesta scalar-factor issue, remain in the frozen recursive context; no weighted-Q, ordinary-R, arbitrary-state stationarity, bounded inverse, or universal finite-energy differentiability claim is restored.
1735: ## 3. Fresh discrete-probability screen and prior overlap
1736: The three candidates below use different probability spaces. None uses active I130/I131 work.
