1737: ### Candidate S: fluctuations of the actual SAT hitting time
1738: For each fixed k>=3, let C_1,C_2,... be iid uniform among the `2^k binom(n,k)` clauses on **distinct variables**, with independent fair literal signs and whole clauses sampled **with replacement**. Put `H_n=inf{m>=1: conjunction(C_1,...,C_m) is unsatisfiable}`. A precise stronger candidate is
1739: `(H_n-E H_n)/sqrt(Var H_n) => N(0,1)`.
1740: This uses the uncapped first unsatisfiable prefix and its finite-n mean and variance. It asserts neither a local limit nor convergence of moments, and does not replace `E H_n` by `alpha_k n`. Even a proof of this candidate would require another estimate for that replacement at scale sqrt(n).
1741: The strongest source includes the October 5 **Linear Variance of the Random 3-SAT Hitting Time**, not just the September k-SAT paper's older n log n bound. For every fixed k>=3 the combined sources already give Var(H_n)=Theta_k(n), with corresponding cap statements in their specified ranges. For k=3 the complete proof uses the two-clause killing probabilities q_d(S), the potential `P_d=sum_(j<d) q_j`, and the drift of its 3/2 power. The root exposure keeps two distinct filtrations: the ordinary clauses determine the background solution set while root-true tests remain hidden. Three disjoint tests give a factor p^3; one collision leaves p^2. Integrated drift bounds the conditional duration, and the three collision classes give squared-delay bounds `98d^3`, `14L d^(3/2)` and `L^2`. Incidence moments and the O(1/n), O(1/n^2) collision probabilities pay a uniform squared omission delay. Efron–Stein then gives the upper variance. The cap at 10n has exponentially small second-moment loss; the Wilson quantile argument retains integer atoms and gives the lower bound. These are complete second-moment operations, not a distributional martingale central limit theorem.
1742: The limiting-threshold proof also already performs the auxiliary Poisson interpolation. Repeated variables are allowed only in that auxiliary model; thinning back to proper clauses costs the explicit probability of no internally repeated clause. Convexity of the forced-variable proportions gives the product comparison. A balanced tree sums the one-sided finite-size comparison errors and proves convergence of centers. The computability paper already combines lower finite-size certificates with finite rational hierarchical pressure trials, semidecides strict negativity using rational intervals, and dovetails both certificate lists until the interval is short. It supplies no efficiency estimate. Repackaging these operations as a coupling, threshold algorithm, or effective convergence rate would not be a new result.
1743: Gaia Carenini's priority for threshold existence is preserved. Her [TR26-229, October 5, 2026](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/229/) treats the same proper with-replacement clause model; its polynomial window and terminal-lifetime questions were read directly. Its Question 5.1's square-root central window is already paid by the stipulated linear-variance result, but that does not answer Question 5.2 about the terminal expected continuation time. Her proof explicitly distinguishes those implications. This external paper is comparison evidence, not an additional stipulated premise.
1744: **Decision:** the CLT candidate remains unresolved and is not selected. Neither the variance bound nor fixed-level quantile separation controls its standardized shape. In the actual prefix coupling, satisfiability indicators at different densities are nested: if m<=l then their covariance is exactly `P_n(l)(1-P_n(m))`. Thus a proposed independent Gaussian noise at two central densities would already be incompatible with this readout. There is no new multi-density theorem in merely writing this covariance. A useful CLT route would need quantitative conditional quadratic-variation and large-increment control for the actual deletion/exposure process, not a name for an unavailable lemma. No such bounds are claimed here.
1745: ### Candidate T: retained observations for dependent permutation products
1746: For n=2^d and b=8 fixed blocks, a precise test candidate is: suppose random permutations X,Y each have exactly uniform complementary image marginals, have zero sign mean, and their joint law admits these same marginal guarantees after deleting o(1) mass; must XY tend to uniform? **This candidate is false even with no deletion:** take X uniform on S_n and Y=X^{-1}. Every individual partial observation is exactly uniform, both sign means vanish for n>=2, yet XY is the identity and its TV distance from uniform is `1-1/n!`. This is an exact permutation-model obstruction, not an objection to independent convolution or to the stipulated shuffle. Revealing X then determines Y; marginal caps cannot substitute for conditional caps.
1747: A viable stronger target would require a specific limited-observation mechanism, for example a stopped transcript of a physical shuffle that retains sufficiently many fresh independent switches and controls the remaining full-law Fourier coefficients conditional on that transcript. No uniform theorem for arbitrary transcripts is possible: a transcript containing the complete switch array determines the permutation. Even observing parity leaves a TV obstruction of 1/2 to uniform S_n, so the conditional reference law must specify parity. These tests are why no generic retained-observation theorem is nominated.
1748: The complete **Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle** proof already gives full-deck worst-start convergence after 1600d **physical shuffles**, and the support lower bound `1-2^(tn/2)/n!` gives `t_mix>=2d-O(1)`. It is not only a partial-deck statement. Its path-cylinder argument identifies exactly the unvisited independent coins. A deterministic sweep kills the centered mean, leaving the last sweep's orthogonal noises with weights 2^-j. Independence between opposite halves is used only after the correct earlier conditioning time. Uniform-list hypergeometric balance is unconditional, and the bounded energy pays its exceptional event. This gives the averaged 7n/8 marginal bound; one common trimming, orbit dimensions that retain arbitrary multiplicity, degree summation, and a separate sign argument complete the full law.
1749: The **random-coordinate frames** companion is stronger than its first 32800d application: its introduction explicitly includes a uniform fixed-list bound at 256d, a 512d consequence with the Fourier companion, stopped/trajectory retention, and repairs whose retained coordinates may be dependent. Fixed-schedule frame maps can use future directions, while the probabilistic flow uses a separately generated chronological observation field. Conflating these fields would invalidate conditional independence. The **partial-permutation-to-Fourier** companion already proves common trimming, random-partition retention, Hilbert–Schmidt isotypic estimates, joint-type bounds and two-sided multiplication with full multiplicity. In particular its independent two-factor completion is already a full operation, including mass loss and sign. No constants change or generic product-of-two-laws consequence is claimed here.
1750: The **conditional-permutations in a revealed switching environment** companion already goes further: after `40000(400+1)` sweeps, the expected conditional TV distance of the entire remaining half-deck slot permutation from uniform tends to zero, uniformly over deterministic layouts, after revealing the outside half-deck's complete paths. Its complete preparation/active proof and reset alternative were read through their endings. The path event is an exact coin cylinder. Preparation trims its canonical increment to domination by a uniform free set and an independent uniform slot permutation. Active retention is invariant under a last free-switch parity flip. Two copies pay independent coin agreements at shared endpoints; character bounds give a normalized trace bound, and preparation domination upgrades it to a positive-operator bound that iterates under the actual evolving outside history. The final missing mass is averaged under the unweighted path law. The reset construction instead uses forward minorization obtained from both forward and reverse marginal estimates; it keeps the actual forward shuffle and marks an exact uniform component. The relative increment becomes central only under that accepted construction. A conditional two-copy collision identity, with separate good marks, then gives uniformity even with reset coins revealed. This is a specific retained-observation/dependence mechanism already paid by the source, not a new corollary available for nomination. Neither conclusion is uniform over every fixed rare environment.
1751: Morris's [2009 primary paper, 0912.2759v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/0912.2759v1) concerns the dyadic full-deck model and the earlier cubic-log upper bound. That historical upper bound is superseded under our stipulated source. It is not a current open target here. Arbitrary even deck sizes, a sharp leading constant and cutoff require distinct work; we do not infer any from a cube frame or from repeated steps that are not independent blocks. **Decision:** park this candidate after the exact dependence/parity obstructions and the companion overlap check.
1752: ### Candidate M: conditional structure beyond the symmetric singularity rate — selected
1753: Fix p in (0,1), once and for all; q=1-p. Let A_n be real symmetric with independent signs on and above the diagonal, each +1 with probability p. Define
1754: `Q=p^2+q^2, c3=p^3+q^3, E_n={some two rows are equal or opposite}`,
1755: `L_n=binom(n,2)[c3 Q^(n-2)+pq(2pq)^(n-2)]`.
1756: The strongest full target tested is
1757: `P(det A_n=0, E_n^c)=o(L_n)`.                                      (M-full)
1758: It would imply `P(det A_n=0)~L_n` and, using the conditional result proved below, that given singularity there is a unique proportional pair and corank one with probability tending to one. For p=1/2, `L_n=n(n-1)2^-n`; the nonsymmetric iid prefactor `(2+o(1))n^2 2^-n` is different. For p!=1/2 the equal-pair term dominates exponentially. Nothing is uniform for p=p_n approaching zero or one.
1759: The stipulated unbiased source proves `2^(-n+o(n))`; the biased source proves `Q^(n+o(n))`. These rates do not imply M-full or a leading prefactor. The two routes below derive a conditional sparse-structure theorem and an exact planted-law transfer. They do not close M-full.
1760: ## 4. Complete-operation comparison for the selected source
1761: The biased paper was read through the endings of all six proof sections. Its reduction first separates very large corank using forced Schur blocks, then flips selected diagonal entries to reach corank one with `2^o(n)` multiplicity and likelihood cost. This is an exponential-rate reduction; it does not classify the original matrix conditionally on singularity. A conditional sign-to-bit congruence keeps independent upper entries with success probabilities p or q and a mean of rank at most two. Its tail-compressible net pays a bound at the leading rate Q with an arbitrary fixed exponent slack, not a strictly smaller rate after deleting exact proportional pairs.
1762: The lower proof does considerably more than a least-singular-value headline. Uniform prime-corank control and a low-height subspace cover support a simultaneous statement for **all** large-index intermediate lattices between BZ^m and Z^m. Saturated determinant minimization produces a lattice with controlled short projected points. Dissociated coordinate subsets supply separated residue sums. An anisotropic dual Gaussian produces several Fourier testers at once; fixed small cells remove their dependence on the matrix before independent cross-block rows are tested. This pays the net entropy and gives the small cube-intersection estimate even for a lattice subsequently selected by B.
1763: For admissible columns, the discriminant pairing is restricted to the subgroup generated by differences of one color, not to the whole discriminant group. Height-two layers either force a large-index subgroup, or have subexponential color cost. Robust extraction, the graph lattice `(z,B^-1 z)`, its rational pairing determinant, low-height inverse-form rank and the small-spectrum bound then control the column set when W_2 is small. The graph metric gives eigenvalues `x/(1+x^2)` and handles the indefinite form without asserting positivity.
1764: The final path argument retains layers in successive quotient groups. It uses one- or two-coordinate principal deletions; the two-coordinate move has a genuine two-column gate. Selected retained layers constrain a fresh column to a large-index lattice. A weaker dissociation bound handles high layers, its exponential moment is multiplied under forward exposure, and the terminal window forces **two** costly constraints or a two-column gate. Only after this fixed-path calculation is there a union over the `2^o(n)` paths. Backwards path selection is not treated as an independent conditional experiment. Thus a bad admissible-column count has cost approximately `r^(2n)`, r=max(p,q), and the ordinary-column entropy gives another strict margin. The compressible sector remains at Q. These already paid operations are the correct baseline for significance.
1765: The unbiased framework uses exact sign-to-bit Schur reduction, an expected admissible-column count, and a discriminant potential with special height-two weight 7/5. Its corank reduction likewise loses `2^o(n)`. The full minor-extension algebra was also read: gated two-coordinate deletion retains two admissible columns, retained layers force an explicitly indexed intermediate lattice, and a finite-module annihilator argument pays a double loss in the symmetric kernel inequality. The static/potential and minor-path operations are stipulated inputs at their exact scopes; they do not give a polynomially precise count of singular configurations. In particular a bound with `2^o(n)` loss is too coarse to settle an `n^2 2^-n` prefactor merely by dividing probabilities.
1766: The new [Hu–Song–Wu, 2609.39463v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39463v1), posted September 30, 2026, proves a leading asymptotic for **all-independent-entry** matrices, with a polynomial remainder. [Jain–Sah–Sawhney, 2010.06554v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.06554v2) gives the analogous structure for nonuniform finite-support iid entries. Their input model is essential. We do not condition an iid matrix on symmetry and divide by an event of probability exponentially small in n^2.
1767: For a real problem comparison, [Sahasrabudhe's 2026 ICM survey, 2512.15077v1, §4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15077v1) discusses the symmetric singularity problem and the conjectural dominance of row/column coincidences at the smallest scales. Its older statement that the nonsymmetric Rademacher prefactor is open is superseded by Hu–Song–Wu; its symmetric exponential-rate question is superseded by the stipulated source. M-full is the remaining finer structural/prefactor question, not a renamed solution of either already-paid rate theorem. This bounded literature comparison supports motivation, not a worldwide novelty claim.
1768: ## 5. Route I — expose a minimal kernel support before testing fresh rows
1769: This route proves an exponential separation for exact small supports. It deliberately does not turn an approximate sparse-vector net into an exact-support statement or conversely.
1770: **Lemma S3 (three nonzero coefficients).** Put r=max(p,q), s=min(p,q), and
1771: `a_p=max{3r^2s, r^3+2rs^2}`.
1772: For independent p-signs X_1,X_2,X_3, every three nonzero real coefficients a,b,c and every real t satisfy
1773: `P(a X_1+b X_2+c X_3=t)<=a_p<Q`.
1774: Proof. The level plane contains no cube edge because no coefficient is zero. Distinct vertices in it therefore have Hamming distance at least two. An antipodal pair cannot share this plane with a third vertex: after orienting one vertex to (1,1,1), their plane has level zero; a third vertex would force the sum on the coordinates on which it differs to vanish, and the complementary sum too, forcing a singleton coefficient to vanish. If there are three vertices, their pairwise distances are two. After a cube symmetry they are three vertices in one parity class. Their equations force equal absolute coefficients, and the fourth vertex of that parity class is not in the plane. Hence there are at most three vertices. A three-vertex set of pairwise distance two has total probability one of
1775: `3r^2s, 3rs^2, r^3+2rs^2, s^3+2r^2s`.
1776: The first two are at most 3r^2s; the last is at most r^3+2rs^2 because their difference is `(r-s)(r^2-rs+s^2)>=0`. For a two-vertex set at distance two or three, its weight is at most `max(r^3+rs^2,r^3+s^3,2r^2s)`, again at most a_p; one vertex is also covered. Finally `3r^2s<=4/9<1/2<=Q`, and
1777: `Q-(r^3+2rs^2)=s(r^2-rs+s^2)>0`.
1778: This proves the strict gap for every fixed p. Conditioning on any other independent signs proves the same bound for a linear form with at least three nonzero coefficients. ∎
1779: A **circuit support** is an inclusion-minimal nonempty support of a vector in ker A_n. For a specified support S of size k, circuit minimality implies that the n-by-k matrix A_[n],S has rank exactly k-1: two independent null vectors would have a nonzero linear combination with a canceled coordinate and smaller support. Select k-1 independent rows I; union over at most `binom(n,k-1)` choices. Expose all entries in A_I,S, respecting the symmetry by exposing each upper-triangular variable only once. If this exposed matrix has a one-dimensional nullspace with every coordinate nonzero, choose a canonical normal v (for example the first coordinate normalized to one); otherwise this choice of I cannot witness the circuit.
1780: For j outside S union I the entries A_j,S are mutually independent p-signs. They are also independent of the exposed variables: an equality of the unordered index pairs would force j into S union I. Different such rows use disjoint upper-triangular variables. Conditional on the exposure, S3 gives probability at most a_p for each equation A_j,S v=0. The remaining rows and internal symmetric constraints can be dropped for an upper bound. Thus for `3<=k<=n/2`,
1781: `P(some circuit of size k)<=binom(n,k)binom(n,k-1) a_p^(n-2k+1)`.       (S1)
1782: All uncountably many candidate coefficient vectors have been replaced by the normal of finitely many exposed row sets. No net, independence between full symmetric rows, or independent lower triangle is assumed.
1783: Let h(x)=-x log x-(1-x)log(1-x). Choose a fixed `0<delta_p<1/4` so small that
1784: `2h(delta_p)-2delta_p log a_p <= (1/2)log(Q/a_p)`.
1785: Such a choice exists because the left side tends to zero. Put `kappa_p=(1/2)log(Q/a_p)>0`. The usual binomial entropy bound, for k<=delta_p n, gives from S1
1786: `P(some circuit of size 3,...,floor(delta_p n)) <= n Q^n exp(-kappa_p n)` . (S2)
1787: The factor a_p in S1 only improves this bound. This is an explicit rate after fixing the displayed delta; constants depend on p and are not endpoint-uniform. If the range is empty the probability is zero.
1788: **Exact elementary events.** For one pair i<j,
1789: `P(row_i=row_j)=c3 Q^(n-2)`,
1790: `P(row_i=-row_j)=pq(2pq)^(n-2)` .                                  (S3)
1791: Outside the pair the first event requires equality of two independent signs per coordinate. Its 2-by-2 corner requires `A_ii=A_ij=A_jj`, giving c3. For the second, the corner requires `A_ii=A_jj=-A_ij`, giving `p^2q+pq^2=pq`, and every external coordinate gives 2pq. This pays the often-missed diagonal/shared-entry constraint.
1792: Let `lambda_p=max(c3,Q^2)<Q`. Two different signed pair events on the same pair are disjoint. For two pairs sharing one vertex, each outside coordinate must satisfy a prescribed signed relation between three signs, whose probability is at most c3; for two disjoint pairs the four independent entries give at most Q^2. Ignoring the remaining corner constraints, each intersection is at most `lambda_p^(n-4)` for n>=4. There are fewer than 2n^4 unordered pairs of signed events. Bonferroni therefore proves the finite bound
1793: `0<=L_n-P(E_n)<=2n^4 lambda_p^(n-4)` .                              (S4)
1794: In particular `P(E_n)=L_n(1+O_p(n^2 (lambda_p/Q)^n))`, and the probability of two distinct pair relations, conditional on E_n, has the same exponential upper bound. This is a bound on intersections, not an independence or Poisson-law assertion.
1795: Let S_n(delta) be the event that ker A_n has a nonzero vector supported on at most floor(delta n) coordinates. A support of size one is impossible because all entries are nonzero. A two-coordinate relation forces its two coefficients to have equal absolute value, hence gives exactly E_n. Every vector has a minimal circuit inside its support. Combining S2–S4, for all sufficiently large n,
1796: `P(S_n(delta_p))=L_n(1+O_p(n^2 e^(-c_p n)))`,
1797: `P(E_n^c | S_n(delta_p))=O_p(e^(-kappa_p n)/n)` .                   (S5)
1798: Here c_p can be any smaller positive constant than both kappa_p and log(Q/lambda_p), with a changed finite multiplicative constant. This is a conditional structural classification under an **actual sparse-kernel event**, not under all singularity. It is a useful quantitative partial, proved without using the source's singularity upper bound.
1799: **Why this route does not close M-full.** Increasing k to a macroscopic fraction consumes the exponent in the two support/row-selection binomial costs. For k near n there may be no fresh rows at all. The source's tail-compressible estimate also allows a fixed exponent slack at the leading rate Q, and its approximate vectors cannot be replaced by the exact circuits in S2. Neither bound eliminates singular matrices whose every circuit is larger than delta_p n. The full target needs a new bound on that remaining sector of size `o(n^2 Q^n)`, not just a further assertion of Var or rank concentration.
1800: ## 6. Route II — compute the planted conditional quotient and pay its changed law
1801: This route starts on a rare event but derives its exact conditional distribution instead of assuming the unconditioned theorem is uniform in a planted row or diagonal.
1802: Condition on `E^+_12={row_1=row_2}`. Delete the second row and column to obtain B of order n-1, and let R be the n-by-(n-1) matrix that duplicates its first coordinate. Then exactly
1803: `A=R B R^T`, `rank A=rank B`, `corank A=1+corank B`.                (Q1)
1804: The upper-triangular entries of B are independent **under this conditioning**, because the equality event separates into constraints on disjoint original sets of variables. Their laws are
1805: `P(B_11=+1 | E^+_12)=p^3/c3`,
1806: `P(B_1j=+1 | E^+_12)=p^2/Q  (j>=2)`,
1807: `P(B_ij=+1 | E^+_12)=p  (2<=i<=j)` .                             (Q2)
1808: Thus for p=1/2, B is exactly the smaller ordinary symmetric fair-sign matrix. If `s_m(p)=P(A_m is singular)`, the exact identity is
1809: `P(corank A_n>=2 | E^+_12)=s_(n-1)(1/2)`  when p=1/2.             (Q3)
1810: For general fixed p the planted law is **not** an ordinary p-law. Relative to that law its density is bounded, pointwise, by
1811: `C_p (r/Q)^(n-2)`,  `C_p=r^2/c3`,  `r=max(p,q)`.                  (Q4)
1812: Indeed a cross-entry density ratio is p/Q on +1 and q/Q on -1; the corner ratio is p^2/c3 or q^2/c3. Every other coordinate has ratio one. Hence
1813: `P(corank A_n>=2 | E^+_12) <= C_p (r/Q)^(n-2) s_(n-1)(p)` .       (Q5)
1814: Invoking only the stipulated rate `s_m(p)=exp(m log Q+o_p(m))` gives
1815: `P(corank A_n>=2 | E^+_12) <= exp(n log r+o_p(n))`.                (Q6)
1816: This decays exponentially because p is fixed in (0,1). For the fair case Q3 gives the sharper exact source expression. No arbitrary diagonal-change theorem, rank-one-addition theorem, or uniform bound for varying row biases has been imported; Q4 explicitly pays the only changed coordinates.
1817: When p=1/2, a fixed diagonal sign congruence converts E^-_12 to E^+_12 and preserves the original fair law, so Q3 also holds for the opposite-pair event. When p!=1/2 such a congruence does not preserve the p-law. We do not use it: the total probability of opposite-pair events divided by the equal-pair sum is exactly
1818: `[pq/c3] [(2pq)/Q]^(n-2)`,                                      (Q7)
1819: which is exponentially small. Sum Q5 over equal pairs, use S4 for the denominator, and use Q7 (or Q3 at fairness) for opposite pairs. It follows that for some `d_p>0` and all sufficiently large n,
1820: `P(corank A_n!=1 | E_n) <= C'_p exp(-d_p n)` .                    (Q8)
1821: For example at bias one may take any d_p smaller than `min{-log r, log(Q/(2pq))}` after reducing it to absorb the source o(n); at fairness take any d_p<log 2. The threshold in n is not made effective by the source's asymptotic rate.
1822: Two distinct signed pair relations are linearly independent (opposite signs on the same pair are impossible). Thus corank one on E_n implies a unique such pair. Combining Q8 with S5 proves the paid conditional theorem:
1823: **M132-sparse.** For every fixed p in (0,1), there are delta_p,c_p,C_p>0 and N_p such that for every n>=N_p, conditional on S_n(delta_p), with probability at least `1-C_p exp(-c_p n)` the matrix has corank one and a unique pair of equal/opposite rows. Its sparse-event probability has the asymptotic in S5. At p!=1/2 the pair is equal, with an exponentially small conditional exception; at p=1/2 the two signs are symmetric. Constants and N_p depend on p. No conclusion about conditioning on all singularity is included.
1824: **A larger exact planted construction.** In the fair model, fix any partition of [n] into m nonempty classes and any prescribed coordinate signs d_i. Condition on the relations `row_i=d_i d_j row_j` within each class. Apply the deterministic congruence D=diag(d_i). The resulting matrix is constant on every block of the partition. The constraints involve disjoint sets of independent upper-triangular entries; each block's common sign is independently fair, even for a diagonal block. Therefore the quotient B is exactly an m-by-m ordinary symmetric fair-sign matrix, and `A=D R B R^T D` for the class-incidence matrix R. Conditional on the specified relations,
1825: `corank A=n-m+corank B` .                                        (Q9)
1826: In particular, whenever m->infinity, the prescribed relation space is the entire kernel except with probability `2^(-m+o(m))`, uniformly over the fixed partition shapes and the prescribed signs. Uniformity follows from exact equality of the quotient laws, not from a uniform-source perturbation theorem. For fixed m the error is s_m and need not vanish. In the biased unsigned case a block containing w independent original entries has conditional + probability `p^w/(p^w+q^w)`; weights are `w=|C_a||C_b|` off diagonal and `w=|C_a|(|C_a|+1)/2` on diagonal. This quotient generally has many different biases and is not covered by the fixed-p source rate. We claim no extension of Q9's decay to arbitrary biased partitions.
1827: **Countertests and exact remaining operation.** A full conditional rank theorem cannot follow just from Q1: outside E_n there is no duplicate to collapse. Moreover the unrestricted assertion that a planted equality always causes exactly one lost rank is false at finite n—B can itself be singular with the strictly positive probability in Q3. Our exponential error retains that possibility. An arbitrary diagonal modification or p_n approaching an endpoint may destroy the decay in Q4–Q6. These restrictions are necessary to the proof, not technical words hiding a stronger claim.
1828: Q1–Q9 are useful normalized rare-event constructions, but their gain over the complete source is limited: exact conditioning, a quotient map, and an explicitly bounded density convert the source's already-paid smaller-dimensional exponential rate. Route I uses a classical circuit exposure/small-ball union bound. Neither mechanism supplies a new dense-kernel estimate. **The contribution is technical, not a ninth substantial finding.**
1829: ## 7. Disposition and evidence boundary
1830: M-full remains unproved and unrefuted. Retain S1–S5 and Q1–Q9, and M132-sparse, as paid partials at their exact conditioning scopes. They neither prove a Poisson multiplicity law nor infer asymptotic independence from the pair intersection bound. Recommend parking the full prefactor/conditional classification until a proof can bound singularity with no proportional pair by `o(n^2 Q^n)`. Repeating the same support union bound, applying a source theorem to an unverified shifted ensemble, or dividing the rate estimate by L_n will not pay that operation.
1831: The original I129 audit passes without repairs for R129-cutoff, the actual reciprocal flow deduction, M1–M8 and P1–P7, with the stated fixed-volume, random-reference and conditional limitations. The literal endpoint-only R2 failure is preserved separately. I126's passed scopes and stronger UI rate, the inherited thermal statuses, and all recursive literal failures remain unchanged. G, thermal B, pinned A, Dini C and physical P112 remain parked. Eight accepted findings remain eight; no nomination.
1832: ## 8. Immutable evidence and replay
1833: The owned package consists only of this report and `frontier/sources/i132_discrete_probability_discovery/` and `frontier/experiments/i132_discrete_probability_discovery/`. The original I129 report, required task, finalized parent response, recursive I126/I123/I120 contexts, instructions, upstream identity and current ledgers are frozen. Only the four original live ledger paths may drift; every snapshot is immutable. Historical mentions of I130/I131 inside a frozen ledger are not bindings to those active outputs.
1834: The selected inventory includes all 245 source representations for the 18 selected family/companion roots. It refers to the existing complete mapping: 13,223 tree entries, 749 manuscript roots and 10,373 original positions, including 776 inherited PDF-to-text bindings. No corpus is copied. Read manifests distinguish supplied inclusive UTF-8 `splitlines()` ranges, decoded JSON fields, whole-file bytes, raw LF counts, terminal LF status, and hashes of LF-joined ranges without an appended newline. They record actual supplied passages and endings; the failure log separately identifies output truncations, recovery ranges, lead-only searches and the few limited ledger displays. Retaining an unread file is not recorded as reading its whole proof. All six biased-source proof sections, the actual stronger 3-SAT variance proof, the original Thorp completion, and the selected transfer and conditional-retention operations were read through their endings. The report does not claim that every proof in all 18 families was read.
1835: The prior-overlap domain excludes report files numbered 130 and later, including concurrently appearing later work. The prior-overlap and selected-source lexical lists preserve every occurrence with line and character offsets, including repeated TeX/PDF representations. They are bounded search evidence, not a novelty theorem. Versioned external bodies, metadata, retrieval failures and deterministic HTML views are frozen. The only fresh PDF extraction is the Carenini paper; original bytes, Poppler version, exact output, layout/form-feed convention and annotation warnings are bound. Replay verifies those bytes without invoking an extractor. Current metadata and selected primary statements support the literature comparison, not an exhaustive survey or external truth stipulation.
1836: `verify.py` first checks every owned immutable byte, exact globally sorted file and directory inventory, snapshots, immutable references, full decoded recursive coverage, lexical occurrence multisets, read ranges and HTML/PDF bindings. A structural adapter tied to the exact original I129 verifier hash executes only its immutable prefix, which in turn validates the I126 immutable prefix. Neither inherited recursive replay is invoked. It then calls the sealed I123 flat engine **once**, validating the remaining inherited packages, all original positions/Git identities and inherited PDF bindings before its first finite diagnostic. I126 and I129 finite checks subsequently run unchanged, followed by I132's exact rational matrix diagnostics. This order puts **all immutable checks before finite diagnostics**.
1837: Preseal validation checks the final report and programs before one final seal is written. The inventory is explicitly **globally sorted, duplicate-free and exact** across all three owned locations, including the preseal receipt. The offline verifier blocks network access, subprocesses, writes, bytecode, repair and resealing; acquisition and preparation tools are separate and are never called by replay. Run from the research directory:
1838: python3 -B frontier/experiments/i132_discrete_probability_discovery/verify.py
1839: The new diagnostics enumerate symmetric upper-triangular signs at small sizes using exact rational weights. They check the three-coefficient bound on a finite coefficient set, pair probabilities, conditional quotient distributions, exact rank identities, fair opposite-pair congruence and planted partitions. These finite checks do not certify universal proofs, asymptotic estimates, the unpaid all-singularity classification, substantiality, human review or worldwide priority. The analytic deductions, source comparison and limitations are the report's responsibility.

1988: ## 2. Independent original I132 audit, without repairs
1989: Throughout this section, `p` is fixed in `(0,1)`, `q=1-p`, and the entries of the symmetric matrix on **and above** the diagonal are independent p-signs. Put
1990: `r=max(p,q), s=min(p,q), Q=p²+q², c3=p³+q³, a=max(3r²s,r³+2rs²)`.
1991: The original report labels both its three-coefficient lemma and its pair-probability display S3; both are audited below. No enumeration substitutes for the universal arguments.
1992: ### 2.1 Small-ball lemma, circuit exposure and S1/S2
1993: **Three-nonzero-coefficient Lemma S3: PASS without repairs**, for every real target and every three nonzero real coefficients. A level plane meets no cube edge. If it contains antipodal vertices, orient one to `(1,1,1)`. The level is then zero; a third vertex splits the coefficients into two nonempty zero-sum subsets, one a singleton, contradicting nonzero coefficients. Without an antipodal pair, any three vertices have pairwise distance two. They belong to one parity class; their equations force equal absolute coefficients, and exclude that parity class's fourth vertex. Thus there are at most three vertices. Their possible probability sums, after recording the orientation's actual p/q weights, are
1994: `3r²s, 3rs², r³+2rs², s³+2r²s`.
1995: The last is bounded by the third because their difference is `(r-s)(r²-rs+s²)>=0`. Two allowed vertices have weight at most `max(r³+rs²,r³+s³,2r²s)`, also bounded by `a`; singletons are covered. Finally `3r²s<=4/9<1/2<=Q` and
1996: `Q-(r³+2rs²)=s(r²-rs+s²)>0`.
1997: Hence the strict atom bound `a<Q` holds for each fixed bias. Conditioning on all other signs extends it to any linear form having at least three nonzero coefficients. The orientation is a classification of cube vertices, not an assumption that biased signs are invariant under sign changes.
1998: **Circuit minimality and witness exposure: PASS without repairs.** If a size-k support is inclusion-minimal in the kernel, the n-by-k column submatrix has rank exactly k-1. Otherwise two independent null vectors have a nonzero linear combination canceling a coordinate. Choose k-1 independent witness rows I, expose their entries in those columns with every unordered upper-triangular variable exposed once, and normalize the unique null vector by its first nonzero coordinate. It has all coordinates nonzero. This finite witness choice eliminates the apparent uncountable union over normals.
1999: For each row index outside `S union I`, its entries in S use disjoint unordered pairs from the exposure and from every other such fresh row. There are at least `n-2k+1` such rows. This is conditional independence of fresh **cross-block variables**, not independence of whole symmetric rows.
2000: **S1: PASS without repairs.** Dropping all other equations and summing support and witness choices gives, for `3<=k<=n/2`,
2001: `P(a circuit of size k)<=binom(n,k)binom(n,k-1) a^(n-2k+1)`.
2002: **S2 and its constants: PASS without repairs.** Let `h(x)=-x log x-(1-x)log(1-x)` and `kappa=(1/2)log(Q/a)>0`. Choose any fixed `0<delta<1/4` with
2003: `2h(delta)-2delta log a<=kappa`.
2004: Existence follows from convergence of the left side to zero. On `k<=delta n`, entropy bounds for both binomial factors, monotonicity of h on `[0,1/2]`, and `log a<0` bound the exponent by `n(log Q-kappa)`; the remaining factor a only improves the bound. Summing at most n terms gives `n Q^n exp(-kappa n)`. Empty ranges have probability zero. Constants are bias-dependent; no endpoint-uniform delta is asserted.
2005: ### 2.2 Elementary pair events, Bonferroni and S5
2006: **Pair-probability S3: PASS without repairs.** For one pair, equality requires its three independent corner entries to agree, with probability c3, and gives Q per exterior coordinate. Opposition requires `A_ii=A_jj=-A_ij`, with probability `p²q+pq²=pq`, and gives `2pq` per exterior coordinate. Thus the exact probabilities are
2007: `c3 Q^(n-2)` and `pq (2pq)^(n-2)`.
2008: This includes the diagonal/shared corner. The sum of the individual signed-pair probabilities is the original
2009: **S4: PASS without repairs.** Set `lambda=max(c3,Q²)<Q`: indeed `Q-c3=pq>0` and `Q²<Q`. Opposite signs on the same pair are disjoint. When two distinct pairs share one vertex, each exterior coordinate imposes a signed relation on three independent entries, of probability at most c3. Disjoint pairs cost at most Q² per exterior coordinate. Ignoring all corner restrictions bounds either intersection by `lambda^(n-4)` for `n>=4`. Fewer than `2n^4` unordered event pairs suffice, so
2010: `0<=L_n-P(E_n)<=2n^4 lambda^(n-4)`.
2011: Since the equality part alone is bounded below by a positive p-dependent constant times `n² Q^n` for large n, division gives the original relative `O_p(n²(lambda/Q)^n)` error. The same intersection sum bounds multiplicity conditional on E_n. It establishes neither independence nor a Poisson law.
2012: **S5: PASS without repairs.** Support one is impossible; a two-coordinate relation between sign columns forces equal absolute coefficients and hence equal or opposite rows. Every kernel vector contains a circuit. Once `floor(delta n)>=2`, E_n is contained in the actual sparse-kernel event S_n(delta), and its complement within that event is covered by S2. Thus
2013: `P(S_n(delta))=L_n[1+O_p(n² exp(-c n))]`,
2014: `P(E_n^c | S_n(delta))=O_p(exp(-kappa n)/n)`
2015: for any `0<c<min(kappa,log(Q/lambda))`, after enlarging the finite constant and threshold. This verifies the exponents and division by the rare event. It says nothing about conditioning on all singularity.
2016: ### 2.3 Exact planted laws Q1–Q9
2017: **Q1: PASS without repairs.** On a fixed equal-pair event, delete one of its rows and columns. With R duplicating that coordinate, `A=RBR^T`. R has full column rank; a left inverse gives the reverse rank inequality. Therefore `rank A=rank B` and `corank A=1+corank B` exactly.
2018: **Q2: PASS without repairs.** The conditioning event factors into constraints on disjoint original upper-entry sets. Quotient upper entries remain independent, with plus probabilities `p³/c3` at the corner, `p²/Q` in the rest of the first row, and p elsewhere. This is an exact conditional law, not the ordinary p-law except at fairness.
2019: **Q3: PASS without repairs.** At `p=1/2` that law is the ordinary symmetric fair-sign law of order n-1. Thus the conditional extra-corank probability is exactly `s_(n-1)(1/2)`, including all finite-n possibilities of extra rank loss.
2020: **Q4: PASS without repairs.** Relative to the ordinary p-law, each changed cross-coordinate density is `p/Q` or `q/Q`; the corner density is `p²/c3` or `q²/c3`. Hence the pointwise density bound is exactly
2021: `(r²/c3)(r/Q)^(n-2)`.
2022: **Q5: PASS without repairs.** Integrating that density over singular quotients gives the stated bound by this factor times `s_(n-1)(p)`.
2023: **Q6: PASS without repairs.** The stipulated fixed-p rate `s_m(p)=exp(m log Q+o_p(m))` converts Q5 to `exp(n log r+o_p(n))`; all fixed corner factors are absorbed in the remainder. Since r<1 this decays exponentially. There is no effective threshold, varying-bias bound, arbitrary diagonal-change theorem or rank-one perturbation estimate hidden here.
2024: **Q7: PASS without repairs.** At fairness, a deterministic diagonal sign congruence converts a fixed opposite relation to equality and preserves the law. At bias it does not preserve the law. The original instead uses the exact ratio of the total opposite-pair sum to the equality sum,
2025: `(pq/c3)[(2pq)/Q]^(n-2)`.
2026: For fixed `p!=1/2` its base is strictly below one. These two cases must stay separate.
2027: **Q8: PASS without repairs.** Sum Q5 over equal pairs, treat biased opposite pairs by their total probability, and divide by S4's asymptotic denominator. For bias, any positive `d<min(-log r,log(Q/(2pq)))` works after absorbing the source o(n); at fairness any `d<log 2` works. Thus `P(corank A!=1 | E_n)<=C_p exp(-d n)` eventually. Two different signed pair vectors are independent unless they concern the same pair; on that pair the opposite events are disjoint. Corank one therefore implies uniqueness.
2028: **Q9: PASS without repairs.** Fix any partition into m nonempty classes and any deterministic signs d_i. In the fair model, congruence by D preserves the law. The required relations make the conjugated matrix constant on blocks. Different blocks involve disjoint original variables, and the common sign on each block, including a diagonal block, is independently fair. With the class-incidence R,
2029: `A=D R B R^T D`, and `corank A=n-m+corank B`.
2030: The error that the prescribed relation space is not the full kernel is exactly s_m, uniformly over the deterministic partition shapes and signs. The decay `2^(-m+o(m))` requires `m->infinity`; fixed m does not suffice. For an **unsigned biased** partition the plus probability in a block with w original independent variables is `p^w/(p^w+q^w)`, with w equal to the product of class sizes off diagonal and `a(a+1)/2` on an a-class diagonal. This is generally an array of different biases. The original correctly declines to apply its fixed-p source rate to it.
2031: **Complete M132-sparse: PASS without repairs.** For every fixed p in `(0,1)`, choose delta as above and then positive `c_p,C_p` and a sufficiently large `N_p`. For every `n>=N_p`, conditional on the existence of an actual kernel vector supported on at most `floor(delta n)` coordinates, the probability of corank one and a unique proportional pair is at least `1-C_p exp(-c_p n)`. S5 supplies the event's probability. At bias the pair is equal except with an exponentially small conditional exception. At fairness the two signs are balanced by sign congruence, which preserves rank and support sizes. The finite threshold ensures the sparse conditioning event is nonempty and contains E_n. No part asserts the theorem under all-singularity conditioning or uniformly as p approaches 0 or 1.
2032: ### 2.4 SAT and permutations
2033: **Nested SAT-indicator covariance: PASS without repairs.** For the actual proper distinct-variable, fair-literal, iid with-replacement clause stream, let I_m indicate satisfiability after m clauses. For `m<=l`, `I_m I_l=I_l`; therefore `Cov(I_m,I_l)=P_n(l)(1-P_n(m))`. The identity needs only nesting and does not produce a CLT or an effective center.
2034: **Exact permutation dependence and parity obstructions: PASS without repairs.** With X uniform on S_n and Y=X^-1, both individual permutations and all their partial observations have their uniform laws; for `n>=2` each sign mean is zero. Yet XY is the identity and has TV distance `1-1/n!` from uniform. This disproves the stated marginal-only implication for dependent products, not an independent convolution theorem or a particular shuffle. Conditioning a uniform permutation on parity gives TV distance exactly 1/2 from uniform S_n; any law supported on that parity class has distance at least 1/2. A conditional reference must therefore record whether parity was revealed. These are obstructions at the stated model scopes, with no Thorp continuation.
2035: ### 2.5 Independent contribution assessment
2036: The biased source was read through all six proof-section endings, and the fair source's complete principal-extension algebra was read. The comparison is with complete operations, not just the singularity-rate headline. The biased reduction pays diagonal flips and corank reduction with subexponential likelihood/multiplicity losses. Its simultaneous lattice theorem retains all intermediate lattices selected from B: saturated determinant minimization, low-height covering, separated quotient sums and anisotropic dual Gaussian testers precede fixed cells and independent cross-block row testing. Its static estimate restricts the discriminant pairing to differences of one color, uses height-two subgroups or graph-lattice determinants, and controls the indefinite inverse form by eigenvalues `x/(1+x²)`. The terminal-path proof exposes fixed paths forward, retains successive quotient maps, pays a two-column gate, removes high layers by a conditional exponential moment, and forces two costly constraints before unioning over paths. The symmetric kernel inequality's factor two is paid by finite-module annihilator duality, not assumed from independent rows. The compressible sector still retains leading-rate slack.
2037: I132 adds a classical exact-support circuit/small-ball argument and normalized rare-event quotient/density conversion to the already available smaller-dimensional rate. These operations are useful and correctly proved. They supply no new operation at polynomial precision in the remaining dense sector. **Technical contribution; no ninth finding.** M-full stays parked unproved/unrefuted, with no new attempt in this round.
2038: ## 3. Fresh screen and strongest-source comparison
2039: The bounded prior-report lexical domain includes reports through I132 only, plus the analysis scout. The actual scope reads include I02, all relevant I07 passages, I36's endpoint discussion and I37's inherited assessment. I07 already distinguishes a valid inverse W^(1,1) conclusion under positive-Jacobian/inverse-Sobolev hypotheses from unsupported higher inverse integrability. I36 already treats an endpoint/tail analysis as technical when its decay mechanism is inherited. Neither report is a source for a new Brennan endpoint. The few Littlewood lexical hits concern Littlewood–Richardson coefficients or Littlewood–Paley cutoffs, not the present polynomial construction. This bounded search is not a novelty theorem.
2040: ### K: a relative circle-domain construction
2041: A precise first target was to retain a prescribed countable compatible family of finite-energy tests and marked components while constructing a circle-domain uniformization, with test oscillation and tail-energy errors tending to zero along the exhaustion. This is **already a lower operation** in the Koebe source's retained-test theorem. Its entire statement and proof ending were read. It admits compatible well-ordered countable lists; the extension selections preserve finite compatibility at countable limits, and a second-countable bad-pair space makes the selection stop before omega_1. The retained finite sets need not be initial segments. Its core and original-metric neighborhoods, positive oscillation-excess l² budget, whole-component marked disks and fixed-contour 5R control are part of the paid construction. Finite continuous scalar tests are already covered by its corollary. Renaming these as relative uniformization would duplicate the source.
2042: The existence theorem covers arbitrary connectivity. It does not by itself identify all uniformizations up to Möbius maps, furnish a boundary homeomorphism for an arbitrary interior conformal map, or turn area zero into conformal removability. The rigidity companion separately uses a removable-boundary hypothesis. Its final proof normalizes an exterior map as `z+sum alpha_n z^-n`, controls flux errors by the disk-radius product tail, and combines source and target area budgets to force both `f'=1` and corresponding radius differences to vanish. The common point-boundary traces, not an assumed extension of the original map, pay the missing flux. This proves the stated Möbius rigidity under its actual hypothesis; it is not a quantitative stability estimate with a uniform constant over arbitrary domains.
2043: The current primary comparison [Ntalampekos–Younsi, v1, September 30, 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.00560v1) distinguishes rigidity, removability and CNED sufficient conditions, and records limitations on the reverse implication. Its external open-question language is not imported as an unpaid target where our stipulated source already supplies the operation. **Screen disposition:** the precise relative construction is source-paid; no quantitative rate is claimed without geometric rate hypotheses. Not selected.
2044: ### L: compatible real Littlewood coefficients
2045: Let `P_N(z)=sum_(j=0)^(N-1) epsilon_(N,j) z^j` with every coefficient in `{−1,1}`. The strongest stipulated ultraflat source supplies, for every eta>0 and all sufficiently large **integer** N, a choice with
2046: `(1-eta)sqrt(N)<=|P_N(z)|<=(1+eta)sqrt(N)` for every `|z|=1`.
2047: The older all-integer-length maximum companion and the newer positive-minimum companion are weaker than this conclusion but contain substantial lower operations. The ultraflat proof was read completely, including its used packing and defect-rounding inputs in the companion. Equal-modulus oscillatory spreading feeds a finite balanced auxiliary wave system. Disjoint derivative slots, boundary tapers and phase gluing pay both the all-circle modulus and the small exterior Fourier tail. Real symmetry keeps the coefficients real; the final defect-sensitive rounding turns them into signs. The positive-minimum correction's proof ending controls its added waves and l¹ exterior leakage. The older maximum reduction already yields its fixed-Lp/merit-factor consequences. Those are not new targets.
2048: A precise compatibility target is a **single** sign sequence whose every sufficiently long initial polynomial is ultraflat. The per-length choices above do not imply it: the good sets at successive lengths are not known to form a tree with compatible prefixes. Compactness of the product sign space alone does not repair this. The external [Erdélyi paper, v1, April 27, 2025](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19336v1), read through its proof ending, already addresses the even larger unimodular-coefficient class and proves an obstruction to all partial sums being ultraflat. Its proof uses a cited Bernstein-factor lemma and a reversed-polynomial derivative identity. That external result is comparison evidence; no contradiction with independently selected real ultraflat polynomials is asserted.
2049: A weaker precise target is arbitrary prescribed prefixes of length `m_N=o(sqrt(N))`. It follows technically by overwriting signs in a source polynomial: the sup-norm change is at most `2m_N`. Choose source errors tending to zero using its all-N thresholds. This preserves exact length and every sign and gives relative error `eta_N+2m_N/sqrt(N)->0`. It provides no compatible prefixes of macroscopic length, and no single all-length sequence. At `z=±1`, the values are integers congruent to N modulo two; this obstructs some exact finite equalities, not asymptotic flatness of independently chosen polynomials. Products, concatenation and padding are not used to evade length or lower-envelope requirements. **Screen disposition:** the full nested target is already addressed externally; the small prescribed prefix is routine. Not selected.
2050: ### B: a logarithmic upper Brennan endpoint — selected, unresolved
2051: Let S be the normalized schlicht class: f is injective and holomorphic on D, `f(0)=0`, `f'(0)=1`. Set `h_f=1/f'`, use ordinary planar area dA, and write `phi=f^-1` on `W=f(D)`. The precise tested target is
2052: **E_gamma:** for every `gamma>1` there exists `C_gamma<infinity`, independent of `f in S`, such that
2053: `integral_D |h_f|²/[log(e+|h_f|)]^gamma dA <= C_gamma`.
2054: A sufficient stronger target **W2** is a uniform weak-L² estimate `area_D{|h_f|>lambda}<=C lambda^-2` for all f in S and all lambda>=1. Neither is proved here. The inverse-map form of E_gamma is exactly
2055: `integral_W |phi'|^4/[log(e+|phi'|)]^gamma dA <= C_gamma`,
2056: because the Jacobian is `|f'|²`; no original-map/inverse-map exponent is exchanged by analogy. Normalization is essential to a uniform constant. Affine rescaling of the image changes this integral; the statement is not invariant under arbitrary rescaling without the corresponding factors.
2057: The strongest inverse-square source gives **uniform** `M_-2[f'](r)<=C_epsilon(1-r)^(-1-epsilon)` for each epsilon>0, not merely a pointwise spectrum assertion. It also gives `B_S(-2)=1`, the sharp t<=−2 spectrum, and open area range `−2<t<2/3` with arbitrary simply connected image domains, bounded or unbounded. Its entire six-section proof was read. The positive dyadic rerooting operator's spectral radius is at most two. A hypothetical larger radius produces an affine weighted law; area escape and compact superlevels allow a Borel, affine-equivariant saddle-to-maximum pairing. Its target-area transport contradicts the weighted-law Jacobian moment when beta>1. The proof first exhausts boxes with bounded displacement, then removes the displacement cutoff. It gives no polynomial bound on `2^-n||L^n||` at critical radius two and no uniform modulus for the critical escape/pairing limit.
2058: The strict inverse-first-power companion, also read through every proof ending, is stronger than a bounded-univalent-class assertion: it gives a fixed positive improvement over exponent 1/4 for all normalized schlicht maps. Its strict pairing excludes ties; the critical equality case forces a pre-Schwarzian identity, then a harmonic logarithm contradicts a rectangle barrier. This pays a gap at exponent −1, but not the constants needed at −2. Interpolation with the −2 estimate still retains positive endpoint loss.
2059: The current [Jianjun Jin paper, v13, June 5, 2026](https://arxiv.org/html/2512.09330v13), was checked through the rational-univalent integral-means theorem and its complete proof ending. It separates boundary derivative zeros and poles, then integrates their local powers. Thus a rational-function pole calculation alone is not a new substantial mechanism. The routes below explicitly retain this limitation. This is a bounded primary comparison, not an exhaustive endpoint literature survey or an open-problem ranking.
2060: ## 4. Route I — what the full uniform source constants actually buy
2061: This route produces a genuine uniform near-endpoint integral bound while retaining its loss. It does not silently turn an o(log) growth exponent into a logarithmic estimate.
2062: ### 4.1 Uniform subcritical constants and tails
2063: For `0<u<2` put `q=u/2` and `epsilon=(1-q)/(2q)`. Hölder on the circle gives
2064: `M_u[h_f](r)<=C_epsilon^q (1-r)^(-(1+q)/2)`, `1/2<=r<1`.
2065: On `|z|<=1/2`, distortion gives `|h_f(z)|<=(1+|z|)^3/(1-|z|)<=27/4`. Radial integration therefore gives the uniform finite bound
2066: `integral_D |h_f|^u dA <= K(u):=1+(pi/4)(27/4)^u+4pi C_epsilon^q/(1-q)`.
2067: The added 1 is harmless and ensures positivity. Consequently, for `u_j=2-1/j`, `K_j=K(u_j)`, j>=2,
2068: `area_D{|h_f|>lambda} <= inf_(j>=2) K_j lambda^(-2+1/j)`.
2069: Area change also gives the paid inverse-map tail estimate
2070: `area_W{|phi'|>lambda}=integral_{|h_f|>lambda}|h_f|^-2 dA <= inf_j K_j lambda^(-4+1/j)`.
2071: These are uniform subcritical estimates. The infimum is a usable bound with the actual source constants, but no unproved growth law for those constants is inserted.
2072: ### 4.2 A nondecreasing subpower denominator with a complete proof
2073: Choose finite increasing numbers T_j, j>=2, recursively so that
2074: `T_j>=exp(j²)`, `T_j>2T_(j-1)` when j>2, and `T_j>=(2^j K_j)^j`.
2075: For `0<=t<T_2` put `g(t)=1`; on `[T_j,T_(j+1))` put `g(t)=2^j K_j t^(1/j)`. Define the nondecreasing Borel envelope
2076: `Lambda(t)=sup_(0<=v<=t) g(v)`.
2077: It is finite at every finite t and at least one. On the jth band, `2^j K_j<=t^(1/j)`, so `g(t)<=t^(2/j)`. Given eta>0, all sufficiently late bands satisfy `2/j<eta`; the finitely many earlier bands contribute a fixed bound. Hence `Lambda(t)<=max(C_eta,t^eta)` eventually and
2078: `log Lambda(t)/log t -> 0`.
2079: For every f in S, the low band costs at most `pi T_2²`. On the jth band, `Lambda(|h_f|)>=2^j K_j |h_f|^(1/j)`, so
2080: `integral_{T_j<=|h_f|<T_(j+1)} |h_f|²/Lambda(|h_f|) dA <= 2^-j`.
2081: Summing proves the uniform result
2082: **R135-gauge:** `sup_(f in S) integral_D |h_f|²/Lambda(|h_f|) dA <= pi T_2²+1/2`.
2083: Exactly the same constant bounds `integral_W |phi'|^4/Lambda(|phi'|) dA`. The gauge is fixed once the source constants are fixed, independently of f. It is not claimed to be a convex Young function; this is a weighted integral theorem. Lambda can be much larger than every fixed logarithmic power, and this construction does **not** prove E_gamma. This is a technical diagonal summation of the fully uniform source estimates, not a new critical conformal mechanism.
2084: ### 4.3 Why a spectral-radius shortcut does not pay the logarithm
2085: Here is a precise obstruction to one proposed abstract inference, not a counterexample in the conformal class. On the compact space `K=N_0 union {infinity}` with its one-point compactification, let
2086: `w_j=exp(sqrt(j+1)-sqrt(j))`, `(T psi)(j)=w_j psi(j+1)`, `(T psi)(infinity)=psi(infinity)`.
2087: This is a bounded positive operator on C(K), since `w_j->1`. Its n-fold weights telescope to `exp(sqrt(j+n)-sqrt(j))`, maximized at j=0. Thus `||T^n||=exp(sqrt(n))` and its spectral radius is one. For `L=2T`, the radius is two but `2^-n||L^n||=exp(sqrt(n))`, larger than every polynomial in n. Positivity, compactness of the state space and the exact spectral radius therefore do not alone give a logarithmic radial loss. The conformal rerooting operator has additional geometry; this example neither realizes that geometry nor disproves E_gamma. The exact remaining operation in this route is a quantitative estimate for the **actual** critical conformal operator or its transport/escape mechanism, strong enough to replace the arbitrary subpower denominator by the requested logarithmic one.
2088: ## 5. Route II — a finite boundary measure and exact critical-point tails
2089: This route supplies a different, sufficient mechanism with explicit constants, then tests it inside the actual univalent model. It pays a boundary representation instead of assuming it follows from compactness.
2090: ### 5.1 Boundary-measure criterion
2091: Suppose f in S has reciprocal derivative admitting, for z in D, the representation
2092: `h_f(z)=g(z)+integral_(boundary D) (z-zeta)^-1 dmu(zeta)`,
2093: where g is bounded analytic, `||g||_infinity<=B`, and mu is a finite complex Borel measure of total variation M. This is an additional hypothesis, not a theorem for all S.
2094: For any measurable E subset D of area m and any zeta in the plane, layer cake gives
2095: `integral_E |z-zeta|^-1 dA <= integral_0^infinity min(m,pi t^-2) dt =2 sqrt(pi m)`.
2096: Tonelli applied to the positive total-variation measure then yields
2097: `integral_E |h_f| dA <= Bm+2sqrt(pi) M sqrt(m)`.
2098: Take `E={|h_f|>lambda}`. For `lambda>B`, rearranging gives the complete weak-type estimate
2099: **R135-measure:** `area(E)<=4pi M²/(lambda-B)²`.
2100: In particular, for `lambda>=L:=max(2,2B)`, the right side is at most `C lambda^-2`, where `C=16pi M²`. If M=0 the high-level sets are empty. The integral estimate itself proves finiteness of the relevant first moments, so this argument does not assume its conclusion.
2101: Level-set equality at a band endpoint is harmless: a nonconstant analytic h has modulus level sets of planar area zero; a constant h in this normalized setting equals one and lies in the low band. For gamma>1, split into `|h_f|<L` and dyadic bands `[L2^j,L2^(j+1))`. Each high band contributes at most
2102: `4C/[((j+1)log 2)^gamma]`
2103: to the logarithmically weighted square integral. Therefore
2104: `integral_D |h_f|²/[log(e+|h_f|)]^gamma dA <= pi L²+64pi M² (log 2)^(-gamma)[1+1/(gamma-1)]`.
2105: The same bound holds for the inverse-map fourth-power integral by exact area change. Also
2106: `area_W{|phi'|>lambda}<=16pi M² lambda^-4` for `lambda>=L`.
2107: These constants are uniform over any family with common B,M. An individual representation with uncontrolled B,M gives only a one-map estimate. No boundary extension of f is needed for this criterion: all identities are inside D and W.
2108: ### 5.2 A genuine univalent subclass, and its exact tail coefficients
2109: Suppose additionally that f is meromorphic in a neighborhood of the closed disk, holomorphic and univalent in D, and normalized as above. Its derivative has no zeros in D. Any boundary zero of f' is simple: if it had order m>=2 at zeta, the radial size would be `O((1-r)^m)`, contradicting the distortion lower bound `|f'(r zeta)|>=(1-r)/(1+r)^3`. There are finitely many such zeros. Poles of f give zeros of h, not poles. Thus the only poles of h near the closed disk are simple boundary poles zeta_j, with residues
2110: `c_j=1/f''(zeta_j)`.
2111: Subtracting their principal parts gives a g analytic on a neighborhood of the closed disk and therefore bounded there. The criterion applies with `M=sum_j|c_j|` and its actual bound B. Neither parameter has yet been bounded uniformly over this class.
2112: There are exact one-map tail asymptotics:
2113: `lambda² area_D{|h_f|>lambda} -> (pi/2) sum_j |c_j|²`,
2114: `lambda^4 area_W{|phi'|>lambda} -> (pi/4) sum_j |c_j|²`.
2115: For completeness, take disjoint fixed neighborhoods of the finitely many poles. Outside them h is bounded. In one neighborhood write `h=c/(z-zeta)+b(z)` with `|b|<=A`. For lambda>A, the high-level set is sandwiched between its intersections with disks of radii `|c|/(lambda+A)` and `|c|/(lambda-A)`. The area of a small disk centered on the unit circle intersected with D is `(pi/2)rho²+O(rho³)`. This proves the first limit. For the inverse-map limit use
2116: `area_W{|phi'|>lambda}=integral_{|h|>lambda}|h|^-2 dA`.
2117: Uniformly near the pole, `|h|^-2=|z-zeta|²/|c|²[1+O(|z-zeta|)]`. Integrating over the same sandwich gives `(pi/4)|c|² lambda^-4+o(lambda^-4)`. Summing the finitely many disjoint contributions proves both limits. If there are no boundary critical points, h is bounded and both limits are zero. This proof is local and quantitative at each fixed map; uniform asymptotics over a family would require common separation and remainder bounds.
2118: This class includes the Koebe map `f(z)=z/(1-z)²`. At zeta=−1 its reciprocal derivative is
2119: `h(z)=(1-z)^3/(1+z)=-z²+4z-7+8/(1+z)`.
2120: Here `c=8`, so the two tail coefficients are exactly `32pi` and `16pi`. In a sufficiently small interior half-neighborhood of −1, the weighted square integral has the same integrability as
2121: `integral_0^a d rho/[rho (log(1/rho))^gamma]`.
2122: It is finite exactly when gamma>1. Consequently E_gamma is false for gamma<=1 even in the actual normalized schlicht class. This is an exact model-respecting endpoint obstruction, not a finite grid test. It does not settle E_gamma for gamma>1. The Koebe partial-fraction identity also gives the concrete sufficient parameters `B=12, M=8`; their weak-type constant is intentionally nonsharp.
2123: ### 5.3 Remaining operation and contribution
2124: R135-measure would prove the tested uniform endpoint if every f in S admitted the displayed representation with common finite B and M. That is a **sufficient unpaid operation**, not a necessary characterization or a claim that such a representation exists. Normal-family compactness controls h only on compact subsets of D. It supplies no uniform total variation of boundary measures and no uniform norm of the bounded remainder. Approximating f by maps analytic across the circle gives useful individual decompositions but does not bound their B,M; Fatou cannot create a missing common integral bound.
2125: The finite-critical-point asymptotics overlap the elementary boundary-zero/pole integration already present in the current rational-univalent comparison. The finite-measure weak estimate is a direct planar potential estimate. The gauge route is a diagonal use of source constants. Both are proved and usable at their full stated scopes, but neither supplies a new conformal representation or critical quantitative transport theorem. **Technical partials only.** Recommend parking the universal endpoint until one of those concrete missing operations becomes available. No implication for the parked matrix or other branches is claimed.
