2292: ## 2. Independent original I135 audit, unchanged statements
2293: Use normalized schlicht f, h=1/f', W=f(D), phi=f^-1, and ordinary planar area dA. Constants below are the original constants, not replacements.
2294: ### A. Prescribed Littlewood prefixes — PASS without repairs
2295: The source theorem quantifies over every sufficiently large integer N for each fixed error, and permits independent signs at each length. For errors 1/k, choose strictly increasing finite thresholds N_k, each at least k and the source threshold. Put k(N)=max{k:N_k<=N} for large N. Then k(N)->infinity and a source polynomial at length N has relative error at most 1/k(N). Overwriting any prescribed m_N signs changes its all-circle modulus by at most 2m_N, by the triangle inequality in both directions. Thus m_N=o(sqrt(N)) gives error at most 1/k(N)+2m_N/sqrt(N)->0, for every sufficiently large N. Exact length and consecutive ±1 coefficients are preserved. This is not one compatible infinite sequence, and gives no macroscopic-prefix result.
2296: ### B. Uniform K(u), tails and area changes — PASS without repairs
2297: For 0<u<2, set q=u/2 and epsilon=(1-q)/(2q). The source genuinely provides C_epsilon uniform over S, rather than merely a spectrum exponent. Circle Hölder gives exponent q(1+epsilon)=(1+q)/2<1. Distortion bounds h by 27/4 on the half disk. With radial measure 2pi r dr, replacing r by 1 on the outer part gives at most 4pi C_epsilon^q/(1-q). Therefore the original
2298: `K(u)=1+(pi/4)(27/4)^u+4pi C_epsilon^q/(1-q)`
2299: is a valid finite uniform bound. For u_j=2-1/j, j>=2, Markov gives `area_D{|h|>lambda}<=inf_j K_j lambda^(-2+1/j)`, for lambda>0. Because phi'(f(z))=h(z) and the Jacobian of f is |h|^-2,
2300: `area_W{|phi'|>lambda}=integral_{|h|>lambda}|h|^-2 dA`,
2301: which is at most `inf_j K_j lambda^(-4+1/j)`. More generally, for any positive measurable denominator A, the inverse fourth-power weighted integral equals the original square-power weighted integral. These identities remain valid as extended nonnegative integrals on an unbounded W. There is no affine-normalization-free uniform constant.
2302: ### C. R135-gauge — PASS without repairs
2303: Every K_j is finite and positive, so the original recursive requirements `T_j>=exp(j²)`, `T_j>2T_(j-1)` for j>2, and `T_j>=(2^j K_j)^j` admit finite choices. They imply T_j->infinity. On each band the original g is finite; finitely many bands meet each compact interval. Consequently its running supremum Lambda is finite, Borel, nondecreasing, and at least 1. On band j, `2^j K_j<=t^(1/j)`, hence `g(t)<=t^(2/j)`. For any eta>0 all sufficiently late bands have 2/j<eta; the earlier bands have a finite supremum. Thus `Lambda(t)<=max(C_eta,t^eta)` eventually, and `log Lambda(t)/log t->0`.
2304: On |h|<T_2 the cost is at most pi T_2². On band j, dividing by Lambda dominates division by `2^j K_j |h|^(1/j)`; its cost is at most 2^-j by K_j. Since sum_(j>=2)2^-j=1/2, the original constant is exactly the safe bound **pi T_2²+1/2**. Area change gives that same bound for the inverse fourth power. The gauge is source-dependent but independent of f; no effective growth, convex Young-function property, or logarithmic upper bound is claimed.
2305: ### D. Positive weighted shift — PASS without repairs
2306: The weights exp(sqrt(j+1)-sqrt(j)) tend to 1, so the stated operator preserves continuity at infinity on the one-point compactification. Products telescope. Concavity of square root makes sqrt(j+n)-sqrt(j) maximal at j=0. Testing the constant function 1 gives the exact norm exp(sqrt(n)); the root limit gives radius 1. Scaling by 2 gives radius 2 and normalized powers exp(sqrt(n)), exceeding every fixed polynomial. This disproves only the abstract inference from positivity, compact state space and spectral radius to polynomial normalized growth. It is not a conformal operator realization or a counterexample to E_gamma.
2307: ### E. R135-measure — PASS without repairs
2308: Assume exactly the additional representation h=g+integral(z-zeta)^-1 dmu, with g bounded analytic, norm at most B, and finite complex measure of total variation M on the circle. For measurable E of area m, layer cake gives
2309: `integral_E |z-zeta|^-1 dA <= integral_0^infinity min(m,pi t^-2)dt =2 sqrt(pi m)`.
2310: Tonelli against |mu| first proves the relevant first moments are finite. Thus `integral_E|h|<=Bm+2sqrt(pi)M sqrt(m)`. For E={|h|>lambda}, lambda>B, rearrangement gives the original **4pi M²/(lambda-B)²**. This includes m=0; if M=0 the high sets are empty. For L=max(2,2B), lambda>=L gives C lambda^-2 with **C=16pi M²**.
2311: On [L2^j,L2^(j+1)), t² is at most four times the lower endpoint squared and log(e+t)>=(j+1)log 2. Each high band costs at most `4C/((j+1)log 2)^gamma`. For gamma>1 the series is bounded by 1+1/(gamma-1). The original bound is therefore
2312: `pi L²+64pi M²(log 2)^(-gamma)[1+1/(gamma-1)]`.
2313: Analytic nonconstant h has area-zero modulus level sets; normalized constant h=1 lies in the low band. The same constant applies to the inverse weighted fourth-power integral, and its tail is at most **16pi M² lambda^-4** for lambda>=L. Uniformity requires common B,M; a separate finite representation for each map does not supply it.
2314: ### F. Meromorphic-univalent subclass and both tail coefficients — PASS without repairs
2315: Assume f is meromorphic on a neighborhood of the closed disk and holomorphic, injective and normalized in D. Interior derivative zeros are excluded. A boundary zero of order m>=2 would give radial size O((1-r)^m), contradicting distortion `|f'(r zeta)|>=(1-r)/(1+r)^3`. Hence boundary zeros are simple. Meromorphic isolation and compactness give finitely many; f cannot have identically zero derivative. Poles of f become zeros of h. The residues at derivative zeros are **c_j=1/f''(zeta_j)**. Subtracting these principal parts leaves a function analytic in a possibly smaller neighborhood of the closed disk, hence bounded. Shrinking that neighborhood excludes any exterior poles; this is part of the original fixed-map decomposition, not a uniform collar assertion.
2316: Near one pole, h=c/(z-zeta)+b, |b|<=A. For lambda>A the high set lies between the two disk intersections with radii |c|/(lambda+A) and |c|/(lambda-A). A boundary-centered disk has intersection area `(pi/2)rho²+O(rho³)`. The bounded complement contributes nothing eventually. Thus the original D coefficient is **(pi/2)sum|c_j|²**. For W, integrate |h|^-2 over the same sandwich. Uniformly locally this weight is `|z-zeta|²/|c|²(1+O(|z-zeta|))`; its half-disk integral is `pi rho^4/(4|c|²)+O(rho^5)`. Hence the original W coefficient is **(pi/4)sum|c_j|²**. These are fixed-map limits; no common pole separation or remainder bound is inferred.
2317: ### G. Normalized Koebe test — PASS without repairs
2318: For f=z/(1-z)², h=(1-z)³/(1+z) equals `-z²+4z-7+8/(1+z)` exactly. The polynomial remainder has norm at most 1+4+7=12 and the measure has variation 8. Residue 8 therefore gives **32pi** for the D-tail and **16pi** for the W-tail. In a small inward cone at -1, |h| is comparable to 1/rho; the full local region has the corresponding upper estimate. The integral has precisely the same convergence criterion as `integral drho/[rho(log(1/rho))^gamma]`: finite exactly when gamma>1. Thus gamma<=1 is genuinely excluded inside the stated normalized class. This does not decide the parked gamma>1 target.
2319: ### Contribution, independently of correctness
2320: These proofs reduce to an overwrite costing 2m, Hölder and radial integration of a fully uniform source bound, diagonal summation of those constants, an elementary positive-operator example, a planar Cauchy-potential estimate, and local integration at finitely many simple poles. The source's uniform theorem and its transfer-norm statement were checked directly; the inherited full-source account retains the affine weighted law, compact superlevel escape, Borel covariant pairing, and bounded-displacement transport exhaustion. None of I135's deductions adds a quantitative critical transport operation or a uniform boundary representation. The fixed rational-map pole mechanism is also present in the frozen Jin comparison. **Technical only**, consistent with the full operations, not just the theorem names. This assessment neither reruns an upstream audit nor reopens E_gamma/W2.
2321: ## 3. Fresh screens and exact source interfaces
2322: The selected inventory has 81 representations from six manuscript roots, including both 149 papers, both 147 papers, the degree-uniform 143 master and exact quintic companion. The strongest statements were read before selecting. Every mathematical section of both mass-action papers was read through its ending. Other screen readings are scoped in the manifest: no claim is made to have freshly read all several hundred pages of unneeded limit-cycle or billiard lower proofs.
2323: The bounded prior search covers 187 iteration/review reports through I135, retaining 86 exact lexical occurrences and their positions. I52 was read in full, I54 in full, I57's original verdict and contribution/ending scope, and I58 in full. Their full-flow billiard spectral screen, relative entropy, additive entropy and restricted projective estimates are not recycled. I58's technical verdict remains. Other hits were inspected: for example I70's Birkhoff transfer function is not a billiard-caustic result; permanent, logistic soft assignments, and gas-collision grazing are different objects. Lexical absence is not novelty.
2324: ### Screen 143: effective global cycle count
2325: Precise candidate: given integer d, produce a terminating procedure returning a numerical integer bounding every degree-at-most-d planar polynomial field's isolated geometric periodic orbits. The source `thm:uniform` supplies a finite B(d) with no coefficient restrictions, including nonhyperbolic isolated cycles and cycles escaping spatially; it explicitly does not furnish an effective formula. Its final counting construction uses finitely many matching words and maximal-minor charts, absolute-to-uniform component bounds N_(w,I), and a common signed rotation that retains at least the original total count across disjoint neighborhoods. The entire rotation lemma and final theorem proof were read. The bound is `T(d)+sum N_(w,I)`; the cited existence of these integers is not an algorithm for computing them. No computable modulus for those operations is supplied here. **Unpaid, not selected.** No inference about nonisolated annuli, basins, cycle location, or times is made.
2326: The companion's exact statement is stronger within its class: x'=y-F(x), y'=-x, every real polynomial F of degree <=5, exactly maximum two isolated cycles, without parity, signs, amplitude or hyperbolicity assumptions. Degree refers to F, so damping F' has degree <=4. Its complete application and sharpness endings were read. It separately matches the two half-orbit midpoint functions on one width interval, uses fitted curvature or slope-intercept separation, and in the two-cycle case an integrating factor yields derivative equal to a positive factor times a nondecreasing function. Its direct small-epsilon example already supplies two hyperbolic cycles. Reprinting a two-cycle numeric bound is source-paid. A period bound would need an additional clock normalization: multiplying a polynomial field by positive epsilon preserves cycle images and degree but multiplies periods by epsilon^-1. No time dependence or added variable is smuggled into an autonomous polynomial hypothesis.
2327: ### Screen 147: finite boundary data versus full grazing annulus
2328: The strongest phase theorem assumes a smooth strictly convex positively curved boundary, an invariant open annulus containing every sufficiently grazing phase point, and a homeomorphism with essential simple closed leaves each individually invariant. It concludes ellipse, and its regularity theorem first constructs a jointly real-analytic physical caustic collar. The physical companion assumes the actual smooth convex caustic leaves filling a whole collar, with only continuous transverse dependence. Its conclusion includes circles and all ellipse moduli. Finite caustics, Cantor families, approximate invariance and arbitrary boundary foliations do not meet either contract. No quantitative grazing twist or analytic-strip constant is imported from these qualitative statements.
2329: One precise finite-data screen asks whether finitely many boundary support-function jets can bound C² distance to the normalized ellipse family by a constant times their defect from circle data. It is false. Normalize mean support to 1 and first Fourier harmonics to zero (scale and translation). Choose four independent smooth bumps in an open arc avoiding the finite sample angles. A nonzero linear combination psi has vanishing mean and first cosine/sine moments. For small nonzero epsilon, h=1+epsilon psi has h+h''>1/2, and defines a smooth positively curved table with exactly the sampled circle jets, even of every order. It agrees with the circle on an open arc but differs elsewhere, so cannot be an ellipse: equality of two conics on an open arc forces equality throughout. Its C² distance from normalized ellipses is positive. Indeed their centers are fixed by the first harmonics, their semiaxes are bounded by the fixed perimeter, and any uniformly convergent limiting ellipse is an ellipse or degenerate segment; neither can equal this smooth positive-curvature support function. Thus a zero right side cannot control that positive distance. This obstructs the specified jet-data estimate only, **not an estimate from finitely many genuine invariant caustics**. The latter needs dynamical input and paid norms/losses and is not pursued. No ellipse ambiguity is resolved by arbitrarily selecting a circular representative.
2330: ### Screen 149: independently intermittent rates — selected
2331: Write a finite mass-action system as `x'=sum_e k_e(t)x^(y_e)(y'_e-y_e)`. Fix the reaction graph, a positive class P=(c+S) intersect positive orthant, K>0, T>0 and 0<alpha<=KT. The new rate hypothesis is measurable
2332: `0<=k_e(t)<=K a.e.; integral_s^(s+T) k_e(t)dt>=alpha for every s>=0 and every edge e`.  (PE)
2333: Individual rates may vanish, at different times. The underlying union graph is weakly reversible; its instantaneous active graph need not be. The tested target **P138** restricts to classes with compact closure and asks for a compact subset of P eventually containing every positive trajectory, uniformly over all PE rate paths, with an entrance bound uniform on each prescribed compact subset Q of P. The set and time may depend on the fixed graph, P,K,T,alpha (and the time on Q). There is no uniform bound over all classes or all initial states, no common invariant-set assertion, and no convergence-to-equilibrium claim. For an empty reaction set P is a singleton and the claim is trivial. **P138 remains unproved/unrefuted for arbitrary finite weakly reversible networks.**
2334: This is distinct from k_e(t) in [a,b], a>0. The latter is already paid by the full source operation, as the next section verifies. It is also distinct from merely requiring each edge to have infinite total activity, which has no fixed window lower bound.
2335: ## 4. What the strongest permanence operation already pays
2336: The September companion constructs an ambient compact convex invariant polytope containing each prescribed positive initial point. The October companion strengthens this to a common compact convex absorbing set in each positive class, including unbounded classes, with individual entry times. Both complete affine-label and dynamical proofs were read. The following is the operative reduction, rather than a novelty claim based on the printed constant-rate headline.
2337: The tolerance includes coordinates and **all complex differences**, including different linkage classes. It can be discontinuous across comparison hyperplanes. The finite affine construction proceeds by the minimum exponent, raised-coordinate types, compensating offsets, closed limiting active sets, and finite overlapping interval gluing. It gives fixed slopes before choosing h, uniformly for every active label, including ties. Its finite separation H>0 yields `r·y<r·z => x^z<=h^H x^y` on B_h=[h,h^-1]^d. Return paths give an upward edge at every crossed complex-level cut; its source monomial dominates the entire downward cut flux. The finite-minimum derivative is controlled for **all** active branches. Constant branches give a compact plateau K_h in int B_sqrt(h); slopes in S-perp cannot be active off the plateau inside the class. These are the nontrivial operations already paid.
2338: For arbitrary measurable rates in [a,b], choose the same h so that `|R| b h^H<=a/2`. Let m=max_y |y|_1. A crossed cut has flux at least `(a/2)h^m`, and any nonzero adjacent level gap is at least 2H. Thus, for an active slope not in S-perp,
2339: `r·f(t,x)>=a H h^m=:delta_h`  (1)
2340: a.e. on B_h. All other active slopes give nonnegative derivative. Caratheodory solutions exist locally because bounded measurable coefficients multiply polynomials locally Lipschitz in x. The finite minimum composed with an absolutely continuous curve is absolutely continuous; at differentiability times its derivative is the active-branch directional derivative. The same source invariance and finite-entry argument therefore applies, with a common delta_h. No regularity of the rates beyond measurability is needed.
2341: This also makes an explicit part of the time budget visible. For c,x in B_h, `0<=F_h(c)-F_h(x)<=2d/h`, using an active slope at x and ||r||_1<=d. A trajectory confined to B_h reaches the plateau within
2342: `2d/(a H h^(m+1))`.  (2)
2343: For compact initial Q in P choose h_0 small enough that every point of Q has exponent coordinates of size <H, as does c. Then Q lies in K_(h0). The source sequence h_(j+1)=min(sqrt(h_j),h_P) reaches h_P after finitely many steps and satisfies K_(hj) subset B_(h(j+1)). Summing (2) at the successive boxes gives a finite common time over Q and every [a,b]-valued rate path. No nesting of the polytopes is assumed. Their geometric thresholds need not be computable; displaying (2) does not compute them. **Variable positive-box rates and compact-initial uniform entry are technical consequences of the complete source construction, not the selected discovery.**
2344: The current primary comparison agrees on the importance of this boundary. Craciun–Nazarov–Pantea, [1010.3050v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1010.3050v2), Definition 2.6, requires rates in (eta,1/eta), initially piecewise differentiable. Its two-species Theorems 6.2/6.4 use source-monomial domination on invariant polygons and a strictly positive compact-region drift; the proofs through the permanence ending were read. They do not allow independently off reactions. Craciun's [1501.02860v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.02860v3), dated September 23, 2026, gives the variable-k inclusion embedding and, in the final Section 8 remark, persistence of **bounded** positive trajectories. Its variable-k condition retains a positive lower bound. The embedding statement and application ending were read; this is a scope comparison, not an independent audit of the entire 81-page construction. Neither external statement is a premise of (1)–(2) or of the new routes.
2345: Why not integrate the source's cut inequality under PE? Its upward contribution is k_e(t)x^y(t), while the comparison depends on the current active slope. The edge with a large time integral may be active when its monomial is tiny, and labels can change within a window. Replacing the integral of this product by the product of its integrals is invalid. Even a fixed monomial order at one time supplies no such replacement. The next routes pay this time-order issue on actual subclasses.
2346: ## 5. Route I — chronological paths, explicit permanence and contraction
2347: Consider a strongly connected monomolecular network on n>=2 species, with reactions A_i->A_j on its fixed directed graph and PE rates. Parallel edges can be excluded by the graph convention; otherwise use total outgoing-rate bounds below. Total mass M=sum x_i is conserved, and each positive class is precisely the open simplex of that mass. Put
2348: `L=(n-1)K, H=(n-1)T, beta=exp(-L H) min(1,alpha^(n-1))`.  (3)
2349: Let U(t,s) be the column-stochastic evolution matrix, Q(t) its column generator. Off-diagonal Q_(ji)=k_(i->j), and the diagonal is minus total outgoing rate. Positivity and column sums follow from the integral equation, or the convergent Peano series for G=Q+LI>=0 with U=e^(-L(t-s))V.
2350: For any i,j choose a simple directed path j->...->i of length m<=n-1. In the mth nonnegative Peano integral over [s,s+H], restrict its successive edge times to the first m consecutive windows of length T. These times are automatically ordered, and integration factors into m edge integrals, each at least alpha. Thus `U_(ij)(s+H,s)>=exp(-LH)alpha^m>=beta`. For i=j use the zeroth term. This proves the estimate without simultaneous activity, reversibility of instantaneous graphs, or exchanging time order. Consequently, for every positive initial point and **all t>=H**,
2351: `beta M <= x_i(t) <= M`.  (4)
2352: This is an actual uniform time independent even of the initial point on this particular simplex. It is a bound on all future states of admissible trajectories; the coordinate-floor simplex is not asserted forward invariant from every one of its points under every fresh PE schedule.
2353: There is also a usable quantitative stability estimate for two solutions driven by the same rates and having the same M. Put b=beta/2 and theta=1-nb. Column stochasticity forces n beta<=1, so 1/2<=theta<1. Over each H block,
2354: `U=b 11^T+theta R`,
2355: where R is column stochastic. The first term kills zero-sum differences and ||R||_(1->1)=1. Remaining shorter intervals are nonexpansive. Hence
2356: `||x(t)-x_tilde(t)||_1 <= theta^floor((t-s)/H) ||x(s)-x_tilde(s)||_1`.  (5)
2357: For a weakly reversible monomolecular graph with several linkage components, apply this to each strongly connected component with its conserved positive mass M_l; isolated species are constant. The full class is the product of those mass simplexes. Uniform bounds across classes require each M_l bounded below; conservation itself prevents an absolute lower bound as M_l->0. No equilibrium convergence under arbitrary time-dependent rates is inferred from (5).
2358: **Operation comparison and limit of route.** Chronological path minorization and stochastic contraction are classical. The current [Proskurnikov–Calafiore extended version, 2105.07183v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.07183v1), allows locally integrable coefficients and uses integral connectivity; its evolution-matrix Lemma 14 and proof turn integrated edges into positive entries, and its uniform-window discussion gives exponential consensus. Its row-consensus and delay conventions differ from the conservative column flow here; our proof independently pays the orientation and constants instead of asserting that transposition preserves a nonautonomous evolution. The retained two-column PDF text is read through that lemma's ending. No exact printed duplicate of (3) is required for the technical classification: the entire mechanism is positive time-ordered propagation plus the elementary stochastic decomposition.
2359: For nonlinear complexes, the state-dependent effective rate along a species transfer may vanish like another concentration. A lower bound on integral k_e alone is not a lower bound on those effective rates. Strong connectivity of the complex graph is not a species-level Markov chain. Thus (3)–(5) do not solve P138 by renaming complexes as species.
2360: ## 6. Route II — exact nonlinear ratio dynamics and sharp scope obstructions
2361: Take the genuinely nonlinear weakly reversible pair
2362: `A+B --a(t)--> 2B`, `2B --b(t)--> A+B`,
2363: with 0<=a,b<=K and each satisfying the same T-window integral bound alpha. Fix M=A+B>0 and positive initial A,B. The stoichiometric class is 0<B<M. Direct mass-action algebra gives
2364: `B'=a(t)(M-B)B-b(t)B²`.
2365: Use the physical ratio r=A/B=M/B-1, not an added species. It obeys exactly
2366: `r'=-M a(t) r+M b(t)`.
2367: Thus, writing A_a(s,t)=integral_s^t a,
2368: `r(t)=e^(-M A_a(0,t)) r(0)+M integral_0^t e^(-M A_a(s,t)) b(s)ds`.  (6)
2369: The formula is valid for measurable rates and gives finite r>0 at finite times, hence a global positive solution with the original conservation law. It does not change the reaction model.
2370: Every interval of length v contains floor(v/T) complete windows, so `A_a(s,t)>=alpha floor((t-s)/T)`. Set
2371: `q=exp(-M alpha), R=MKT/(1-q), ell=M alpha exp(-MKT)`.  (7)
2372: Partitioning the integral in (6) backwards from t into T-length pieces gives its upper bound R. For t>=T, retain its last window and use A_a(s,t)<=KT there; the b integral is at least alpha. Therefore
2373: `ell <= r(t) <= q^floor(t/T) r(0)+R` for t>=T.  (8)
2374: Choose `N=max(1,ceil(log^+(r(0))/(M alpha)))`. For all t>=NT,
2375: `B(t)>=M/(2+R), A(t)>=M ell/(1+ell), A(t)+B(t)=M`.  (9)
2376: These are explicit PE permanence bounds on a nonlinear network with bounded class. Each constant is uniform over the rate path. For a compact initial subset with r(0)<=R_0, replace r(0) by R_0 in N.
2377: For a parameter range 0<M_-<=M<=M_+<infinity, common choices are
2378: `R_*=M_+KT/(1-exp(-M_-alpha)), ell_*=M_-alpha exp(-M_+KT)`.
2379: Replace both numerator M's in (9) by M_-, R by R_*, ell by ell_*, and M in the entrance denominator by M_-. This pays class-parameter uniformity only on that declared range. Two solutions with the same M and rate path satisfy exactly
2380: `|r(t)-r_tilde(t)|<=exp(-M alpha floor((t-s)/T))|r(s)-r_tilde(s)|`.
2381: Since the map r->(Mr/(1+r),M/(1+r)) is 2M-Lipschitz into l1 for r>=0, this yields a concentration stability estimate with the same decay and factor 2M. It asserts synchronization for a common forcing, not convergence to a fixed equilibrium.
2382: This route uses the actual autocatalytic term but is a Bernoulli/logistic linearization, not a new general network mechanism. CNP's positive-box theorem covers this graph under its stronger pointwise positivity hypothesis; (6)–(9) pay the off intervals directly. The exact solvability explains the technical contribution without needing a novelty claim based on missing terminology.
2383: ### Genuine obstructions to broader conclusions
2384: **No uniform entrance time over all positive initial states, even at fixed rates.** In this same model, B'<=MK B, so B(t)<=B(0)e^(MKt). To reach any fixed delta>0 takes at least log(delta/B(0))/(MK). As B(0)->0 this diverges while M and positive constant rates stay fixed. Thus compact-initial uniformity in P138 is substantive; its global replacement is false.
2385: **Unbounded-class PE extension is false in the original mass-action model.** Take 2A reversible with 3A. With period 2, use k_+=1,k_-=0 for the first unit interval and k_+=0,k_-=1 for the second. Both rate integrals equal 1 on every length-2 window; PE holds with K=1,T=2,alpha=1. Starting at x(0)=2 gives x'=x² until time 1 and x(t)=2/(1-2t), which blows up at t=1/2. The union network is weakly reversible and has unbounded positive class. This refutes the proposed all-classes/global-existence PE extension, not the stipulated autonomous theorem or positive-box extension. Adding a positive pointwise lower reverse rate removes this particular example's hypothesis.
2386: **A common positive compact set invariant for every fresh PE schedule is too strong.** Already for A reversible with B, mass M, permit the same alternating schedules. A nonempty compact subset K of the positive simplex invariant for every schedule would in particular be invariant under the time-one pure A->B map. Iterating that fixed map on a point of K sends A to zero while remaining in K, contradicting compactness inside the positive simplex. The iteration is an argument about invariance of the same set under one map, not a claim that the concatenated pure-reaction path satisfies PE. Eventual bounds such as (4) and (9) survive; arbitrary-restart common invariance does not.
2387: ### Deepest paid partial and missing operation
2388: Equations (3)–(5) and (6)–(9) are complete estimates with rate/class/time quantifiers, and the three obstructions respect the actual reaction models. In the general bounded class, positivity for finite time follows because each consumption monomial contains the consumed species and all coordinates are bounded; a Gronwall lower estimate prevents finite-time boundary hitting. This does not give an eventual lower bound independent of initial distance to every boundary face.
2389: To finish P138 one would need a uniform lower bound on chronological **state-weighted** reaction fluxes near every possible boundary face, or another boundary-repulsion construction stable under independently off edges. The source's instantaneous active-label comparison supplies neither a window-invariant label nor this weighted flux bound. Route I pays it for linear propagation; Route II pays it through a special exact ratio equation. Neither argument closes it for arbitrary complexes and linkage classes. No substantial finding is nominated, and no external open-problem ranking or worldwide-priority assertion is made.

2557: ## 2. Independent audit of ORIGINAL I138, unchanged
2558: ### 2.1 Finite support jets and dynamical screens — PASS without repairs
2559: Use the support function h(theta), with mean (2pi)^−1 integral h=1 and first cosine/sine coefficients zero. These fix scale and translation; rotations and ellipse eccentricity remain allowed. Select four nonzero smooth bumps with disjoint supports in an open arc avoiding every sample angle. The linear map from their span to the three moments (mean, cosine, sine) has a nonzero kernel. Disjoint supports ensure a nonzero coefficient vector gives a nonzero function psi. Every derivative of psi vanishes at each sampled angle. For
2560: `0<|epsilon|<1/[2 max(1,||psi+psi''||_infinity)]`,
2561: h=1+epsilon psi has h+h''>1/2, hence is a smooth strictly convex positive-curvature support function with the stated normalization and exactly the circle's sampled jets of every order. This explicit smallness bound spells out the original “small epsilon”; it does not repair a false original constant. The table agrees with the circle on an open arc and differs elsewhere. An ellipse agreeing there is the same conic everywhere, impossible.
2562: The distance conclusion is also valid. For centered normalized ellipses, perimeter=integral h=2pi and perimeter≥4a, where a is the larger semiaxis; thus a≤pi/2. The semiaxes and rotations form a compact parameter closure allowing degenerate ellipses. If the C² distance were zero, a sequence would converge uniformly to this h and also to an ellipse or segment support function. No degenerate limit has its smooth positive curvature, and no genuine ellipse equals it. The distance is positive. This disproves the specified finite **support-jet** defect bound only. Finitely many genuine invariant caustics are dynamical constraints and are not covered by this construction.
2563: The degree screen is precise. The source's finite B(d), assembled from finite matching words, minor charts and common signed rotation, does not supply a procedure computing its constituent uniform component bounds. The exact quintic Lienard maximum two is already paid; its polynomial degree is the degree of F, not F'. Multiplying an autonomous polynomial vector field by epsilon>0 preserves its degree and geometric cycles and multiplies every period by epsilon^−1. Thus no unnormalized universal time bound follows. Adding variables, switching or forcing would change the contract. The strongest billiard hypotheses remain full invariant grazing annulus/physical caustic collar, not finite jets, finite caustics, Cantor leaves or approximate invariance.
2564: ### 2.2 Positive-box measurable rates and compact-initial entry — PASS without repairs
2565: I read all mathematical sections of **both** September and October mass-action source papers through their endings. The affine operation fixes slopes before h. It uses the minimum exponent, successively raised-coordinate types with compensating offsets, closed limiting active sets, and overlapping finite interval gluing. Its tolerance contains coordinate directions and **every complex difference**, including sources in distinct linkage classes. Nonzero comparisons have matching signs and a finite separation H>0; every active label, including every tied minimizer, obeys them. Equal slope levels need not have equal monomials.
2566: For measurable a≤k_e(t)≤b, impose the original |R|b h^H≤a/2. With m=max_y|y|_1 and x in B_h, every monomial is at least h^m. For a crossed ordered-level cut, weak reversibility provides an upward crossing. The comparison x^z≤h^H x^y makes its flux minus the entire downward sum at least (a/2)x^y≥(a/2)h^m. A cut with only upward crossings is even easier. Every nonzero adjacent level gap is at least 2H. The cut decomposition therefore gives exactly the safe original
2567: `r·f(t,x) ≥ delta_h := a H h^m`
2568: for every active slope outside S-perp, with nonnegative derivative for all other active slopes, almost everywhere. On P outside K_h an S-perp slope cannot be active: its value equals its value at c and is at least F_h(c). This pays the uniform strictness without a rate-path-dependent compactness argument.
2569: Bounded measurable coefficients and local polynomial Lipschitz bounds give unique Caratheodory solutions. A finite affine minimum is Lipschitz, so its composition with an absolutely continuous solution is absolutely continuous. At almost every time of differentiability of the curve and composition, the active-branch directional-derivative formula applies; every active derivative has the required bound. Integration, first exit and compact continuation give the same trapping argument. No differentiability of the rates is added.
2570: For c,x in B_h, an active slope at x yields 0≤F_h(c)−F_h(x)≤2d/h. Dividing by delta_h gives the original time bound
2571: `2d/(a H h^(m+1))`.
2572: For compact Q⊂P, bounded log coordinates permit one h_0≤h_P with every point of Q and c having exponent coordinates of magnitude <H. Every active label there is constant and has the common least constant value, so Q⊂K_h0. The capped square-root sequence h_(j+1)=min(sqrt(h_j),h_P) reaches h_P finitely. K_hj⊂int B_sqrt(hj)⊂B_h(j+1) supplies confinement at each next stage. Sum the displayed time bound at those next stages. **No nesting of K_h is used.** The set and finite compact-initial time are uniform over the positive-box rate paths, even on unbounded classes. This is already a consequence of the complete source operation; its geometric thresholds are not claimed computable.
2573: ### 2.3 Original monomolecular equations (3)–(5) — PASS without repairs
2574: Keep the original simple directed graph convention: no parallel reactions. Otherwise replace L by an actual uniform total-outgoing-rate bound; L=(n−1)K cannot be retained for arbitrarily many parallel edges. For n≥2, strong connectivity, and
2575: `L=(n−1)K, H=(n−1)T, beta=exp(−LH) min(1,alpha^(n−1))`,
2576: the column generator has Q_ji=k_(i→j) off diagonal and Q_jj equal to minus the outgoing sum. Thus G=Q+LI is entrywise nonnegative a.e. The Peano series for V and U(t,s)=e^−L(t−s)V(t,s) preserves positivity and column sums one; this is the correct nonautonomous column evolution, not a casual transpose of a row-consensus theorem.
2577: For any starting s≥0, a simple path j→i has m≤n−1 edges. Restrict its chronological Peano variables to the first m successive disjoint T-windows inside [s,s+H]. Ordering is then automatic up to measure-zero endpoints, and the nonnegative integral factors into m rate integrals, each ≥alpha. Hence U_ij(s+H,s)≥e^−LH alpha^m≥beta. The zero-length path for i=j contributes the identity and is essential when alpha>1. The minimum in beta correctly covers both alpha≤1 and alpha≥1. Rolling the interval gives (4), beta M≤x_i(t)≤M, for **every** t≥H, not only block endpoints.
2578: Column stochasticity implies n beta≤1. With b=beta/2 and theta=1−nb, one has 1/2≤theta<1 and U=b11^T+theta R with R column stochastic. The first term annihilates equal-mass differences and ||R||_1=1. Every remaining shorter interval is nonexpansive. This proves precisely (5), theta^floor((t−s)/H), for arbitrary starting s. Each linkage component has its own conserved positive mass and simplex; isolated species are constant. No class-independent positive mass floor or fixed-equilibrium convergence is claimed. Common-forcing synchronization is the exact scope.
2579: ### 2.4 Original nonlinear equations (6)–(9) — PASS without repairs
2580: For A+B reversible with 2B, conservation gives A+B=M and the original mass-action equation B'=a(M−B)B−bB². Differentiating the physical ratio r=M/B−1 gives r'=−Ma r+Mb exactly. Variation of constants is valid for measurable a,b and gives original (6), finite strictly positive r on finite intervals, and hence a global positive physical solution.
2581: An interval of length v contains floor(v/T) consecutive complete windows, regardless of its starting point. With q=e^−Malpha, the backward T-block sum in (6) is at most MKT sum_(j≥0)q^j=R. On the final window, integral b≥alpha and integral_s^t a≤KT, giving ell=Malpha e^−MKT. Thus original (8) is valid for t≥T. If
2582: `N=max(1,ceil(log^+(r(0))/(M alpha)))`,
2583: then q^floor(t/T)r(0)≤1 for t≥NT, including r(0)≤1 and the integer-equality case. Substitution into B=M/(1+r) and A=Mr/(1+r) proves both bounds in (9): M/(2+R) and Mell/(1+ell). Compact initial ratio bounds give the claimed common entrance integer.
2584: For M∈[M_−,M_+], the original R_*=M_+KT/(1−e^−M_−alpha) and ell_*=M_−alpha e^−M_+KT are valid upper/lower bounds. Replacing the concentration numerators and entrance denominator by M_− is safe; ell/(1+ell) is increasing. Subtracting the ratio equations gives the stated exponential synchronization on every [s,t]. The physical map is 2M-Lipschitz into l1 for r≥0. This does not synchronize different masses or imply a fixed equilibrium under time-dependent forcing.
2585: ### 2.5 Three stronger assertions obstructed; finite-time positivity — PASS without repairs
2586: The all-initial entrance obstruction keeps M and constant positive rates fixed. B'≤MK B implies B(t)≤B(0)e^MKt. Every compact subset of the open simplex has a positive B floor, and its first entrance time diverges as B(0)→0. This does not obstruct compact-initial uniformity.
2587: The unbounded-class example 2A reversible with 3A has period-two rates (1,0) then (0,1), each active for one unit. Translation invariance of the integral of a periodic function over a full period gives each integral exactly one on **every** length-two sliding window. At x(0)=2 the solution x=2/(1−2t) blows up at t=1/2, before the first reverse phase. The union graph is weakly reversible and the class is unbounded. This is an exact counterexample to the unbounded PE extension, not the stipulated fixed-positive theorem.
2588: For the fresh-schedule common-invariant-set assertion, already A reversible with B suffices. Any nonempty compact positive set invariant under every fresh admissible schedule must be invariant under the first time-one pure A→B map of the same alternating PE schedule. Iteration of that map sends A to zero inside that same set, impossible. The concatenated all-forward schedule is not asserted PE. This disproves the stronger invariance property, while leaving the exact eventual target untouched.
2589: On a compact class, write Delta_e=y'_e−y_e and choose M≥1 bounding all coordinates on its closure. Every negative Delta_ei has y_ei≥1. The explicit constant
2590: `C=max_i K sum_e (−Delta_ei)_+ M^(|y_e|−1)`
2591: therefore gives x_i'≥−Cx_i. Integration yields x_i(t)≥e^−C(t−s)x_i(s)>0 at finite times. Stoichiometry preserves c+S, compactness bounds the vector field, and local Caratheodory continuation makes the solution global. This is finite-time positivity, not an eventual floor.
2592: ### 2.6 Complete-operation contribution assessment
2593: The finite-jet construction is the finite-codimension bump argument with the actual convexity and ellipse-family closure paid. Time rescaling is an exact clock obstruction. The variable-positive-rate result uses the entire stipulated affine operation—discontinuous tolerance, fixed slopes, all comparisons, cut regrouping, all-active derivatives, compact plateau and scale iteration—not only its permanence headline. The monomolecular proof is chronological positive-matrix propagation followed by stochastic minorization; the nonlinear pair is an exact Bernoulli ratio linearization. The three obstructions are genuine but elementary at their scopes. None introduces the missing general PE boundary mechanism. **Independent disposition: technical; no substantial nomination.**
2594: ## 3. Exact P138 and a general usable flux estimate
2595: Fix a finite union graph, P=(c+S)∩R^n_>0 with compact closure, K,T>0, 0<alpha≤KT, and measurable rates satisfying 0≤k_e≤K a.e. and integral_s^(s+T)k_e≥alpha for every s≥0 and every edge. The desired compact set may depend on graph,P,K,T,alpha; the finite entry time may additionally depend on compact Q⊂P. No universal fresh-schedule invariance, equilibrium convergence or all-class/all-initial uniformity is demanded. Empty reaction graph gives a singleton class and is trivial.
2596: Retain M,C above and put d=max(1,max_e|y_e|). For u∈[s,s+T], the one-sided estimate gives
2597: `x(u)^y ≥ exp(−C|y|(u−s)) x(s)^y`.
2598: Consequently every actual state-weighted edge flux satisfies
2599: `integral_s^(s+T) k_e(u)x(u)^y_e du ≥ alpha exp(−CdT)x(s)^y_e`.  (F1)
2600: More usefully, the coordinate variation inequality is
2601: `x_i(s+T) ≥ exp(−CT)x_i(s) + alpha exp(−C(d+1)T) sum_e (Delta_ei)_+ x(s)^y_e`.  (F2)
2602: Indeed x_i'≥−Cx_i+sum_e(Delta_ei)_+k_ex^y_e, and integrating the factor e^Cu then using the preceding monomial estimate proves (F2). No equality between a flux integral and a product of averages is used. These estimates hold on **arbitrary starting windows**. They retain every original reaction and its actual consumption bound.
2603: There is no matching uniform upper comparison to x(s)^y. In the conservative nonlinear pair 2A reversible with A+B, mass M, activate the forward edge alone for a unit, then the reverse alone for a unit and repeat. This is K=1,T=2,alpha=1 PE. For M=1 and A(0)=1−epsilon, the first phase gives A(t)=A(0)/(1+A(0)t) and B(t)=1−A(t). As epsilon→0, B(1)→1/2 while B(0)=epsilon. During the reverse phase AB has an integral bounded below independently of epsilon, whereas A(0)B(0)→0. Thus the averaged effective reverse coefficient obtained by dividing the two-window weighted flux by its initial monomial diverges. This is an exact obstruction to that averaging method, **not** a counterexample to P138. F1 supplies a lower effective coefficient but no upper box for applying the source operation.
2604: ## 4. Route I: nonlinear chronological propagation under a siphon condition
2605: A nonempty species set Z is a siphon if every reaction with a product in Z has a reactant in Z. Assume additionally:
2606: **(SC)** For every siphon Z there is a nonzero w_Z≥0 with support contained in Z and w_Z·Delta_e=0 for every reaction e.
2607: It is enough to check minimal siphons. On the fixed positive class let m_Z=w_Z·c>0. There are finitely many Z; choose
2608: `epsilon=min(1, min_Z m_Z/(2||w_Z||_1)) >0`.
2609: If there are no siphons, use epsilon=1. At any starting state x(s)∈P, put J_0={i:x_i(s)≥epsilon}. Its complement cannot contain a siphon: otherwise the conserved m_Z would be strictly less than epsilon||w_Z||_1≤m_Z/2. Starting from J_0, repeatedly add every product species of a reaction all of whose reactants already belong to J_r. If a nonempty complement remains when this process stops, that complement is a siphon, a contradiction. Therefore the closure contains all species after at most n rounds. This is species-support logic with all reactants required; it does not replace the complex graph by a species Markov chain.
2610: Define the positive decreasing thresholds
2611: `delta_0=epsilon, gamma=alpha exp(−C(d+1)T),`
2612: `delta_(r+1)=min(exp(−CT)delta_r, gamma delta_r^d), r=0,...,n−1`.  (F3)
2613: All delta_r≤1. If every species of J_r has concentration at least delta_r at time s+rT, an old species retains exp(−CT)delta_r at the next endpoint. A newly added species i has y_ei=0 and y'_ei≥1 for an edge with all source species in J_r. Integer stoichiometry gives (Delta_ei)_+≥1 and x^y_e≥delta_r^|y_e|≥delta_r^d. Formula F2 then gives gamma delta_r^d. Induction proves x_i(s+nT)≥delta_n for all i. Padding completed rounds simply uses the old-species bound.
2614: Apply this at s=t−nT for every t≥nT. It proves
2615: `x(t) in {x in closure(P): x_i≥delta_n for all i}, for every t≥nT`.  (F4)
2616: The displayed set is compact and lies in P. Constants are uniform over all admissible schedules and, in this restricted case, over all positive initial points of the fixed class. This stronger entrance statement is compatible with I138's obstruction because that autocatalytic network has an unprotected siphon. The proof does not assert that the displayed floor set is invariant under every fresh schedule.
2617: A genuine nonlinear, rank-two example is **A+B reversible with C, C reversible with D**. Its positive classes satisfy A+C+D=u>0 and B+C+D=v>0 and have compact closure. The minimal siphons are {A,C,D} and {B,C,D}, protected by those two conservation laws. The degree-two binding flux makes this a nonlinear application beyond a monomolecular flow or I138's exact ratio equation. With M=max(1,u,v), one may use d=2, C=max(KM,2K), epsilon=min(1,u/6,v/6), and F3 with n=4. These deliberately safe constants are explicit.
2618: The same proof works under the slightly more geometric condition that no siphon face meets closure(P): take epsilon less than half the minimum, over siphons Z and x∈closure(P), of max_(i∈Z)x_i. Condition SC is an explicit conservation certificate for that separation. This observation does not remove the critical-siphon case; compact closure alone does not supply that separation.
2619: **Comparison.** Angeli–De Leenheer–Sontag's 2011 paper, Sections 2–7 through both technical proof endings, uses a positive pointwise kinetic envelope and proves that extinction sets are siphons, then excludes them with conserved semiflows. Its proof controls residence near a boundary point using bounded speed and positive inward drift. F1–F4 replace that pointwise step by chronological window propagation and quantify a finite uniform delay. The conserved-siphon mechanism remains the same; this is a technical quantitative PE extension, not a new general boundary mechanism. [Primary paper](https://sontaglab.org/FTPDIR/angeli_leenheer_sontag_siap_online2011.pdf).
2620: ## 5. Route II: a window return argument for arbitrary rank-one classes
2621: Here assume **dim S=1**, with any number of species, arbitrary nonnegative integer complexes and any number of linkage classes. Apart from self-loops, the graph is weakly reversible. Compact closure makes P an open bounded segment. Parametrize it as
2622: `x(z)=a+vz, 0<z<L`,
2623: where a and a+vL are the two boundary endpoints. Choose the orientation toward the second endpoint. Write Delta_e=c_e v. At the lower endpoint Z={i:a_i=0} is nonempty, v_i>0 for i∈Z, and sigma=sum_(i∈Z)v_i>0. For a complex y define p_y=sum_(i∈Z)y_i. Then
2624: `p_(y')−p_y = c_e sigma`.  (R1)
2625: Discard self-loops. In a nontrivial linkage class, the minimum p is achieved at a complex with a path to a larger p. The first edge on such a path already leaves the minimum: two distinct complexes in that linkage class differ by a nonzero multiple of v, so R1 prohibits a zero p difference. Let p be the minimum of p_y over all nontrivial linkage classes. Select an increasing edge e_* from a p-minimizer. Every decreasing edge has source order at least p+1, because its target has smaller **integer** order at least p. This remains true across linkage classes; isolated complexes do not enter p.
2626: Fix 0<rho_0≤min(1,L/3) so all coordinates outside Z stay bounded below by positive numbers on [0,rho_0]. Factor
2627: `x(z)^y=z^(p_y) g_y(z)`,
2628: where g_y is continuous and strictly positive on that closed interval. Its factors are v_i^y_i for i∈Z and (a_i+v_i z)^y_i for i outside Z. Set g_y^−=min g_y>0, g_y^+=max g_y, and
2629: `a_*=c_(e_*) g_(y_e*)^− >0,`
2630: `D=max(1, K sum_(c_e<0) |c_e| g_(y_e)^+ rho_0^(p_y−p−1))`.
2631: The actual scalar equation, with no averaging and no ratio linearization, obeys for 0<z≤rho_0
2632: `z' ≥ a_* k_(e_*)(t) z^p −D z^(p+1) ≥ −D z`.  (R2)
2633: Define
2634: `c_*=(a_* alpha/2) exp(−pDT)`,
2635: `rho=min(rho_0, a_* alpha/[2DT 2^(p+1) exp((2p+1)DT)])`.  (R3)
2636: These numbers depend only on graph,class,K,T,alpha and the fixed parametrization. The p=0 case is included.
2637: **Window increment.** Suppose z([s,s+T])⊂(0,rho]. If z(s+T)>2z(s), the increment exceeds z(s). Otherwise the last inequality in R2 gives, for every u in the window,
2638: `exp(−DT) z(s) ≤ z(u) ≤ exp(DT) z(s+T) ≤2 exp(DT)z(s)`.
2639: The upper estimate here is conditional on this particular nondoubling return. It is obtained backwards from the endpoint; no general two-sided relative bound for a producing species is assumed. Integrating the first inequality in R2, using PE on the one selected edge and z(s)≤rho, yields
2640: `z(s+T)−z(s)`
2641: `≥ a_* alpha exp(−pDT) z(s)^p − DT 2^(p+1)exp((p+1)DT) z(s)^(p+1)`
2642: `≥ c_* z(s)^p`.
2643: In both cases,
2644: `z(s+T)−z(s) ≥ min(z(s), c_* z(s)^p)>0`.  (R4)
2645: This is a proved return-map inequality on every window that stays in the band. It neither integrates an instantaneous minimum nor freezes an active affine label.
2646: **Entry and protection after exit.** If 0<z(0)<rho and the trajectory has not yet reached rho, all successive full windows stay in the band and their endpoints increase by at least
2647: `eta(z(0))=min(z(0),c_* z(0)^p)>0`.
2648: Thus it reaches rho by time
2649: `T[1+ceil(rho/eta(z(0)))]`.  (R5)
2650: This bound is intentionally loose and handles exact endpoint equalities. Once a trajectory has reached or started above rho, every later open excursion below rho starts at rho. Such an excursion has length <T: a whole T-window beginning at rho and staying at most rho would contradict R4. Within the excursion, R2 gives z≥rho exp(−DT). Therefore after the first exit,
2651: `z(t)≥delta_left:=rho exp(−DT)` for all subsequent t.  (R6)
2652: For a compact initial Q, let z_Q=min_(x(z)∈Q)z>0 and replace z(0) in R5 by min(z_Q,rho); eta is nondecreasing for every integer p≥0. Initial points already above rho need no transient. Repeat the argument with w=L−z at the other endpoint, with its own p,a_*,D,rho,c_* and delta_right. Both bands were chosen of width ≤L/3. At the maximum of the two compact-initial entry bounds, and at every later time,
2653: `delta_left ≤ z(t) ≤ L−delta_right`.  (R7)
2654: This compact segment lies in P and is uniform over all PE schedules. Its eventual-membership property does not say every point in it is safe under every fresh schedule. Measurable solutions and global finite-time positivity were already paid in Section 2.5. Rank zero is the singleton case.
2655: This proves P138 **for every rank-one weakly reversible network on a compact class**, not just a reversible pair, common clock, fixed degree, one linkage class or exactly soluble ratio system. For example, on A+B=M the two linkage classes 2A+B reversible with A+2B and 4A+B reversible with A+4B give a polynomial equation of degrees three and five, with independent intermittent coefficients; the preceding endpoint-order proof applies without a Bernoulli reduction.
2656: **Comparison.** The complete current Boros–Hofbauer v2 proof was read. Its normalized monomial path estimate makes an entropy derivative negative using a pointwise coefficient floor, projects to boundary faces, and removes vertex/edge/higher-face neighborhoods to form a trapping set. It treats arbitrary dimension with one linkage class under that stronger rate hypothesis. R1–R7 instead exploit scalar endpoint order, an integer gap and a conditional nondoubling return estimate. They allow multiple linkage classes but only rank one. No general-dimensional version of the source's face removal follows from this argument. [Boros–Hofbauer v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.03071v2).
2657: ## 6. Counterexample search and what is still missing
2658: The averaging obstruction in Section 3 is model-respecting, bounded and PE, but is only a method obstruction. In rank one R1–R7 rule out the desired counterexample completely. Under SC, F1–F4 also rule it out, with a common time. These deductions remove broad candidate classes before a numerical search.
2659: For an uncovered test family, take the six complexes (2,1,0),(1,2,0),(0,2,1),(0,1,2),(1,0,2),(2,0,1), connected in a bidirectional ring. Total concentration is conserved, and the two independent edge vectors give the open mass-one simplex, of compact closure. The union is weakly reversible. Coordinate vertices are invariant boundary points; for example {B,C} is a critical siphon, so SC does not apply. The rank is two, so Route II does not apply either.
2660: The search used twelve fixed permutations of the twelve directed edges. Each schedule repeats a period of length 12, activating each edge alone for one unit at rate one. Thus every edge has integral exactly one on every sliding length-12 window, not just aligned windows: split a shifted period into its tail and next head. Hence K=1,T=12,alpha=1. Positive global exact solutions follow analytically from compactness and the consumption estimate, independently of the integrator. Starting near the A vertex at (1−2·10^−5,10^−5,10^−5), the retained finite experiment follows sixty periods per schedule. It records numerical positivity and mass error, late-period coordinate minima and the entire schedule permutations. The smallest recorded coordinate at period endpoints in the last twenty periods was 0.256869964175835; the largest observed mass error was 1.45·10^−14. It found no trajectory suggesting extinction over that finite horizon. An initial NumPy-dependent implementation could not run because NumPy was unavailable; the actual run uses plain-Python RK4, step .02, with **no certified error bound**. No conclusion about eventual compactness, arbitrarily late excursions, or all admissible schedules follows from these samples.
2661: There is a further exact reason the immediate vertex-attraction attempt is difficult in this ring. Near A=1 put w=B+C. Edges with source 2A+B or 2A+C give either transfers between B,C or positive net growth in w; no linear term decreases w. Negative w terms have at least quadratic rare-species order. This rules out a negative first-order total-rare-mass drift at that vertex for any schedule. It does not control travel along edges or repeated multiscale face changes and is not a permanence proof for the ring.
2662: For the general target, F1 controls each flux below by its starting monomial, but its upper effective coefficient can diverge. Production of a rare species can change the monomial hierarchy by an arbitrarily large relative factor in one window. The source's affine minimum can then switch labels. Even if every original reaction appears in a time-discretized step, there is no justified monotonicity of that **continuous** affine minimum: the integral of a changing minimum is not the minimum of fixed-label integrals, and one must control all active branches at ties. Complex graph return paths are not species transfer paths. F2 closes support propagation only when the initial large-species closure reaches everything. A critical siphon can stop that closure. Rank-one ordering supplies an extra power in all harmful terms, but distinct rare coordinates in higher rank need not have one common scalar order. Finite-horizon continuity and compactness do not fill this gap.
2663: The precise unpaid mechanism is uniform repulsion of all accessible critical-siphon faces, including changes among rare-species scales and active labels, under independent PE schedules. Neither a proof nor a counterexample to that exact claim is supplied. The deepest paid new results are F1–F4 and R1–R7. Their complete mechanisms are technical propagation and one-dimensional return estimates; no ninth nomination is forced.
2664: ## 7. Primary comparison and evidence boundary
2665: The current primary checks confirm CNP 1010.3050v2 (March 2, 2011), Craciun 1501.02860v3 (September 23, 2026), and Boros–Hofbauer 1903.03071v2 (March 13, 2019). A search-result date suggesting a 2022 Boros revision was not used: the actual primary history and PDF agree on 2019. CNP's Definition 2.6 and complete Section 6 proof endings retain a pointwise positive box; its monomial domination and strictly positive compact-region drift are not PE estimates. Craciun's embedding and final Section 8 bounded-trajectory application likewise retain positive lower rates. Those selected external passages were read; a fresh audit of its full 81-page construction is not claimed. Both OpenAI mass-action papers' full mathematical sections and all of Boros–Hofbauer were read. ADS's kinetic hypotheses and full relevant siphon/extinction proofs were read. [CNP](https://arxiv.org/abs/1010.3050v2), [Craciun](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.02860v3).
2666: Searches also found Komatsu–Nakajima's 2025 delayed, time-varying paper. Its publisher abstract/introduction describes a sufficient persistence criterion and boundary-limit analysis, but direct full-text retrieval returned 403. No assertion is made that its full hypotheses or proof exclude, include, or duplicate the present PE subclasses. Searches for intermittent/PE/switching reaction persistence also returned stochastic molecule-count extinction, parameter-identification excitation and switched SIS models; these are not exact P138 counterexamples. The retained search scope is bounded, not a claim of exhaustive literature coverage, priority or open-problem rank. [2025 publisher record](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.sysconle.2025.106206).

It has one flat immutable-reference union and one original-map traversal, retaining ancestor seals and finalized diagnostic receipts without running ancestor verifiers or diagnostics. Only new finite algebra/structure/window checks run after immutable verification. Numerical search output is retained as an exploratory receipt, not rerun as proof.
2668: Read records use UTF-8 decoded, zero-based half-open character ranges, exact range hashes/endings, raw LF counts, splitlines totals and terminal-LF flags. PDF form feeds explain splitlines/LF differences. The start_line/end_line fields count LF; separate inclusive splitlines_start/splitlines_end fields locate the same character ranges, with possible clipped first and last lines. These conventions are explicitly distinct. New PDF/text pairs bind actual pdftotext -layout products; replay does not re-extract PDFs. New HTML responses are retained raw, with exact submission-history substrings; no new HTML conversion is claimed. Lexical records preserve exact matches and zero-based positions on splitlines, including repeated representations; absence is not novelty.

This was a scope error, not an authorized dependency. Later traversal is limited to the assigned research evidence. 2790: Consider the (time-independent) mass-action system
2791: I141 passes all original I138 paid assertions without repairs at their original constants and scopes. It preserves finite support jets versus dynamical caustics, the simple directed graph convention for L=(n-1)K, all-active affine derivatives, nonnested scale iteration, rolling monomolecular windows, nonlinear ratio bounds and common-forcing synchronization, and the distinct stronger invariance/unbounded-class/all-initial-entry obstructions. This parent review is selective, not a second full original-proof audit.
2792: On a compact class each consumed species occurs in the source complex. Thus the explicit C bounds all negative consumption by C x_i, giving a positive exponential lower bound and global Caratheodory continuation. Multiplication over source exponents gives F1 on every starting window; variation of constants for x_i gives F2 with the deliberately conservative extra exp(-CT) factor.
2793: The 2A reversible with A+B schedule has period two and is PE on every length-two sliding window. Its pure forward solution is A(t)=A0/(1+A0 t); in the next reverse phase, A increases by an amount bounded away from zero as epsilon tends to zero. Since that increase is the integrated AB flux, division by A0 B0 diverges. This excludes a bounded upper effective coefficient frozen at the initial state, not permanence.
2794: Under SC, no siphon can lie in the initial low-species set because its conserved positive weighted mass would be less than half its fixed value. If the iterative all-reactants support closure stops before covering all species, its complement is exactly a siphon. At a newly added species the selected reaction has source exponent zero and positive integer product exponent, so F2 supplies gamma delta_r^d. Old species keep the exponential floor. At most n rounds and arbitrary rolling starts give the stated all-initial delay nT.
2795: For A+B reversible with C and C reversible with D the two displayed conservation vectors annihilate every reaction. The minimal siphons are exactly {A,C,D} and {B,C,D}; the stated M,d,C and epsilon are valid. In particular C needs two outgoing C terms, whereas A and B each need at most KM.
2796: In rank one, all complexes in one nontrivial linkage class differ along v. At a segment endpoint, v_i>0 on the zero coordinates, so sigma>0 and the rare-species order difference equals c_e sigma. Distinct complexes cannot tie in order within that class. Weak reversibility supplies an increasing edge from a minimum; every decreasing source has integer order at least p+1, also across linkage classes after taking their minimum p. Self-loops and isolated complexes are excluded as stated.
2797: The factors g_y are strictly positive on the chosen closed boundary band; coordinates outside the endpoint zero set have a positive minimum there. Summing negative terms gives -D z^(p+1), while one PE edge supplies a_* k z^p. Since rho0<=1 and p is a nonnegative integer, this implies z prime >=-D z, including p=0.
2798: If a whole window remains in the band and its endpoint is at most twice its start, integration of z prime >=-D z forward and backward yields exp(-DT) z(s)<=z(u)<=2 exp(DT) z(s). The proposed rho makes the integrated negative term at most half the PE positive term. The complementary doubling case gives increment greater than z(s). Thus R4 is valid with the stated constants without any unconditional upper monomial comparison.
2799: For a trajectory remaining below rho, complete-window endpoints increase by at least eta(z0), which is nondecreasing in z0 even at p=0. R5 is a safe loose exit-time bound. Any subsequent excursion below rho cannot last T: applying R4 from its start at rho would contradict remaining below rho. Gronwall within that shorter excursion gives rho exp(-DT). Repeating at the other endpoint gives R7 and compact-initial uniform entry, not fresh-schedule invariance.
2800: The proposed six-complex ring is conservative of rank two with accessible critical siphons. At the A vertex the source complexes of rare degree one are 2A+B and 2A+C; outgoing reactions either transfer B and C or increase B+C. Negative total-rare-mass terms have order at least two. This excludes a negative linear total-rare-mass drift at that vertex, not repeated excursions or transitions between faces.
2801: The twelve periodic schedules have exactly one unit of each reaction per period and hence satisfy PE on every shifted period. The recorded RK4 values have no certified numerical error or asymptotic significance. They are exploratory evidence only and were not rerun by the parent or verifier.
